# Judgments in the target book

Generated by `tools/report-judgments.ts` from the decision records in `books/target`. Do not edit: regenerate.

627 decisions. The target book holds 22 concepts, 84 tables, 47 state machines, 223 rpcs, 143 Core SDK flows, 81 workflows, 14 procedures and 215 operations.

## Decided by Aaron (1)

- **system.frozen-behaviour**: The freeze keeps behaviour, not spelling. Keep the behaviour, free the spelling. The 215 operations stay as the list of capabilities, and what each acceptance claim requires to happen stays as its evals: that is the hill. The exact flags, `--json` shapes, refusal codes and wording are no longer frozen; the target book designs them as one consistent system. A refusal is a behaviour (a signed-out caller is refused; a payment over the balance is refused), checked by its code only where the target's own error vocabulary keeps it. A renamed command, a rewritten claim or a retired one carries a decision.

## For Aaron (17)

Decisions flagged for Aaron's review, or whose text names a call that is his. Each recorded choice stands, and the build follows it, until he changes it; a change goes into the record first.

- **environments.staging**: A new staging: fresh v4, nothing migrated, host chosen at the spine. Chosen: A new standing environment with a fresh v4 deployment on Base Sepolia, no migration from old staging, its host chosen at the spine.
- **procedures.accounts-admit**: One authority procedure: an invitation admits its invitee to the beta. Chosen: Organizations' invitation rpc invokes `accounts.admit` in its own transaction.
- **rules.global-tenancy**: A global table has no tenant and is written only through its own concept. Chosen: `global`: no tenant column; every row may be read by every tenant and holds nothing private; written only by the owning concept's own procedures, rpcs and workflows, never directly by a tenant. Prices, flags and the directory of Parties.
- **system.size**: The rebuild's hand-written code is at most a quarter of the old system's. Chosen: At most 100,000 hand-written, non-test, non-generated lines across the backend, Core SDK, CLI, React SDK, MCP server, indexer and devnet: a quarter of the old system's 400,000 or so. The build session sets each concept's lines (the concept record's budget, checked by sys:loc) after the pilots (B3), from the lines per operation they measure, and records it here.
- **parties.directory-tenancy**: The directory is global; a person's private profile is theirs. Chosen: `parties` is `global`: written only through parties' own procedures and rpcs, readable by every concept that depends on parties; it holds nothing private (no mailbox, no profile). The private half is `profiles`, tenancy `person`, `person_id` first.
- **accounts.no-acceptance**: dev fund and the waitlist grant get no acceptance lines from the design. Chosen: Write none. Their returns, refusals, events and budgets are still evals; new lines need Aaron.
- **board.dropped**: The old proposals', directors' and grants' plumbing is not ported. Chosen: Raw `propose` and `changeOwners` (no frozen command on v4), `pay` and `cancelScheduled` (payments'), every `sendSigned` (approvals'), `load`, `ownerLabels`, `ownerCandidates`, `paymentRecipient`, `record`, `recordSubmission`, `recordOutcome`, `settle` and `emitBoardTransitioned` (steps or transitions now), `noticeFacts` and `notify` (notices'), the directors' follow job (the change's process), and the granter key's reserve, sign, broadcast, drive, verify and retry (P9: grants are Board batches).
- **board.failed-proposal**: A proposal whose execution the Safe reports failed is `failed`. Chosen: A fifth state, `failed`: the nonce is used and nothing it carried happened.
- **agents.org-owned**: An Organization can own an agent; every change to it is a Board proposal. Chosen: Build it: with `--org` the company Safe is the AgentOwner owner, and the create and every later change is one Board proposal (`board.propose`) whose proposing Director's approval comes with it. FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE stays reachable through the proposal's own `owner-safe-proposals` gate.
- **books.facts-without-chart**: Money facts are kept from the first money, chart or not; no backfill. Chosen: `books.record-money-fact` records every Organization side whether or not a chart exists; a money fact names no account, so the chart only maps facts to accounts. The import makes a Safe account for each asset the facts already moved. No backfill, no chart stamps.
- **documents.acceptance-rewrites**: `document render` #1 names the target's employer document. Chosen: 'A document that names an employer and an employee (a payslip, or an agreement's terms) renders their names, not their Party IDs.', the old line in `was`.
- **contacts.members-write**: Any member may write an Organization's book. Chosen: Any active Seat, as the frozen `who` says ('a member of the Organization for its book').
- **payments.birth-width**: A send's birth invokes three procedures from three concepts. Chosen: Keep the three in the birth's transaction.
- **payroll.scope**: Payroll designs agreements only; runs, groups, terms, departments are not here. Chosen: Agreements and their terms only, as concepts.payroll and the landings assign; runs, items, groups and terms dropped; departments are budgets'.
- **equity.acceptance**: Twenty-three equity operations keep no acceptance line. Chosen: Keep none: the `no-acceptance` gap shows them until Aaron accepts new lines (spec 30).
- **notices.claim-escrow**: Money waiting in a claim escrow sends no email, as before (for Aaron). Chosen: Send nothing, as the old system did; the payer tells the payee themselves.
- **devnet.no-acceptance**: The dev commands without acceptance lines get none from the design. Chosen: Write none. Their returns, refusals and spans are still evals, and the harness exercises every one of them when it builds the worlds; new lines need Aaron, as for equity.

## shared (152)

### components.backend: One backend service on Node, with its durable work inside it

- **Question:** What runs Capxul's server side: one service, or a service per concept, and where does durable work run?
- **Chosen:** One Effect 4 Node service hosts every concept's rpcs, HTTP routes and durable processes (Effect Workflow and Cluster on Postgres).
- **Why:** A1 fixes Effect 4 on Node and A2 fixes Effect Workflow and Cluster on Postgres. Concepts are modules, not services: a procedure runs in its invoker's transaction, which only one process and one database can give. Cluster already runs the workflow runners inside the same service and spreads them over its instances, so a separate worker adds a deploy and a network hop for nothing. If the A2 spike fails, DBOS runs in the same process, so this choice survives it.

### components.bundler-paymaster: Bundler and paymaster are one component, called only by the backend

- **Question:** Is the bundler a separate component from the paymaster, and may the SDK call it?
- **Chosen:** One component (the AA gateway on staging, alto with the mock paymaster on a devnet); only the backend calls it.
- **Why:** Staging serves both behind one URL and one token, and the backend reads no gateway secret but that URL, so two records would describe one thing. Sending stays server-side because the approval's durable process must own the one send and its lookup (K4: one send per signed operation), and the paymaster's token must not ship in a client. A4 gives the SDK signing, not sending.

### components.cli: The CLI calls exactly one SDK method per command

- **Question:** May the CLI orchestrate several SDK calls, or call the backend itself?
- **Chosen:** Each command calls one Core SDK method; the CLI parses, prompts, prints and exits, nothing else.
- **Why:** A4. The old CLI held about 20k lines of orchestration that duplicated the SDK, imported the protocol package in 64 files and called the backend Site directly in three places. With one method per command, the operation's `implementedBy` is that method's flow or rpc, and the React app and the MCP server get the same behaviour.

### components.core-sdk: The Core SDK owns every flow and all signing, and calls only the backend

- **Question:** What does the Core SDK depend on, and where do multi-step operations and signatures live?
- **Chosen:** Flows and signing in the SDK (A4); it reaches only the backend's rpcs, never the indexer, the chain or the bundler.
- **Why:** A4 accepts the first half. The second follows from P8: a direct indexer read needs its own authorization, which is what built the old component cycle; an rpc over a Safe-scoped view gets tenancy from row-level security for free. One path also gives one span tree per operation (P6).

### components.devnet: The devnet is a component: a supervisor with a real Postgres

- **Question:** Is the devnet a component, and what database does it run?
- **Chosen:** A supervisor process that boots the stack, deploys v4, snapshots worlds and clocks; a real Postgres server from a pinned binary.
- **Why:** Eval worlds are snapshots of the chain, the database and Ponder together, so something must own them: the supervisor. The backend and Ponder share one Postgres with row-level security, which PGlite cannot serve to two processes; Docker is not available in the swarmD sandboxes the spec targets. A pinned Postgres binary boots in seconds.

### components.files: Stored files live in Postgres

- **Question:** Where do uploaded files (logos, photos, verification documents) live without Convex file storage?
- **Chosen:** In Postgres, behind a BlobStore service, with media's size limits (24 MB at most).
- **Why:** A1 says one Postgres, and the files are few and small: logos, photos and identity documents of at most 24 MB. A bucket would add a component, a secret and a second thing to snapshot in every devnet world. The BlobStore service makes a move to a bucket a layer change if volume ever needs it.

### components.graph: The component graph has no cycle

- **Question:** Which component depends on which?
- **Chosen:** chain, postgres: none; ponder: chain, postgres; bundler-paymaster: chain; backend: postgres, ponder, chain, bundler-paymaster; core-sdk: backend; react-sdk, cli, mcp-server: core-sdk; devnet: everything it boots.
- **Why:** The old backend read the indexer and the indexer fetched the backend's keys: a cycle the as-is build reports. Reading Ponder in Postgres removes the indexer's edge to the backend. The Core SDK's devnet entry reaches the supervisor through an integration, not a component edge, so a staging client depends on nothing devnet.

### components.mcp-server: The MCP server is a Core SDK host like the CLI

- **Question:** How does the MCP server reach Capxul?
- **Chosen:** Its tools call Core SDK methods with the person's OAuth grant, like the CLI.
- **Why:** Its tools are the same operations as CLI commands, so they should be the same flows; the OAuth grant is access' and reaches it through the SDK.

### components.ponder: Ponder writes its schema; Capxul reads it in Postgres, never over HTTP

- **Question:** How do the backend and clients read indexed chain facts, and does Ponder call the backend?
- **Chosen:** The backend reads Ponder's tables as views in the same Postgres; Ponder serves only health and metrics and calls nothing.
- **Why:** The old indexer authorized every query against a signed `safes` claim it verified with the backend's JWKS, so the indexer depended on the backend and the backend on the indexer: a component cycle. With views in the same Postgres, row-level security does the scoping (P8), the SDK reads through rpcs, and Ponder depends only on the chain and Postgres. The SQL-over-HTTP path also forced compiled query shapes to authorize; views need none.

### components.postgres: One Postgres with an app schema and a Ponder schema

- **Question:** How are the concepts' tables, Ponder's tables, the workflow state and files laid out in Postgres?
- **Chosen:** One database: `app` (concept tables with RLS, workflow state, outbox, files) and `ponder` (Ponder-owned, read through views).
- **Why:** A1 says one Postgres with Ponder on it, and P8 reads chain facts through views joined to the Safe-ownership table, which needs both in one database. Ponder rebuilds its own schema on a reindex, so it must be separate from the app's and nothing in `app` may hold a foreign key into it. A schema per concept adds nothing the book's boundary rules do not already check, and makes every procedure's transaction span schemas for no gain.

### components.react-sdk: The React SDK projects the Core SDK and nothing else

- **Question:** Does the React SDK get its own transport or logic?
- **Chosen:** Hooks over Core SDK flows and live rpcs; no transport and no logic of its own.
- **Why:** One client implementation keeps one span tree and one error mapping. Live rpcs replace the 27 Convex subscriptions, so React needs no transport of its own. The approval page is built on it.

### components.service-keys: A4's 'all signing' is the actor's; Capxul's service keys sign in the backend

- **Question:** A4 gives the Core SDK all signing. The frozen designed text has Capxul's own keys sign some transactions: Companies House's claim role and tier key, Budgets' executor, the escrow relayer and the faucet. Where do they sign?
- **Chosen:** In the backend's service-signer integration: each key does one job on chain, never for a person or an agent.
- **Why:** A4 is about the actor's authority: every flow and every signature a person or an agent gives is the SDK's, and the backend never holds their keys (integrations.openfort). The service keys are Capxul's own, held in the operator's store, and the designed text names them ('Companies House's claim role, held by a Capxul service key in Infisical'; the tier key sends `Installer.deployMoney`; Budgets' executor runs a dated payment on its day). A client cannot hold them, and the actor's own Safe cannot replace them: the claim exists because the person has no Safe yet, and a dated payment is sent when nobody is there. Each key can do only its one job on chain, and the integration keeps one send in flight per key (responsibilities.service-lanes).

### concepts.access: Access keeps sign-in and sessions and moves to the bottom

- **Question:** What does access own, and how does it get to depend on nothing?
- **Chosen:** Principals, sessions, sign-in, assistant grants and gates; the waitlist, referrals, consent and the indexer scope leave it.
- **Why:** Every concept asks access who is calling, so it must depend on nothing. The old imports were for what moves out: the indexer read scope needed the person's and Organization's Safes (now row-level security), the waitlist's earned invites needed live Safes (now accounts, from the chain), and consent needed applications (now applications'). An agent signs in by key; that is proved on chain (`AgentOwner.agentOf` and the Safe's owners through chain-rpc), so access needs no agents table. Feature gates stay: 39 frozen refusals in 30 operations of six areas name FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE, so they sit below everything; a gate for an Organization keys on its id as a value. The commands that sign in and then report the account's setup are accounts' flows (operations.sign-in-in-accounts). The device flow's native client grants are not ported (concepts.no-device-flow-or-consent).

### concepts.accounts: Accounts keeps the personal Safe and gains admission

- **Question:** What does accounts own once approvals, agents and balances leave, and where do the waitlist and referrals go?
- **Chosen:** Accounts owns admission (waitlist, referrals), the personal Safe's setup and test-network grants.
- **Why:** A referrer earns an invite when the person they referred gets a live Safe, so referrals need Safe claims: in access that would put access above accounts and break the bottom. Counting claims from the chain (CapxulSafeClaimed) lets accounts own both without a loop. Admission and the Safe's setup are one journey (join, be admitted, set up your Safe), so a separate concept would be a boundary inside one flow. Approvals, agents and balance reads leave for their own concepts.

### concepts.activity: Activity is the read model at the top

- **Question:** What is activity once its annotations leave, and where do balances and `address` go?
- **Chosen:** Activity reads payments, indexing, budgets, books and documents to serve the feed, balances and `address`.
- **Why:** A balance answers for whoever you act as, a person or an Organization, and its 'available' amount needs pending payments; a feed row shows its account and documents. Only a concept above payments, books and organizations can read all of that, and nothing needs to read activity back. In accounts, balances would pull accounts above payments.

### concepts.agents: New concept: agents, split from accounts

- **Question:** Do agents stay in accounts?
- **Chosen:** Agents is its own concept above the Board, because an Organization can own an agent.
- **Why:** With `--org` a Director makes the Organization the agent's owner, and every later change is then a Board proposal, so agents must depend on board. Accounts must sit below organizations (a founder must be admitted and set up first). Both cannot hold in one concept. AgentOwner is also its own v4 contract with its own lifecycle.

### concepts.applications: Applications stays separate, above organizations, with health

- **Question:** Merge applications into access, and where do consent and the deployment health check go?
- **Chosen:** Applications above organizations, owning apps, keys, origins, bootstrap, quickstart keys and health; no consent table.
- **Why:** An Organization's publishable key needs the Invoicing hat ('For an Organization, a member who holds issue invoices'), so applications must sit above organizations; access must sit below everything. Merging them would make a loop. A person's consent to an application would be the application's record, but nothing in the target writes one: its only writer was the device flow (concepts.no-device-flow-or-consent). `doctor --online` checks the deployment and the app key, which is what bootstrap already describes, so health lives here.

### concepts.approvals: New concept: approvals, every UserOperation from a person's own Safe

- **Question:** Where do building, sponsoring, signing, sending and following a person's UserOperation live?
- **Chosen:** One low concept owns every approval and its one send, opened by the concept that prepares it.
- **Why:** v4 D1 makes every action a UserOperation from a personal Safe: payments, hat grants, Board approvals, agent changes, budget spends, agreements, equity. The old system spread this over payments' `moneyExecutions` (read at 19 sites by its neighbours), accounts' approvals and lanes, and borrowed executions, and that sharing was one of the import loops. One concept gives one approval page, one `dev approve`, one sponsorship and send path that keeps K4 (one send per signed operation) and K6 (only chain proof ends an attempt), and one place for `bud:` on chain-submit. It is 'approvals' because that is the product's word: 'opens your approval in the browser'.

### concepts.banking: Banking gains verification and saved bank accounts

- **Question:** Where do Bridge verification and saved bank accounts live?
- **Chosen:** Both in banking, with every other Bridge call.
- **Why:** Verification is Bridge's KYC and KYB and gates only bank transfers ('identity verification for bank transfers'); saved bank accounts are Bridge external accounts used only by bank payouts and encrypted with banking's record key. In banking, every Bridge call and every Bridge secret is in one concept. A business verification invitation (`inv_kyb_`) is banking's too; `invite accept` routes to it by id.

### concepts.board: New concept: board, the Directors, proposals and officer hats

- **Question:** Where do Directors, the threshold, Board proposals and the officer hats live?
- **Chosen:** One board concept above organizations; officer hat grants and revokes are Board proposals there.
- **Why:** v4 makes every officer permission a hat granted by a Board batch (CAP-1101), and a Board proposal is one company Safe transaction approved by Directors' own approveHash. Grants, Director changes and every other concept's Board batches share that one machine, so it is one concept. It reads members from organizations. An invitation's offered grants become one Board proposal that budgets opens through `board.propose` (operations.member-invite-in-budgets), so organizations never depends on board.

### concepts.books: Books gains annotations and prices

- **Question:** Where do activity's annotations and the Chainlink price sync go?
- **Chosen:** Both in books: an annotation gives a row its account, which is a book fact; prices value book facts.
- **Why:** The frozen text says a fact is 'a change to the chart, an account given to a row, or a money event'. If activity owned the annotation, either activity writes a book fact (a cross-concept write that is no money fact) or books reads activity while activity reads books' chart: a loop. In books, `activity annotate` is one transaction. Prices were already `bookPrices`; the Chainlink sync is its writer (P3 keeps it).

### concepts.books-token-scan: Books scans the tokens an Organization adds; indexing has no workflow

- **Question:** P3 keeps the scan of tokens added at run time. Which concept owns it, given that only books knows which tokens were added?
- **Chosen:** Books: one cron scans each added asset's Transfers to and from the company Safe from the block it was added, and writes book facts.
- **Why:** The frozen text says the books 'record its transfers' and 'Capxul starts scanning its transfers from the current block'; its facts carry the token's symbol and no USD. The scan's only reader is books, and its list of tokens is books' own `assets`. In indexing, the scan would have to follow books' transition, which needs indexing above books, a cycle; a watched-token procedure is not one money fact. In books it reads its own table and the chain (chain-rpc `getLogs`), and `org books assets remove` stops it. Indexing is then Ponder's views only.

### concepts.budgets: New concept: budgets, from allowances, departments and ceilings

- **Question:** Where do spending allowances, departments and payout ceilings live under v4?
- **Chosen:** One budgets concept: allowances and departments are both v4 budgets, with office hats and ceilings.
- **Why:** The designed text says it directly: 'A spending allowance becomes a small budget under a hat the member wears', and a department is a top or child budget. Both are one v4 contract (Budgets), one tree and one authority rule (the wearer of the budget's hat or of any above it). Keeping them apart would duplicate the tree and its authority check.

### concepts.contacts: New concept: contacts, split from parties

- **Question:** Where does the address book live?
- **Chosen:** A small contacts concept above organizations.
- **Why:** An Organization's address book is acted on by its members, which only a concept above organizations can check; parties must stay below organizations because an Organization registers its Party at birth. A hidden runtime check would be a dependency the book cannot see. In payments, banking (bank accounts on a contact) would still have to reach into payments for a non-money record.

### concepts.devnet: Devnet replaces operator

- **Question:** What happens to old operator's tools (diagnostics, receipt holds, proof identities, faucet, fixtures, probes, mail sink, notice records)?
- **Chosen:** A devnet concept for devnet-only tools; the faucet to accounts, diagnostics to applications' health, probes dropped, notice records to notices.
- **Why:** P2 sends operator probes to a devnet concept with devnet-only rpcs, and P8 moves test tables there. The faucet must also work on staging (`dev fund`), so it is accounts' test-network grant. Tenancy probes existed to test tenancy by hand; P8's generated `tbl:<t>/tenancy` evals replace them. The devnet's tables are what the integrations' fakes use, so no production concept depends on devnet.

### concepts.documents: Documents stays, below everything that stamps a document hash

- **Question:** Where does documents sit, and how does it know who may read a document?
- **Chosen:** Below payments, requests and payroll; participants are given when the document is stored.
- **Why:** A document is born with its subject (K2: a command, its Payments and their documents are born together), so the subject's concept stores it through a procedure and must depend on documents. Documents then cannot read its subjects; giving it the participants at store time means it never has to.

### concepts.equity: New concept: equity, with shareholder votes

- **Question:** Where do the cap table, options, rounds, SAFEs, exits, shares and shareholder votes live?
- **Chosen:** One equity concept, low priority, including the Governor's votes.
- **Why:** They are the v4 equity clones (Captable, ShareClass, Vesting, Options, Rounds, Exits, Governor), all low priority and off the critical path (spec 40), so one concept keeps them out of the critical concepts' budgets. Shareholder votes weigh shares and run on the Governor, an equity clone; Board decisions are board's proposals.

### concepts.indexing: Indexing keeps its name and owns every chain view

- **Question:** Who owns Ponder's tables in the book, and what is left of old indexing?
- **Chosen:** Indexing owns every chain-tenancy view and no workflow; drift reconcile goes; the added-token scan goes to books.
- **Why:** Ponder is one app with one schema, and the views are read across concepts: Hats wearers by board, budgets, books, requests, applications and organizations; Transfers by payments and activity. Per-concept ownership would force the dependency order to follow the contracts' call graph and still need Hats at the bottom. Old indexing's drift reconcile existed because Convex copied chain rows; nothing copies them now (P3 lists drift only while it is needed). The added-token scan leaves too (concepts.books-token-scan). The name stays: `chain` is already the component, and the old name already meant chain facts.

### concepts.line-budgets: No line budgets on concepts yet

- **Question:** Should the concept records carry `lines` budgets now?
- **Chosen:** Leave `lines` unset; each concept's design sets its budget from its own records.
- **Why:** A line budget is only as good as the design under it, and the per-concept designs have not been written. The old lines are mostly plumbing (about two thirds of an area is ceremony, and 166 files are emit, intent, load or record), so they would anchor the numbers on the system being replaced, the same objection P5 made to per-operation budgets.

### concepts.local-commands: Commands that touch only this machine are booked under access

- **Question:** Which concept designs `schema`, `use env`, `use personal`, `config telemetry`, `auth list` and `whoami`?
- **Chosen:** Access: they read or write the client's own session and environment state, as Core SDK flows with local steps.
- **Why:** A4 makes each of them one SDK method too. They are about who and where the client is (the current person, the environment, consent to telemetry, what the CLI can do), which is access' question on the client side. A concept with no tables and no rpcs would be an empty concept.

### concepts.map: 22 target concepts in one dependency order

- **Question:** How many concepts, and in what order, so that every old responsibility lands once and nothing loops?
- **Chosen:** 22 concepts in this order: access, media, parties, indexing, approvals, accounts, organizations, board, agents, budgets, books, documents, contacts, payments, payroll, banking, requests, applications, equity, activity, notices, devnet.
- **Why:** 15 of the 17 old concepts formed one import loop, and the loop ran through real responsibilities in the wrong place: permissions inside organizations' authority, executions inside payments, Directors inside organizations, waitlist credit inside access. v4 itself splits them: Budgets, Agreements, Hats and the Signer Gate, AgentOwner and the equity clones are separate contracts with separate authority. Each target concept owns one of those nouns, and its dependencies point only at concepts it must read or invoke. Merging into fewer concepts would break the line budgets (`sys:loc`) and hide ownership (P4 option C's objection). The new ones are small and each has a decision below.

### concepts.media: Media stays, and owners point at files

- **Question:** Merge media into its users, or keep it, and which way do its links point?
- **Chosen:** Keep media as the file store at the bottom; logos, photos and verification documents point at media's files.
- **Why:** Three concepts use files (parties' photos, organizations' logos, banking's identity documents), so a file store under all of them is simpler than three copies. The old media imported organizations, permissions and profiles to bind an image to its owner; inverting the link removes all three edges.

### concepts.no-device-flow-or-consent: Access keeps assistant grants only; applications keeps no consent

- **Question:** Access dropped the device flow's native client grants (access.no-device-flow) and applications dropped the consent table (applications.no-consent). The shared records still named both, a JWKS route and the MCP exchange secret. What do they say now?
- **Chosen:** Align them: access owns sessions, sign-in, assistant grants and gates; applications owns no consent; the backend serves no JWKS and admits a browser origin by the bootstrap's origin token; MCP_EXCHANGE_SECRET leaves both environments.
- **Why:** The shared records are what every designer reads first, and a map that names records no concept builds misleads them. Both drops follow P2: no client starts a device request (the CLI keeps its v1 grant records only to delete them), and the consent table's one writer was the device flow's accept. Access finds an opaque session or assistant token by its hash in the request's own transaction (access.sessions, access.assistants), so nothing needs a JWKS, and the MCP server passes the person's token through the Core SDK with no exchange. The daily client-grant sweep P3 keeps is access' daily sweep of sessions, codes and ended assistant grants. The origin token is applications.origin-token's.

### concepts.notices: New concept: notices, every email after a change

- **Question:** Who sends the emails that follow a change (invitations, requests, dated payments, agreements, referrals)?
- **Chosen:** One notices concept at the top: one workflow per notice kind, triggered by the source concept's transition.
- **Why:** P3 makes each notification an after-commit workflow fed by the outbox, one per notice kind. Putting them in one concept gives one place for templates, recipients and delivery records (which `dev mail notices` reads), and keeps every money concept free of mail. It sits at the top because it reads the facts of the concepts it tells about. The sign-in code stays in access: sending it is the operation itself, not a notice after one.

### concepts.organizations: Organizations keeps the workspace; Directors and equity leave

- **Question:** What stays in organizations, the old 30k-line reference concept?
- **Chosen:** Identity, setup and install, the deployment record, members and invitations; Directors and proposals to board; equity and votes to equity.
- **Why:** Members and invitations are needed by almost every concept (who may act for this Organization), so they must sit low; Board proposals are opened by budgets, payments, payroll, agents and equity, so the Board must sit above organizations and below them. Keeping both in one concept is what tied organizations and permissions into a two-way loop (76 and 37 imports). Equity is low priority and off the critical path, so it should not share a line budget with the workspace.

### concepts.parties: Parties is the directory and absorbs profiles

- **Question:** What does parties own, and where do profiles, contacts, verification and engagements go?
- **Chosen:** Parties owns Parties, handles, Safes, owners, profiles and resolution; contacts, verification and engagements leave.
- **Why:** A profile is a person's own Party seen by others: its display name is what a counterparty sees, so it belongs with the Party (old profiles was one `identities` table linked to the person's Party). Contacts need an Organization's membership, which sits above parties, so they move up (concepts.contacts). Verification is Bridge's KYC for bank rails only, so it moves to banking. Engagements were what payroll runs paid; v4 Agreements replace them. What remains is the one thing everyone below organizations needs: a name, the Safe it routes money to, and who owns that Safe.

### concepts.parties-scope: Parties' directory is the Safe-ownership table P8 scopes by

- **Question:** Which table says whose Safe is whose, for the row-level security on chain views?
- **Chosen:** Parties' directory (Safe, owner kind, owner id) is the scope; indexing's views join it.
- **Why:** Every Safe a tenant owns already has a Party (a person's, an agent's, an Organization's) with its owner, so a second table would be a copy that can disagree. Chain events alone cannot say which Organization id a company Safe is. With the scope in parties, indexing depends on parties and nothing below indexing needs it.

### concepts.payments: Payments keeps the ledger and is born from chain facts too

- **Question:** What does payments own once executions leave, and how do payments Capxul did not prepare appear?
- **Chosen:** Commands, routes, settlement, reversal, dated payments, Movements, and Payments born from indexed PaymentSent facts.
- **Why:** On v4 every movement of money ends in one `PaymentSent` (architecture.md: one way money leaves), whoever starts it: an agreement collection, an escrow payout, `Budgets.execute`, a round payment. Settling from that one event gives one settlement path (fixing M2 and M3), one place that writes book facts (K10) and one ledger activity can read. A payment Capxul prepared is matched by its command; any other is born from the fact. Payroll, requests and equity then never need to write a Payment, which is what kept them in loops with payments.

### concepts.payroll: Payroll keeps agreements; runs, items and groups are dropped

- **Question:** What does payroll own on v4, and do payroll runs survive?
- **Chosen:** Payroll owns v4 Agreements only; runs, items and saved groups are dropped; departments go to budgets.
- **Why:** No frozen operation makes, lists or shows a payroll run, so by P2 it has no caller and is deleted by default; it comes back only with a named one. On v4, recurring pay is an Agreement collected by the payee, and a one-off batch is `payment send` or `org department pay`. Dropping runs also removes the settle-run procedure that tied payments to payroll (dependencies.payments-payroll).

### concepts.permissions-dissolved: Old permissions is dissolved

- **Question:** What happens to old permissions (allowances, managePeople, assignments, Roles commands, receipts)?
- **Chosen:** Allowances to budgets, managePeople to board's People hat, assignments and receipts to indexing's chain views, Roles commands gone.
- **Why:** On v4, Capxul 'reads the permission only from Hats', and a spending allowance is a budget. The Zodiac Roles commands were the pre-v4 way to change permissions; v4 uses Roles only for the payout role the install wires (P9 drops the rest). The receipt-verified record existed because Convex copied chain state; indexing's views are that record now.

### concepts.requests: Requests gains the bank deposit match

- **Question:** Who owns `bank match`, which links a deposit to the request it paid?
- **Chosen:** Requests: it marks its request paid and marks the deposit matched through a banking procedure.
- **Why:** A request is paid by a Payment, an escrow or a bank deposit: the means are below it, and the request reacts to them. With the match in requests, requests depends on payments and banking and neither depends on requests. The deposit and the request it paid are one money fact (what the money was for), so the deposit side is a procedure.

### contracts.addresses: Addresses: the devnet deployment now, staging when it is deployed

- **Question:** Which addresses do contract records carry?
- **Chosen:** The devnet deployment from the v4 address book; staging's after the fresh deployment; none for per-company proxies and per-escrow contracts.
- **Why:** v4 is deployed only on the devnet today, and P9 gives the new staging a fresh deployment with nothing migrated, so old staging's addresses (V2 and V3) must not appear. A Safe, a company's Roles and Gate, and each escrow are proxies with one address each, so their records carry none; the clone contracts carry their implementation's.

### contracts.equity-events: The equity events that equity's processes follow are recorded

- **Question:** Equity's processes and tables name 12 v4 events that the contract records lacked. Record them?
- **Chosen:** Add Options' OptionsAccepted; Rounds' SafeRoundCreated, SafeInvited, SafeSigned, Invited, Allocated and RoundCancelled; Exits' SaleApproved, TransferOffered and TransferApproved; Vesting's Granted and Revoked.
- **Why:** contracts.functions records every event a concept must follow, and equity/processes.ts and equity/data.ts name these; Ponder indexes them for indexing's views either way. Recorded, equity can trigger on or name them by record, and the unconsumed-chain-event gap shows exactly which it still follows only in words. Escrow's InvoicePaymentCompleted is not added: nothing reads the surplus it reports (indexing's report).

### contracts.functions: Record the functions the designed calls name, and the events concepts follow

- **Question:** Which functions and events go on each contract record?
- **Chosen:** Every function a frozen designed `call` names, the views a write must read first, and every event a concept must follow.
- **Why:** The build reports a function nothing calls and an event nothing consumes as gaps, so recording only what the product uses makes those gaps meaningful: each one left after the concept designs is either a missing design or a contract function the product does not need. Admin and wiring functions (configure, setAgreements, setUp) belong to the install, which the Installer's batches build. A call one contract makes to another inside a call the product makes (Budgets.commit inside Agreements.offer, Budgets.payReserved inside Agreements.collect, Captable.forcedTransfer inside Exits.buy, Captable.setConversionPrice inside Rounds.close) is the callee's business and is left out. The designed text's `Budgets.setOffice` is v4's `setHat` (IBudgets.sol:203), recorded under that name.

### contracts.hats-elections: Hats Elections and the Installer's election calls are recorded

- **Question:** `org vote create` names `board-settings`, `elect` and `remove` (designed, W6), whose Governor calls go to a seat group's Hats Elections clone and the Installer's election batch. contracts.v4-set left Elections out. Record it?
- **Chosen:** Record the Elections clone (elect, recall, setNextTerm, startNextTerm, currentTermEnd, nextTermEnd) and the Installer's deployElections, buildElections and ElectionsDeployed.
- **Why:** P10: a designed block is the specification, and the shared records should not be what keeps a designed matter unbuilt; equity.vote-matters names this record as one of its two blockers. The calls are v4's: IHatsElectionEligibility and InstallerElections are in packages/contracts/v4, and the Governor's allowed targets include the predicted Elections clone. The other blocker, board's seat groups and terms, is board's design; until it lands the three matters keep NOT_IMPLEMENTED, and equity builds them once it does.

### contracts.hats-view-hat: Hats' `viewHat` is recorded; `getNextId` is not

- **Question:** Budgets plans a new hat's id from indexing's hats, which can lag the chain (budgets.hat-planning). Record `Hats.getNextId` or `viewHat` so the plan reads the chain now?
- **Chosen:** Record `viewHat`, which v4's IHats declares: a hat's supply, modules, `lastHatId` and whether it is active.
- **Why:** The contract records hold what v4 calls (contracts.v4-set): v4's IHats lists viewHat and not getNextId, and the Installer itself reads viewHat. The next child's id is the admin's id with `lastHatId + 1` at the child's level, so viewHat at the chain head gives what getNextId would. Neither closes the race the budgets designer names (a hat made under the same admin between proposal and execution); v4 has no guard, and budgets applies a change only on its indexed effect with the planned hat (budgets.one-process-per-change), so a shifted id never reads as applied.

### contracts.no-checkpoint-reads: No checkpoint reads for `org cap-table --at` yet

- **Question:** Equity asks for the share classes' checkpoint reads so `org cap-table --at` can answer. Record them?
- **Chosen:** Record none: the designed call says `--at` is not built yet, so it answers NOT_IMPLEMENTED.
- **Why:** The frozen designed `call` says '`--at` (checkpoints) is not built yet', so NOT_IMPLEMENTED is the frozen surface, not a weakening. ERC20Votes keeps checkpoints of votes, not balances: a holder who delegated shows no votes, so a past vote count is not a past holding, and only the company Safe's balance has its own checkpoint (safeBalanceAt). A past cap table reads better from indexing's history of the classes' Transfers; when `--at` is designed, equity and indexing choose.

### contracts.v4-set: The v4 contracts plus the third-party contracts v4 wires

- **Question:** Which contracts does the target book record (P9: v4 only)?
- **Chosen:** The 15 v4 address-book contracts (Escrow and its factory counted separately) and the third-party contracts the v4 install wires and the product calls: Safe, Safe4337Module, MultiSendCallOnly, EntryPoint v0.7, Roles 2.1, Hats, the Hats Signer Gate v2, Hats Elections and ERC-20.
- **Why:** P9 drops V2, V3, CapxulEscrow, PayrollDepartments and the rest. The third-party contracts are not pre-v4: v4's architecture lists Hats, the Signer Gate and Roles as its authority group, and every approval goes through the EntryPoint and the Safe module. Without them the book could not name the calls the designed text makes (approveHash, mintHat, claimSignerFor). The libraries are left out: no frozen operation calls them. Hats Elections was left out at first and is in since the assembly (contracts.hats-elections): the designed shareholder votes call it through the Governor.

### contracts.test-token-mint: The test tokens' open `mint` is recorded on the ERC-20 record

- **Question:** `dev fund` and the welcome grant mint test money. The ERC-20 record had no `mint`. Record it?
- **Chosen:** Add `mint(address,uint256)` to the ERC-20 record, for the test networks' synthetic tokens only.
- **Why:** On devnet and Base Sepolia the admitted assets are Capxul's synthetic test tokens, which anyone may mint; the faucet key calls `mint` through the service signer. It is not a Protocol v4 contract, but neither is any ERC-20, and P9 is about the protocol, so the one ERC-20 record carries it with its test-only scope; production tokens have no such function and production refuses `dev fund`. The accounts designer left its rpc's `chain` empty waiting for this (accounts.faucet).

### dependencies.accounts-payments: Payments depends on accounts; accounts never reads payments

- **Question:** The old accounts and payments imported each other (21 each). Which way does the target point?
- **Chosen:** Payments reads accounts; claim escrows waiting for a person are found from indexing's escrow views.
- **Why:** Payments needs whether the sender and the recipient have live Safes (pay directly, or into a claim escrow). Accounts needed payments only for balances (now activity), sending approved operations (now approvals) and the claim escrows to collect at setup, which are chain facts (EscrowCreated with the person's Safe as payee). So accounts reads indexing.

### dependencies.activity-books: Activity reads books; books never reads activity

- **Question:** Old activity and books imported each other. Which way now?
- **Chosen:** Activity reads books' annotations, chart and prices; books owns annotations.
- **Why:** Annotations moved to books (concepts.books), so activity is a pure reader above books and nothing reads activity.

### dependencies.agent-seats: A removed agent's Seats end from the chain event

- **Question:** `agent remove`: 'the backend ends its Seats'. Seats are organizations', agents sits above organizations. How?
- **Chosen:** Organizations' workflow, triggered by AgentOwner's `AgentRemoved`, ends every Seat the agent's Safe holds.
- **Why:** The removal is final only when `AgentOwner.remove` lands, so the Seats must follow the chain, not the request: a procedure at prepare time would end Seats for a removal that never lands. A chain-event trigger needs only organizations' existing dependency on indexing.

### dependencies.approvals-low: Approvals sits low, under every chain-writing concept

- **Question:** Which concepts depend on approvals, and what does approvals depend on?
- **Chosen:** Approvals depends on access and indexing only; every concept that writes the chain from a person's Safe depends on it.
- **Why:** An approval needs only its owner (access: a person's Safe is a pure function of their email; an agent's is on its principal) and chain proof (indexing). It carries a summary for the approval page, given by the concept that opens it, so it never reads its openers. Accounts, organizations, board, agents, budgets, payments, payroll and equity open approvals.

### dependencies.books-below-money: Books sits below payments and banking, which write its money facts

- **Question:** Which way do books and payments point, and how is a settled Payment's book fact written?
- **Chosen:** Payments and banking depend on books and write a money fact's book fact through its procedure, in the same transaction.
- **Why:** ADR-0042 decision 1 (accepted) and P4 put a book fact in the caller's transaction so a fact is never lost or doubled, and K10 says a settled Payment always has its book facts. Books needs nothing from payments: its facts carry what they record. The old code had the door (`recordSettledPaymentInTx`) and never used it; the target imports only the door.

### dependencies.budgets-parties: Budgets depends on parties

- **Question:** Budgets' commands name people: `--holder <email>` 'the person or AI', `--member`, `--assign-to`, and lists that show who wears each hat. Budgets had no edge to parties. Is one needed?
- **Chosen:** Add parties: resolve an email or an agent's `@handle` to its Party and Safe, and name a wearer's Safe.
- **Why:** A Seat carries a Party and a principal, not a mailbox or a handle. A person's email reaches their principal through access, but an agent is named by its `@handle`, which is parties' namespace, and a hat wearer is shown by the name its Safe's Party has. Every other concept that names people (board, payments, payroll, equity) already depends on parties. Parties sits far below budgets, so the edge makes no cycle.

### dependencies.notices-top: Notices depends on what it tells about; nothing depends on notices

- **Question:** Which way do notices and the concepts that cause emails point?
- **Chosen:** Notices depends on accounts, organizations, board, budgets, payments, payroll, banking, requests and equity; their transitions trigger it.
- **Why:** An email may lag its change and must never roll it back, so it is an outbox consumer (P3, P4), and the consumer depends on the producer. Only devnet (the mail sink) sits above it.

### dependencies.notices-budgets: Notices depends on budgets, for the allowance email

- **Question:** 'Once a v4 assign executes, the member is emailed the allowance at its amount now.' Notices did not depend on budgets. Who sends it?
- **Chosen:** Add budgets to notices: one notice workflow follows budgets' change transition and reads the allowance.
- **Why:** Only budgets knows that a proposal carried an allowance assign, to whom and on which budget; a Board proposal does not say. Notices sends every email and depends on what it tells about (dependencies.notices-top), and budgets sits below it. The budgets designer asked for this edge (budgets.notices-need-budgets).

### dependencies.of-access: What access depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must access read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on nothing.
- **Why:** Every concept asks it who is calling, so it depends on nothing (concepts.access).

### dependencies.of-accounts: What accounts depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must accounts read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals.
- **Why:** access: admission, setup and sign-in are a principal's; the sign-in flows call access' code and session rpcs. parties: the person's Party gets its Safe at setup; `dev fund --org` resolves an Organization's handle to its Safe. indexing: Safe claims (CapxulSafeClaimed) earn referral invites, and the claim escrows waiting for a person are chain facts. approvals: collecting the claim escrows at setup is an approval from the person's Safe.

### dependencies.of-activity: What activity depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must activity read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, organizations, budgets, books, documents, payments.
- **Why:** access: the reader. parties: the counterparties a row names. indexing: Transfers and Safe balances. organizations: whether the reader may read an Organization's activity. budgets: what an Organization's budgets have left, for `available`. books: a row's account and memo, and USD prices. documents: the documents a row carries. payments: Payments, pending payments and Movements.

### dependencies.of-agents: What agents depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must agents read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals, accounts, organizations, board.
- **Why:** access: the owner is a principal, and the agent signs in through access with its key. parties: an agent registers its Party (handle and Safe) at birth. indexing: AgentOwner's events say what became of each change. approvals: a person-owned agent's change is an approval from the owner's Safe. accounts: a person must have a claimed Safe to own an agent. organizations: with `--org` the owner is the Organization's Safe, acted for by a Director. board: every change to an Organization-owned agent is a Board proposal.

### dependencies.of-applications: What applications depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must applications read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, indexing, organizations.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc: a person owns their own applications and keys. indexing: the Invoicing hat's wearers may manage an Organization's keys. organizations: an Organization's applications, and its Invoicing hat id.

### dependencies.of-approvals: What approvals depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must approvals read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, indexing.
- **Why:** access: an approval belongs to a principal, whose Safe is its sender. indexing: an approval ends only on chain proof: the UserOperationEvent on its nonce.

### dependencies.of-banking: What banking depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must banking read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, media, parties, indexing, approvals, organizations, budgets, books, contacts, payments.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. media: verification documents are stored files. parties: bank details are per Party and currency, and a deposit lands in a Party's Safe. indexing: the token Transfer a bank deposit becomes, and Director hat wearers for a business's people. approvals: the payer signs a bank payout's approval: an agent with its own key at once (dependencies.payers-sign-and-name-budgets). organizations: who may manage an Organization's banking, and the routing of `invite accept`. budgets: `bank pay --from` names the budget an Organization pays from by its key, and who may spend from it. books: a bank deposit writes its book fact in its own transaction. contacts: a saved bank account may belong to a contact, and `contact get` reads the entry before its accounts. payments: a bank payout's money leaves as a Payment born through payments.prepare-command.

### dependencies.of-board: What board depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must board read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals, organizations.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. parties: a Director or a grantee is named by handle or email and resolved to their Safe. indexing: Director hat wearers, the company Safe's owners, threshold and approvals, and ExecutionSuccess. approvals: each Director's approveHash, and the founder's batches, are approvals from their own Safes. organizations: the company Safe, its members and hat ids, and the invitation whose grants it proposes.

### dependencies.of-books: What books depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must books read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, indexing, organizations.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. indexing: the Transfers, PaymentSent facts and Safe balances it reconciles against, and the Books hat's wearers. organizations: books are an Organization's, and the Books hat id is in its deployment.

### dependencies.of-budgets: What budgets depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must budgets read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals, organizations, board.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. parties: a holder or wearer is named by email or an agent's `@handle` and resolved to their Party and Safe; lists name them (dependencies.budgets-parties). indexing: Budgets' tree, amounts and spends, and each budget hat's wearers, are chain views. approvals: an office holder's change is an approval from their own Safe. organizations: the company Safe, members, hat ids from the install, and the invitations whose grants it proposes. board: top budgets, payout ceilings, allowance hats and an invitation's grants are Board batches.

### dependencies.of-contacts: What contacts depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must contacts read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, organizations.
- **Why:** access: the owner of a personal address book. parties: a contact names a Party, or a handle, email or address that resolves to one. organizations: an Organization's book is acted on by its members.

### dependencies.of-devnet: What devnet depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must devnet read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, approvals, organizations, notices.
- **Why:** access: `dev approve` signs in as another person, and `dev gates set` writes gates. approvals: `dev approve` signs an approval with the dev signer. organizations: `dev gates set` for an Organization needs its membership. notices: `dev mail notices` reads notices' delivery records (operations.dev-mail-notices-in-devnet).

### dependencies.of-documents: What documents depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must documents read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, organizations.
- **Why:** access: the reader of a document. parties: a document's participants are Parties, given when it is stored. organizations: an Organization participant's members may read its documents.

### dependencies.of-equity: What equity depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must equity read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals, accounts, organizations, board, documents.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. parties: investors, grantees and shareholders are resolved to Parties and Safes. indexing: the equity clones' events and positions. approvals: an investor's, grantee's or shareholder's call is an approval from their own Safe. accounts: an invited investor needs a claimed Safe to commit or pay. organizations: the company, its members and its equity deployment. board: every Board matter (issue, round, options, exit) is a Board proposal. documents: round, SAFE and grant documents are stored with their offers.

### dependencies.of-indexing: What indexing depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must indexing read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on parties.
- **Why:** parties: every chain view is scoped by joining parties' directory of Safes and their owners (P8).

### dependencies.of-media: What media depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must media read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access.
- **Why:** access: who uploaded a file, and that only they or its owner may read it.

### dependencies.of-notices: What notices depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must notices read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, accounts, organizations, board, budgets, payments, payroll, banking, requests, equity.
- **Why:** access: the sender address and a recipient's principal. parties: a recipient's mailbox. accounts: referral invites and admissions. organizations: invitations sent, resent and expiring. board: a proposal waiting for a Director. budgets: an allowance assigned to a member, at its amount once the assign executes (dependencies.notices-budgets). payments: a dated payment due or failed, and money received. payroll: an agreement offered, changed or ending. banking: a business verification invitation and a payout returned. requests: a request issued, paid or declined. equity: a round or SAFE invitation.

### dependencies.of-organizations: What organizations depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must organizations read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, media, parties, indexing, approvals, accounts.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. media: an Organization's logo points at a stored file. parties: the Organization's Party is registered at birth; an invitee is resolved by email, handle or Party. indexing: hat wearers, the minter's and Installer's events, and AgentRemoved, which ends an agent's Seats. approvals: the founder's install batch is an approval from the founder's Safe. accounts: the founder must be admitted with a claimed Safe, and an invitation admits its invitee (procedures.accounts-admit).

### dependencies.of-parties: What parties depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must parties read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, media.
- **Why:** access: a person's Party and profile are their principal's, and only they edit them. media: a profile photo points at a stored file.

### dependencies.of-payments: What payments depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must payments read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals, accounts, organizations, board, budgets, books, documents.
- **Why:** access: the payer. parties: the recipient is resolved to a Party and its Safe. indexing: PaymentSent, Transfers, escrow and Budgets events settle, reverse and birth Payments. approvals: a personal or budget payment is an approval from the payer's Safe. accounts: whether the sender and recipient have claimed Safes decides a direct send or a claim escrow. organizations: an `--org` payment's Organization and its company Safe. board: `--as-directors` payments and a company-owned claim escrow's refund are Board proposals. budgets: which budget pays, what it has left and who may spend from it. books: a settled Payment writes its book fact in its own transaction (books.record-money-fact). documents: a Payment's document is stored at its birth (documents.store).

### dependencies.of-payroll: What payroll depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must payroll read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals, organizations, board, budgets, documents.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. parties: the payee is resolved to a Party and its Safe. indexing: Agreements' events drive each agreement's process. approvals: offers, changes, acceptance and collection are approvals from the actor's Safe. organizations: an agreement is an Organization's, read by its members. board: an agreement on a top budget is offered or changed by Board proposal. budgets: an agreement pays from a budget, whose office holder or anyone above may act on it. documents: an agreement's terms document is stored at its offer.

### dependencies.of-requests: What requests depends on, and why

- **Question:** Which concepts must requests read or invoke, and does each edge point the right way?
- **Chosen:** Depend on access, parties, indexing, approvals, organizations, budgets, documents, payments, banking.
- **Why:** access: the caller of every rpc. parties: the issuer and the payer are Parties. indexing: an invoice escrow's events pay a checkout request. approvals: the payer signs an inbox payment's approval: an agent with its own key at once (dependencies.payers-sign-and-name-budgets). organizations: an Organization's requests need the Invoicing hat; its inbox is read by members. budgets: `inbox pay --from` names the budget an Organization pays from by its key, and who may spend from it. documents: a request's invoice document is stored at issue. payments: paying from the inbox births a command; a request's paid state follows payment_transitioned. banking: a matched deposit is marked through banking.match-deposit.

### dependencies.organizations-permissions: Budgets and board depend on organizations, never the reverse

- **Question:** The old organizations and permissions imported each other (76 and 37 imports). Which way does the target point?
- **Chosen:** Budgets and board depend on organizations; organizations reads hats from indexing, never from them.
- **Why:** Organizations needs to know whether an inviter wears the People hat, which is a chain fact (indexing) plus the hat id in its own deployment record. Budgets and board need the company Safe, the members and the hat ids, which are organizations'. The one place the old code went down, an invitation offering grants, becomes the invite flow's second step: budgets proposes the grants for the invitation through the Board (operations.member-invite-in-budgets).

### dependencies.payers-sign-and-name-budgets: Requests and banking depend on approvals and budgets

- **Question:** `inbox pay` and `bank pay` birth a Payment through payments.prepare-command, which opens its approval. Their flows must then let the payer sign, and `--from` names the budget an Organization pays from. Requests and banking depended on neither approvals nor budgets. What do they need?
- **Chosen:** Add approvals and budgets to both: the flow reads and signs the approval, and the rpc resolves `--from` by the budget's key and checks who may spend.
- **Why:** The frozen surface needs both. 'An AI with its own key signs at once': an agent payer signs in the flow (A4), which calls approvals' rpcs, as every other paying concept's flow does. The designed examples name budgets by key (`bank pay ... --from office-costs`, `inbox pay ... --from operations`) and print the budget's name and what it has left, which only budgets holds. The requests designer recorded both as open problems and fell back to the approval page and chain ids (requests.inbox-pay-approval, requests.inbox-pay-funding); a proxy in payments would duplicate approvals and budgets inside the ledger. Both concepts sit below payments, so neither edge makes a cycle.

### dependencies.payments-banking: Banking depends on payments for bank payouts

- **Question:** Old payments drove banking's payout and banking drove the Payment's in-transit states. Which way now?
- **Chosen:** Banking births the payout's Payment through `payments.prepare-command` and follows it; the payout machine holds in-transit, delivered and returned.
- **Why:** The money leaves the Safe in one `Payments.send` to Bridge's deposit address; that Payment settles on chain like any other. What happens at the bank afterwards (in transit, delivered, returned) is Bridge's, so it is the payout's machine (K13 kept, on banking's side). Payments then never needs banking, and a return arrives as a new deposit.

### dependencies.payments-payroll: Payroll sits above payments' dependencies, and payments never depends on payroll

- **Question:** Old payments called payroll's settle-run inside its settlement, and payroll built payments. Which way now?
- **Chosen:** Payments does not depend on payroll; payroll depends on budgets; collections are born as Payments from chain facts.
- **Why:** P4 kept settle-run as a procedure because a settled Payment with its run still open was a money bug. With runs dropped (concepts.payroll), there is no run to settle. An agreement collection is `Agreements.collect`, whose PaymentSent payments settles like any other; the reservation it pays from is on chain. Neither concept writes the other's tables.

### dependencies.payments-requests: Requests depends on payments; the paid notice goes through the outbox

- **Question:** Old payments and requests called into each other at a request's birth and at settlement. Which way now?
- **Chosen:** Requests invokes `payments.prepare-command` to pay from the inbox and follows `payment_transitioned` for paid.
- **Why:** A request is the receivable; a Payment is one way to pay it. The payment of a request and the Payment are one money fact, so the request's rpc births the command through a payments procedure. The request's paid state and its notice may lag a second (P4's own example of the outbox), so they follow the Payment's transition.

### dependencies.board-below-proposers: Every concept that needs the Board depends on board

- **Question:** Budgets, payments, payroll, agents and equity all make Board batches. Which way do they point?
- **Chosen:** They depend on board and open a proposal in their own transaction; they follow `proposal_transitioned`.
- **Why:** The concept that knows the intent (a department, a Board payment, an org-owned agent's freeze) builds the calls; the Board only collects approvals and executes. If board built them it would depend on every money concept. A proposal and the intent it carries are one fact (rules.procedures), so opening it is a procedure.

### dependencies.parties-registration: Organizations and agents register their Party at birth

- **Question:** An Organization's and an agent's handle must be unique across both and route money to their Safe. Who writes it, and when?
- **Chosen:** The birth transaction invokes `parties.register` (handle, Safe, owner); a taken handle fails the birth.
- **Why:** The frozen text says a taken handle creates no Organization, and an invalid logo leaves no Organization or setup row: the birth is all or nothing. A handle and the Safe it routes money to are one money fact (a wrong binding sends money to the wrong Safe), so this is a procedure under rules.procedures. A flow step first would leave reserved handles with no owner; separate handle tables cannot share one unique index. The company Safe's address is known before it is minted (`companySafeAddressFor`).

### environments.devnet: The devnet runs everything on one machine, with worlds as snapshots

- **Question:** What does the devnet environment run, and how fast must it boot?
- **Chosen:** All ten components on loopback; worlds are supervisor snapshots; boot from a golden snapshot within 2 minutes.
- **Why:** Every machine eval runs on a devnet as a black box (spec 30), and settlement is now triggered by Ponder's events, so a devnet without Ponder could not settle a payment. The 2-minute boot is the spec 40 spike's exit criterion.

### environments.staging: A new staging: fresh v4, nothing migrated, host chosen at the spine

- **Question:** What is the new staging?
- **Chosen:** A new standing environment with a fresh v4 deployment on Base Sepolia, no migration from old staging, its host chosen at the spine.
- **Why:** P9 drops pre-v4 entirely, and old staging runs V2 and V3. Y3 leaves the host to the spine, so the record names what runs and what it needs, not where. Turning old staging off is Aaron's irreversible step (P9) and is not taken here.

### integrations.bridge-bank-details-read: Bridge gains a read of one virtual account; no payout cancel, no remove

- **Question:** Bridge had no read of one virtual account, so banking rewrote `bank settle` #1-#4 and could not keep 'It writes nothing to Bridge when Bridge's answer is unusable'. It has no cancel for an unfunded transfer, and a remove nothing calls. Change which?
- **Chosen:** Add `readBankDetails` (one virtual account: status, deposit token, details), as the old client's readVirtualAccount; add no cancel; drop `removeBankAccount`.
- **Why:** A frozen claim is never weakened: `bank settle` #4 is an outcome a black box sees (the Bridge fake records no update when the first answer is unusable), and without a read the target can only promise less. The old settle read the account, checked it, wrote, then read it back (settle.ts:39-46); the read has the shape of getVerification and takes the same numbers and failures. A cancel is not needed: no frozen line asks for one, an unfunded transfer lapses at Bridge, and `bank pay`'s 'nothing is sent' for a changed account is kept by withdrawing the unsigned approval (banking now depends on approvals), not by a call to Bridge. `removeBankAccount` goes: the old client never deleted an external account at Bridge, and removing a saved account calls Bridge no more than it did (banking.not-ported).

### integrations.chain-rpc-live-reads: chain-rpc serves every read of the chain as it is now

- **Question:** chain-rpc's about limited it to reads before a write, an agent's sign-in and the token scan. Notices reads a company Safe's owners and live figures when it sends (notices.chain-reads); payroll, budgets and equity read live views for a show. Within it?
- **Chosen:** Yes: any view whose answer is the chain's state now, which Ponder holds late or not at all, read when a concept needs it as of now: before a write, for a show a person waits on, or when a notice is sent.
- **Why:** A fact that happened is Ponder's, read through indexing's views; state as of now (a budget's left, what an agreement has released, a balance, a Safe's owners) is a view call, which is what chain-rpc answers within its node-read timeout and p95. Notices has no indexing edge and should not gain one for a recipient list (dependencies.notices-top). What stays: chain-rpc never replaces an indexed fact (a settlement, a claim) and scans history only for books' added-token window.

### integrations.indexer: The indexer is a service for what depends on Ponder's progress

- **Question:** Ponder's tables are in the same Postgres. Why an integration at all, and what are its calls?
- **Chosen:** Plain indexed reads are table reads; head, awaitBlock and factsOf are calls, because Ponder may lag, reindex or be rebuilt.
- **Why:** A read that needs a fact Ponder has not indexed yet must wait for it or fail with a code, not answer with nothing (the old system answered 'pending' forever when the indexer was down, old R2). Making those reads calls gives them a timeout, a failure mapping and a `call.indexer.*` span (P6), so a lagging Ponder is visible in every trace that waits on it.

### integrations.posthog: PostHog only through the connector gateway, and nothing waits on it

- **Question:** How does the product reach PostHog?
- **Chosen:** Through the connector gateway, which holds the key: the outbox relay's events, the runtime's telemetry and the flag sync.
- **Why:** The task fixes 'PostHog through the gateway' and Y4 has another thread building it; the key then never sits in the backend's secrets. Every PostHog call is on a background path (relay, export, flag sync), so `balance` #3's 'answers without waiting on PostHog' holds by construction.

### integrations.set: Eleven integrations, two of them new

- **Question:** Which outside systems are Effect services in the target?
- **Chosen:** chain-rpc, bundler, indexer, bridge, mail, openfort, openfort-shield, chainlink, posthog, service-signer and devnet-control; chain-evidence folds into indexer; roles becomes a contract.
- **Why:** The bundler is no longer reached through chain-rpc: it has its own failures and its own budget class. Old chain-evidence checked receipts beside the indexed facts; with one settlement path, a transaction's facts are read from Ponder's tables together (indexer). The service keys (Companies House, the Budgets executor, the escrow relayer, the faucet) were scattered across concepts and an integration folder; one service gives one send-once rule. Openfort has a backend half (Shield's recovery session) and a client half (the signer), on different components. The devnet's control socket is an outside system to the Core SDK.

### integrations.openfort: Openfort is the person's signer in the Core SDK; agents sign locally

- **Question:** Where does signing happen, and which signers are outside systems?
- **Chosen:** The Core SDK's Signer service: Openfort in a browser, the agent's own key or a dev signer locally; only Openfort is an integration.
- **Why:** A4 gives all signing to the Core SDK, and the v4 design keeps the person's key out of Capxul's hands. An agent's key is on the agent's machine (`CAPXUL_PRIVATE_KEY` or a file). Only Openfort is an outside system with failures to map.

### integrations.sponsor-refusals: A sponsorship simulation's budget reverts answer with the budget codes

- **Question:** The bundler's `sponsor` mapped Budgets' `OverBudget` to INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE and every Zodiac Roles revert to ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED. Which codes do the frozen refusals give these reverts?
- **Chosen:** As the designed refusals name them: `OverBudget` and Roles' AllowanceExceeded (the payout ceiling) are PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED, `BudgetIsClosed` WRONG_STATE, `ChildrenExceedParent` CHILDREN_EXCEED_PARENT, `AmountBelowUse` AMOUNT_BELOW_USE; Budgets' `NotAuthorised` and every other Roles or Gate revert stay ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED, and a token's shortfall INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE.
- **Why:** The designed refusals of `payment send`, `payment retry`, `org department pay`, `bank pay`, `inbox pay` and the agreement offers name the contract error beside the code: `OverBudget(id, left, requested)`; Roles `AllowanceExceeded` is PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED, and `BudgetIsClosed(id)` is WRONG_STATE (P10 rung 1). Concepts read `leftOf` before birth (K3), so the simulation is the backstop for a race; a backstop that answers a budget shortfall as the Safe's balance, or the payout ceiling as a missing hat, tells the payer the wrong fix. K18 already says Roles refusals are authority refusals; the ceiling is the one Roles status the frozen text calls a budget refusal. PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED's message now covers a spend as well as an offer or a raise.

### integrations.timeouts: Timeouts and p95s come from the budget classes, not the old 30 s

- **Question:** How are each call's timeout, retries and p95 set?
- **Chosen:** From P5's hop table, so a call fits its rpc's class default; money-moving calls are never retried blindly.
- **Why:** A chain-read operation has p95 2,000 ms (P5), so a 30 s attempt cannot be inside it; one eth_call is 400 ms p95, so 1.5 s per attempt and three nodes keep the worst case near the class. Vendor calls are 2 s p95 at 10 s timeout. A send (bundler, service signer) has no retry: its answer can be lost after it took effect, so the rule is look up, never resend (K4, CAP-1097). Mail retries in the notices workflow, not in the call.

### operations.contact-get-in-banking: `contact get` is banking's flow, because it returns the contact's bank accounts

- **Question:** `contact get` returns `bankAccounts`, which banking owns, and contacts sits below banking. Which concept owns it?
- **Chosen:** Banking: its flow calls contacts' read of the entry, then its own masked accounts for that Party.
- **Why:** The frozen `returns` names `bankAccounts` (P10 rung 1), and the old CLI made a second call for them. A flow composes only concepts its concept depends on, and banking is the one concept above contacts that owns the accounts, as `contact bank add` already is. The contacts designer found this and kept the record in contacts until the map moved it (contacts.get-bank-accounts); the entry's rpc stays contacts' for banking's flow to call.

### operations.dev-mail-notices-in-devnet: `dev mail notices` is devnet's, reading notices' delivery records

- **Question:** `dev mail notices` answers only on a local devnet. May notices, a production concept, own it?
- **Chosen:** Devnet owns it and depends on notices to read the delivery records; notices owns the records and no operation.
- **Why:** P2 gives devnet-only rpcs to the devnet concept, the devnet environment says devnet-only rpcs are the devnet concept's, and CONVENTIONS rule 7 says they live there. `dev mail notices` refuses WRONG_ENVIRONMENT off a local devnet, so in notices it would put a devnet-only rpc in a concept staging runs, the case P2's `devnet-in-production` rule exists to refuse. Reading another concept's table needs only the dependency, and devnet sits at the top, so the edge makes no cycle (dependencies.notices-top already put devnet above notices).

### operations.invite-accept-in-banking: `invite accept` is banking's flow, because it routes business verification

- **Question:** `invite accept` accepts an Organization invitation, or for an `inv_kyb_` id starts the person's verification with `--input`. Which concept owns the flow?
- **Chosen:** Banking: the flow sends an `inv_kyb_` id to its own verification rpc and every other id to organizations' accept rpc.
- **Why:** The CLI calls one SDK method per command (A4), so the routing is one flow, in a concept that depends on both. Banking is the lowest such concept. Keeping a person's verification answers out of organizations matters more than where a two-branch flow sits; the Organization invitation's rpcs and state stay organizations'.

### operations.member-invite-in-budgets: `org member invite` is budgets' flow, because its grants include allowances
