- **Why:** EntryPoint orders each key on its own, so no approval waits behind another or holds a lane for one. v4 has no ordered Roles changes from a personal Safe (old key 0's reason); the Board's order is the company Safe's nonce, which board owns. A retry of a lapsed approval is safe on a fresh key, because `expired` already proves the old nonce never moved. The one case that must share a nonce is the void of a dated payment (payment cancel: 'on the payment's own nonce lane'), so whichever lands first displaces the other.

### approvals.one-table: One approvals table holds the approval and its one UserOperation

- **Question:** The old system kept a prepared operation in three stores: accounts' `approvals` (the page's record and the signature), payments' `moneyExecutions` (the operation, its nonce lane, the send claim, the outcome, the sweep's holds; read at 19 sites by neighbours) and `providerSendAttempts` (one row per bundler call). Which stores does the target keep?
- **Chosen:** One `approvals` row per UserOperation: calls, summary, sponsorship, signature, send and chain proof.
- **Why:** Every approval carries exactly one operation and every operation has exactly one approval (the old relayed kinds already faked an execution id for theirs), so two tables only add a join and a second writer. A send attempt is a span (`call.bundler.send`, P6) and the `claim_send`, `accept` and `bounce` transitions with their events; a log row would be telemetry stored as data. The pre-v4 Attempt states the old validator kept for history (11 of 19), the sweep's recovery holds and the optional fields 'for rows written before them' go (P9).

### approvals.open-answers-live: Opening the same subject again answers the signer's live approval

- **Question:** Frozen lines say 'Asking twice answers with the same approval' and 'Asking again for the same proposal opens the same approval'. Where is that kept?
- **Chosen:** `approvals.open` answers the signer's live approval of the same opener and subject (`opened: false`); an ended one lets a new one open.
- **Why:** One rule for every opener, and the old CAP-943 case (a rebuilt proposal step with a new digest) answers the open approval instead of opening a second signature for one intent. The opener still decides what its subject id is (a command, a proposal and step, an agent change).

### approvals.send-once: One send per signed operation: claim, look up a lost answer, bounce a refusal

- **Question:** How does the process send a signed operation so that it is never sent twice and never lost?
- **Chosen:** Commit `sending` before the call; a lost answer is looked up by hash, never sent again; a definitive refusal the bundler confirms it does not hold returns to `signed` and is sent again after 30 s while the window is open.
- **Why:** K4: a send whose answer was lost may be held, so only a lookup is safe. A definitive refusal of an operation the bundler does not hold cannot land from that send, and the old agreement path already handed such a write back to its approval (CAP-1018: 'A write the bundler refuses goes back to its approval'). Ending it refused would break K6: the signed bytes stay valid until the window closes. The bundler's own `send` takes no retries.

### approvals.sender-and-signer: The sender is the principal's own Safe; the signer is read from that Safe

- **Question:** Where do the sender Safe and the key that must sign come from?
- **Chosen:** Sender from access' `principals.safe_address` at birth; signer from the Safe's owners on chain at sponsorship.
- **Why:** v4 D1: every action comes from the actor's own Safe, so the opener should not be able to name another. Access already holds each principal's Safe and approvals depends on it. Approvals sits below accounts, so it cannot read accounts' setup; the Safe's one owner on chain is the truth the signature must match anyway, and an unclaimed Safe (its owner still Companies House's claim role, or no code) is SMART_ACCOUNT_MISSING before anyone signs.

### approvals.signing-area: Four signer rpcs replace the old seven approval rows and four execution rows

- **Question:** Which of the old public rows the signer used survive, and in what shape?
- **Chosen:** get (live, replacing get and observe), review (kept), sign (replacing approve, consume, submit and submitCommand), decline (replacing reject and abandon).
- **Why:** The process sends, so a client never consumes or submits (A4: the SDK signs, the backend sends). A live read makes `observe` and the SDK's polling wait one subscription. `request` is the opener's procedure now. `review` stays because the frozen approval shape returns `reviewedAt` and the approval page has a caller for it. Recovering a key is local CPU, so `sign` is one transaction (the old action existed only because viem's recovery failed inside a Convex mutation). `readSendWitness` was an e2e probe; a send count is in the trace. Another principal's approval and an unknown id are one answer, INVALID_INPUT `approvalId` (the vocabulary's 'names nothing you can see', and `payment send`'s 'the execution is unknown'), not the old page rows' ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED.

### approvals.sponsor-in-process: The approval's process sponsors it; refusals before signing end it refused

- **Question:** Where does sponsorship happen: in the opener's prepare rpc, in an SDK step, or in the approval's process?
- **Chosen:** The process's first step, after the birth commits; the flow reads the approval live until it is pending.
- **Why:** A procedure makes no outside call and approvals is the bundler's only caller, so the opener's rpc cannot sponsor. Some approvals are opened by workflows with no flow waiting (the founder's install batch), so an SDK step would leave them unsponsored until a page opened. In the process it is one path for all, retried on a paymaster outage and durable. The simulation's refusal keeps the code the bundler's sponsor call maps it to (K18), in `refusal`: INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE, PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED (Budgets' OverBudget, or the payout ceiling's AllowanceExceeded), CHILDREN_EXCEED_PARENT, AMOUNT_BELOW_USE, WRONG_STATE (BudgetIsClosed), ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED (any other Roles, Budgets or Gate refusal) or TRANSACTION_FAILED (updated at assembly to integrations.sponsor-refusals, which once named only three). Each opener maps it to its own frozen refusal (board answers WRONG_STATE `execution_reverts`). The cost is one workflow start on the chain-submit path, inside the operation's budget.

### approvals.summary-from-opener: The opener writes the page's summary once; approvals never reads its openers

- **Question:** The old `request` built the page's summary by reading Payments, Permissions, Organizations, Profiles and Parties, and branched on 13 approval kinds. What builds it now?
- **Chosen:** The opener passes the summary and a free `subject_kind` label in `approvals.open`; approvals stores and shows it.
- **Why:** Approvals sits below every opener (dependencies.approvals-low), so it cannot read them, and the opener already has every name and amount in the transaction that births the intent. The 13 kinds were dispatch for that reading and for per-kind sends; with one send path nothing branches on kind, so it is only the page's label.

### approvals.tenant-is-signer: An approval is its signer's row: tenancy person, keyed by person_id

- **Question:** Whose row is an approval: its signer's, or the Organization's whose money it moves?
- **Chosen:** Tenancy `person`, `person_id` the signing principal (a person, or an agent with its own key); `org_id` only names the Organization.
- **Why:** Only the signer may read or answer an approval (the old page rows refused everyone else), and other Directors follow a proposal, not each other's approvals. Access gives every principal, person or agent, one `person_id`, so one tenant column covers both. An Organization tenant would let every member see each Director's and payer's pending signatures. Three lookups must cross tenants and say why: by id alone (the process, the openers' workflows, the page's link) and by a Safe's nonce (the indexed event names only the Safe).

### approvals.withdraw-procedure: New procedure approvals.withdraw: an ended intent takes its unsigned carrier

- **Question:** When an opener's intent ends before anyone signed its approval, how does the approval end with it?
- **Chosen:** `approvals.withdraw`, in the opener's transaction, for a sponsoring or pending approval; a signed one is replaced, not withdrawn.
- **Why:** Money fact (rules.procedures, 'a chain write and the approval that carries it'): if a cancelled Payment command and its still-pending approval disagreed at a commit, the person could sign and move money Capxul records as cancelled. Letting it lapse leaves that window open for up to the whole deadline. Approvals cannot follow its openers, which sit above it. A signed approval cannot be withdrawn (K6), which is why the frozen cancel of a dated payment is a void.

## banking (30)

### banking.acceptance-rewrites: Seven acceptance lines that named a mechanism, rewritten as outcomes

- **Question:** Which frozen acceptance lines of banking's operations name a mechanism a black box cannot see (P1)?
- **Chosen:** `invite accept` #1 (one transaction, a durable drive call), `bank accounts` #5 and `bank deposits` #5 ('schedules nothing'), `bank accounts add` #2 and `contact bank add` #3 (a scheduled job), and `bank settle` #2 and #3 (a queued event). `bank settle` #1 and #4 are kept: the trace observer sees Bridge's calls in order.
- **Why:** A scheduler, a queue, a transaction and the order of internal calls are mechanisms. Each rewrite keeps the outcome the old test proved and still passes against the old system: a read leaves the world unchanged; a save sends one event and a replay none; settling a just-opened account succeeds; a token change is recorded once and the same token changes nothing. `bank settle` reads Bridge before it writes again, now that the shared Bridge integration has a read of one virtual account (banking.settle-reads-first).

### banking.bank-details-machine: Bank details: `failed` is reached and left, and a token change is a transition

- **Question:** Old bank details had a `failed` state nothing reached, and changed the settlement token outside the machine. Keep that?
- **Chosen:** Bridge refusing to open is `fail`, asking again is `reopen`; `change_token` is a transition on an active account.
- **Why:** The frozen `bank details` returns `failed`, so it must mean something: a refusal to open. A token change is what `bank settle` records once (its old acceptance queued one `bank_details_changed`); as a transition it sends the machine's one event (P7), and the same token sends none. Deactivate and reactivate stay Bridge's: no frozen operation does them, so the old rpcs go (P2).

### banking.budgets: Class budgets, with three overrides and the person's wait excluded

- **Question:** Which banking records leave their class default (P5)?
- **Chosen:** `verify start` and its flow (p95 10 s: document uploads), and `bank pay` with its quote rpc and `bank settle` with its rpc (p95 5 s: two Bridge calls in sequence); every prompt and approval excluded as `wait.person`, and `bank pay`'s chain confirmation.
- **Why:** A vendor call is 2 s p95 (integrations.timeouts), so two in sequence cannot meet the vendor class's 4 s, and uploading up to 24 MB documents is network time no class anticipates. Everything else is one Bridge call (vendor), a read, a write, or background work.

### banking.contact-get: `contact get` is contacts' entry, then the accounts saved on it here

- **Question:** The architecture review moved `contact get` to banking (shared operations.contact-get-in-banking) and contacts dropped its record (contacts.get-moved). How does banking book it, and where do a contact's accounts come from?
- **Chosen:** One flow, `client.account.address-book.get` (the frozen method): at a terminal with no PartyId the picker (`contacts.book.list`, hidden included), then `contacts.book.get`, and for an entry a new query `banking.accounts.list-for-contact` over the `bank-accounts-by-party` index. Null ends it with no second read.
- **Why:** A4 gives the operation one SDK flow, and the old CLI made exactly these calls (the entry, then the contact's accounts). `banking.accounts.list` implements `bank accounts`, whose frozen page lists only your own; a filter would give one rpc two meanings. A banking rpc reading contacts' table would repeat contacts' book rule (manual or member). The acceptance observations and the guided-read budget (p95 1,600 ms) are the ones contacts handed over; the refusals start from contacts' world person-with-contacts and signed-out.

### banking.deposit-credit: A deposit is credited from the indexer at finality, with its book fact

- **Question:** How does a deposit become credited, and what makes its book fact once?
- **Chosen:** A process per arriving deposit reads Bridge's transaction from the indexer at finality and credits the exact Transfer; an Organization's book fact is keyed by that Transfer.
- **Why:** A credit waits on the chain, so it is a process with its own wait, not a sweep (P3). Reading at the finality depth means a reorg cannot undo a credit, so banking never reverses a deposit's fact. The book fact's key is `transfer:<chain>:<tx>:<log>`, the key payments' Movement of the same Transfer uses, so books keeps one live fact whoever writes first (books.money-key). A person's deposit has no book fact: books are an Organization's.

### banking.deposit-machine: A deposit's state is its public status; its match is a machine of its own

- **Question:** Old deposits kept Bridge's seven bank states and a refund state and derived the public status from them. What does the target store?
- **Chosen:** The five public statuses as the machine, and the match (unmatched, request, not request) as a second machine.
- **Why:** No frozen operation shows Bridge's finer states or an in-flight refund; `bank deposits` shows the status reached, and storing it makes every write to it a named transition (P7). An in-flight or failed refund is Bridge's affair and reads as processing. The match changes independently of the status and only once, through requests' procedure, so it is its own machine on the same row.

### banking.flows: Six flows; the one-call operations are their rpc

- **Question:** Which banking operations need a Core SDK flow (A4), and what are the flows called?
- **Chosen:** Flows for `verify start`, `verify status`, `invite accept`, `bank pay`, `contact get`, and one shared by `bank accounts add` and `contact bank add`; every other operation is one rpc.
- **Why:** A flow is for an operation that composes rpcs with prompts, uploads, waits or polls. The two add commands share the frozen SDK method `client.account.bankAccounts.add`, so one flow serves both. `bank settle`'s picker only chooses `--to` from two tokens before the one call, so it stays an rpc. `bank pay`'s frozen method `client.approvals.prepare` was shared by every payment command; its flow is named `client.banking.payouts.pay` so it has one meaning. `contact get` composes contacts' entry with the accounts saved on it (banking.contact-get). `bank pay`'s signing follows banking.payers-sign: an agent paying from an Organization's budget signs at once.

### banking.inbox: Bridge's events are applied in the transaction that stores them

- **Question:** Old banking stored each event, scheduled a processing run, backed failures off and parked them, and leased the catch-up. How does the target take Bridge's events?
- **Chosen:** Store and apply in one transaction; what cannot apply yet stays pending for the five-minute catch-up.
- **Why:** Applying an event is a few writes and signals, with no outside call, so it fits the webhook's own transaction, and Bridge's retry plus the catch-up cover any failure: a process per event would be plumbing (P3). The catch-up is the one Bridge sweep P3 keeps; as a singleton it needs no lease, and every five minutes is enough behind a webhook that normally arrives in seconds. The devnet's `readsFrom` goes with the old shared Bridge sandbox it guarded. The inbox's own status is a machine because the catch-up reads it.

### banking.invite-accept-declined: Declining an invitation's offer at the terminal ends CANCELLED

- **Question:** `invite accept` at a terminal shows the offer and uses its digest. What does a 'no' to it end with?
- **Chosen:** CANCELLED, from the flow's `digest` prompt, which lists it in its `errors`; nothing is accepted.
- **Why:** vocabulary.declined-preview-cancelled: a 'no' is the person's choice, not a usage mistake. A script still passes `--offer-digest` and `--confirm` and never sees the question; the invitation stays pending for a later accept or decline.

### banking.keys-from-records: Bridge's Idempotency-Key comes from the record, so a write calls Bridge first

- **Question:** Old banking wrote a row with its creation key, called Bridge in an action, then finalized the row (prepare, finalize). An rpc is one transaction now. How is a Bridge create made exactly once?
- **Chosen:** Derive each key from the record (owner, currency, request key), call Bridge, then write once.
- **Why:** Bridge replays the first answer for a repeated key, so a retry after a lost answer or a failed transaction reaches the same customer, virtual account, external account or transfer; with the key derivable, nothing must be stored before the call. It removes eight old rpcs (the prepare, finalize and load pairs) and keeps every write one transaction (A3). A verification's key is the owner's; bank details' the owner, currency and attempt; an account's and a payout's the owner and request key.

### banking.manage-authority: Managing an Organization's banking needs a Director or the Books officer

- **Question:** The frozen text says opening details, settling, saving and removing accounts for an Organization needs 'current authority to manage it'. Which hats are that on v4?
- **Chosen:** A Director (the Director hat) or the Books officer (the Books hat); verifying the business needs a Director.
- **Why:** Old banking checked the Organization's manager role, which v4 replaces with the Director hat and the three officer hats. Bank details and saved accounts are part of the company's books of money in and out, the Books officer's office; Bridge's KYB needs a controlling person, so the business's verification stays with Directors. A payout itself is authorized by the budget's hat on chain, not by this. Reads (lists, deposits) need only a Seat.

### banking.match-in-requests: Matching moves to requests; banking keeps only the deposit's side

- **Question:** Old banking listed match candidates, matched, and auto-matched by reference. What stays in banking?
- **Chosen:** Only `banking.match-deposit`; candidates and the match by reference are requests'.
- **Why:** concepts.requests puts `bank match` in requests, which reads banking's deposits (it depends on banking). A match by reference is a request's judgment, so requests reacts to `bank_deposit_transitioned` (credit) for it. The deposit and the request it paid are one money fact, so the deposit side is the procedure, with `none` for not a request.

### banking.new-payee: A new payee in `bank pay --input` is a saved account, not a new contact

- **Question:** Old `bank pay --input` could create a contact with their bank details. Does banking create a contact?
- **Chosen:** Save the payee's bank account with their name (scope payee); the person adds a contact with `contact add` if they want one.
- **Why:** An address book entry is not part of the money fact, and every contact names a Party, which a name with bank numbers is not. Saving the account alone pays the bank exactly as before and keeps contacts' book something its owner curates. No frozen acceptance line of `bank pay` names the contact.

### banking.not-ported: Old banking rpcs and workflows with no target caller are not ported

- **Question:** Old banking booked 41 rpcs and 8 workflows. Which have no place in the target?
- **Chosen:** Drop the prepare, finalize and load pairs, bank details' deactivate and reactivate, the payout conversion, notice facts, overdue and watchdog, the staging mint, the inbox's load, record and back-off, the catch-up's cursor, lease, release, recover and advance, deposit candidates and auto-match, and verification document upload and delete.
- **Why:** P2: each is either an internal hop the one-transaction rpcs and processes absorb, an operation no frozen command reaches (deactivate, reactivate, the conversion of pre-v4 rows, P9), a read another concept now does on banking's tables (notices' facts, requests' candidates), or media's (document upload). `payments.quotes.quote`, which old banking implemented for payments, is `banking.payouts.quote`. Removing a saved account calls Bridge no more than the old one did, so Bridge has no remove call (integrations.bridge-bank-details-read dropped it).

### banking.one-bank-account-store: One store of saved bank accounts, for yourself, a contact or a named payee

- **Question:** Old parties kept bank accounts in `partyAccounts` and again as the bank kind of `counterpartyDestinations`. Which does the target keep?
- **Chosen:** One `bank-accounts` table in banking with a scope: self, contact (a contact's Party) or payee (a holder name only).
- **Why:** Both described the same thing, an external bank account a payout goes to, and only payouts use one; parties keeps wallet destinations (responsibilities.destinations). A payee known only by name has no Party, since contacts always name one, so its account carries the holder name and no Party. The frozen `partyAccountId` is the row's id.

### banking.owner-tenancy: Every banking row is an owner's: a person's or an Organization's

- **Question:** Old rows were keyed by a Party, an account or an actor id, each table its own way. What tenant column do they carry?
- **Chosen:** Tenancy `owner`: `owner_kind` and `owner_id` (a person_id or an org_id) on every row, leading the key and every index.
- **Why:** The row-level policy sets the session's person or Organization tenant (P8), which is an owner, not a Party; contacts and payments' rows use the same pair. The Party stays a column where the row names one (whose Safe a deposit lands in, whose account a payout pays). A business verification's people are the Organization's rows; the invited person reaches and submits their own through its invitation id and their email, the one cross-tenant read and write in banking, which the generated row-level policy must allow by that clause. Bridge's inbox and cursor are the deployment's (`global`). The old tables mixed `ownerPartyId`, `actorId` and `declaredBy`, so a tenancy by join.

### banking.payers-sign: `bank pay --from` takes a budget's key, and an agent payer signs at once

- **Question:** Banking now depends on approvals and budgets (shared dependencies.payers-sign-and-name-budgets). The designed `bank pay --org acme --from office-costs` names the budget by key and prints its name; an Organization's payer may be an agent Seat wearing the budget's hat. What does banking use them for?
- **Chosen:** The quote resolves `from` through budgets' table (key to chain id and name; a chain id as it is) and answers the budget in the view; the flow follows the sponsored approval (`approvals.signing.get`), and an agent signs its digest with its own key and gives it to `approvals.signing.sign`; a person still approves on the page.
- **Why:** The designed example takes the key and prints 'from Office costs' (P10 rung 1), and requests' `inbox pay` already resolves `--from` the same way, so one key means one budget everywhere. A person's bank pay needs their own Bridge verification, but an Organization's needs only the Organization's (KYB), so an agent wearing the budget's hat can pay and, under A4, signs in the flow with its own key, as payroll's and requests' flows do. A declined approval ends CANCELLED and an expired one WRONG_STATE (approvals.machine).

### banking.payout-deadline: A payout's Payment cannot move after its quote; a changed account withdraws it

- **Question:** Banking reported two holes: nothing stopped a payout's Payment landing, or being retried, after the quote's deadline, and a changed bank account could not withdraw an unsigned approval. Payments now takes `not_after` and an item with no document (payments.door-inputs) and leaves the withdrawal to the opener (payments.withdraw-by-opener). What does banking do?
- **Chosen:** The quote passes `not_after` = the quote's `expires_at` and an item with a null document and the zero hash, and keeps the approval's id on the payout; the payout's process, ending a quoted payout on its account's removal or deactivation, invokes `approvals.withdraw` in that transaction. Answered `signed`, it stays quoted and follows its Payment.
- **Why:** bank.pay's WRONG_STATE says 'Nothing is sent' for both an expired quote and a changed account. With the deadline on the command, the approval's window and any retry end at the quote's expiry; with the withdrawal, nobody can sign after the account changed. Only an approval already signed can still land, which only the chain can end (K6): the payout then follows its Payment, and Bridge delivers to the account it still holds. The withdrawal and the payout's end are one fact (the payout ended and its unsigned carrier with it), which is `approvals.withdraw`'s own money fact (P4).

### banking.payout-machine: Seven payout states; `funding` is read from the Payment

- **Question:** Old payouts had twelve states for a dispatcher, timers and a funding claim. Which does the target keep?
- **Chosen:** quoted, in_transit, delivered, returning, refunded, expired, needs_attention; `funding` derived from the Payment on read.
- **Why:** The quote now creates Bridge's transfer before the payout exists, so `Creating` and `Refused` cannot be stored; Bridge lets an unfunded transfer lapse, so `Cancelling` and `Cancelled` are `expired`; `ClosedUnfunded` is a reason of needs_attention. `Funding` was the payout's copy of its Payment being sent: payments owns that (`submitted`), so `banking.payouts.get` reports stage `funding` from it rather than copying it. `delivered` is not final, because a bank can return a delivered payout (K13).

### banking.payout-payment: The quote births the payout and its Payment in one transaction

- **Question:** Old payments called back into banking at the send claim to accept the quote. How are the payout and its Payment bound?
- **Chosen:** The quote rpc reads the funding, then in one transaction writes the payout, names Bridge's deposit address as an outside Party and births the Payment command through `payments.prepare-command`.
- **Why:** The payout and the Payment that funds it are one money fact (rules.procedures): a Payment to a deposit address with no payout following it would send money Capxul cannot account for. Births in one transaction remove the old callback loop (payments never reads banking, dependencies.payments-banking). The deposit address is a counterparty the Payment routes money to, so it is bound by `parties.resolve-recipient`. The funding read precedes the transaction, as prepare-command requires.

### banking.payout-return-reference: A payout's return fact references the payout's Payment

- **Question:** Books' reclassify #3 needs a v4 row with money facts in two periods (books.straddling-row): a payout settled in one month and returned in the next. Which reference does banking pass on the return's `payout_returned` fact?
- **Chosen:** `{kind: payment, id: <the payout's Payment id>}`, keyed (`money_key`) by the return's Transfer as before.
- **Why:** Books groups a row's facts by their reference. With the payout's Payment as the reference, the settlement and the return are one row's two money facts, the shape the old commitment had, so a return after the month's end straddles it. The money key stays the Transfer, so payments' Movement of the same Transfer is still the same fact and is not booked twice (books.money-key). The quote's other new inputs to `payments.prepare-command` are stated too: `pay_at` null (a payout pays at once) and `resume` false (a payout never reopens a lapsed command; a new quote is a new payout).

### banking.processes: One process per payout replaces the dispatcher, timers and watchdog

- **Question:** Old payouts were driven by a dispatcher, one live timer each with its job, a ten-minute watchdog for lost timers, a notify workflow and one door with a log. What drives a payout now?
- **Chosen:** One durable process per payout, with its own waits (the Payment, the deadline, Bridge's events, the refund).
- **Why:** A3 makes a bank payout one process, and the workflow engine (A2) keeps its timers and survives crashes, which is what the dispatcher's leases, the timer jobs and the watchdog existed for. Each wait has the old limit: the deadline, 7 days to deliver, 14 days for a refund, and a 60-day window after delivery for a bank's return. No cancel call is booked: Bridge lets an unfunded transfer lapse, and the Payment cannot land after the deadline unless payments lets it (a problem left to payments' design).

### banking.resolve-signal: An operator's resolution stays, as the payout process's signal

- **Question:** Old payouts needing attention were resolved by hand from the Convex dashboard. Keep a resolution?
- **Chosen:** Keep `banking.payouts.resolve`, an operator-only signal of the payout's process.
- **Why:** Money that left a Safe and stopped (Bridge moved it unfunded, a wrong amount, a refund past its limit) needs a person, and without a resolution the process would wait to its timeout. As a signal it is reached (P2), and the process, not the rpc, writes the outcome, so the machine keeps one owner.

### banking.sandbox-deposits: Test networks' deposits get their tokens from the Bridge fake, not from banking

- **Question:** Old banking minted test tokens once for each sandbox deposit on staging. Keep the mint?
- **Chosen:** Drop it: the devnet's Bridge stand-in (and staging's sandbox harness) sends the test tokens itself.
- **Why:** A mint step is code that runs only off production and makes the credit path differ by environment; the fake is where an environment's difference belongs (the devnet's integrations are fakes). The credit process then has one path everywhere: Bridge names a transaction, the indexer shows it.

### banking.settle-reads-first: `bank settle` reads the virtual account at Bridge before it writes

- **Question:** Banking had rewritten `bank settle` #1 to #4 because Bridge had no read of one virtual account. The shared Bridge record now has `readBankDetails`. Does settle use it, and which lines go back to their frozen words?
- **Chosen:** Read, then change only a different token: #1 ('reads Bridge before it writes') and #4 ('writes nothing to Bridge when Bridge's answer is unusable') are kept word for word, observed by the trace; #2 and #3 stay rewritten.
- **Why:** A frozen claim is rewritten only when a black box cannot see what it names (P1); the order of two outside calls is visible to the trace observer, so the rewrite had stood only for want of the call. Reading first is also the safer write: a token Bridge already has is no change, and an unusable read sends nothing. #2 and #3 still name a queued event, which P7 replaces. The second call needs a 5 s p95, as `bank pay`'s two calls do.

### banking.tables: Eight tables: old banking's and old parties' rows, consolidated

- **Question:** Old banking had eight tables (two retired but holding rows) and old parties four more for verification and bank accounts. Which does the target keep, and in what shape?
- **Chosen:** verifications, verification-people, bank-details, bank-accounts, bank-deposits, bank-payouts, bridge-events, bridge-sync; the retired quotes and rail operations, the payout log and verification documents go.
- **Why:** Each kept table is a business object a frozen operation shows or a process follows. The retired `quotes` and `railOperations` rows exist only for the old conversion (P9: nothing is migrated). The append-only `payoutTransitions` log is the workflow engine's history and the `bank_payout_transitioned` events now. Verification documents were transfer buffers: media stores them (concepts.media). Every table drops the plumbing columns (observation context, journey, correlation and surface ids, back-off, park, lease and run columns, P2, P3), and every private value is encrypted bound to its row with the one RECORD_ENCRYPTION_KEY, so a value copied to another row does not decrypt.

### banking.verification-files: A verification admits only held identity documents of the right purpose

- **Question:** Media admits each file for one purpose and leaves the check to its consumer (media.files: INVALID_INPUT, raised by the consumer). Which files may `banking.verification.start` send to Bridge?
- **Chosen:** Each read at the caller's own key: `id_front` and `id_back` must be `id-image`; every other document field `id-image` or `document`; each still `held`. Anything else is INVALID_INPUT naming the field.
- **Why:** Bridge's ID fields need a photo (JPG or PNG) and its other fields take a PDF too, which are exactly media's two document purposes. A `held` file is one whose bytes still exist (30 minutes after upload), so an expired one fails here with the field named rather than at Bridge with a reason the person may not see. The caller's own key means a file someone else uploaded names nothing.

### banking.verification-people: A business's people each submit their own details, by invitation

- **Question:** A Director can add the business's people. Who submits each person's identity details?
- **Chosen:** Each named person gets an `inv_kyb_` invitation and submits their own details with `verify start` or `invite accept`.
- **Why:** Old parties did this, and it keeps another person's identity documents out of the Director's hands: the Director gives only names, email and legal roles. The invitation grants no Organization permission. Notices sends its email from `verification_person_transitioned` (invite), so banking sends no mail. The delivery state leaves the row.

### banking.verification-process: A verification's process follows Bridge to its decision, then ends

- **Question:** rules.durable-processes gives a verification one durable process. What does it own, and what applies Bridge's changes once the decision is made?
- **Chosen:** The process applies Bridge's snapshots until every currency is decided, then ends; the inbox applies later changes.
- **Why:** Until the decision there is something to wait for (the person on Bridge's page, every named person of a business, Bridge's review) and one place must hold a business out of review until its people submit, which the old `businessApprovalReported` flag did. After it, Bridge's rare later changes need no waiting and the inbox applies them in the same order. `verify start` drives `begin` and Bridge's immediate answer, as a signal rpc may; `verify status` reads Bridge back and writes nothing, as its frozen `changes` says.

### banking.worlds: The devnet worlds banking's refusals start from

- **Question:** Which named devnet snapshots reach each refusal of banking's twelve operations?
- **Chosen:** New: person-verified (approved for USD, EUR and GBP, no details or accounts); person-with-bank-details (plus USD and EUR details, EUR settling in EURC); person-with-gbp-account (verified, a registered GBP account, 1,000 USDC in the Safe); person-with-deposits (bank details and 21 USD deposits: credited, arriving, below minimum, returned, some matched); org-verified (Globex, KYB-approved: Fay the founding Director, Gus wearing the `office-costs` hat with 2,000 USDC left, Hal a member with no hat, Ivy outside; a registered GBP account and USD details); bridge-unreachable (person-with-bank-details with the Bridge stand-in failing every call). Reused: signed-out, person-ready, org-with-pending-invitation and org-with-expired-invitation (organizations.worlds), person-with-contacts (contacts.worlds).
- **Why:** Each world is built by running operations as the devnet's fixed identities against the Bridge stand-in, and saved as a snapshot named for what it holds; reusing the other concepts' worlds keeps one snapshot per situation. A quote expiring before approval is reached from person-with-gbp-account with `dev time warp`.

## board (23)

### board.acceptance-events: Lines about the old Board events name each machine's one event

- **Question:** Lines of the six Board changes say a landing 'posts `board_seat_transitioned`' or `board_threshold_transitioned`; grant and revoke say 'it schedules one event'. P7 keeps one event per machine. How are the lines kept?
- **Chosen:** Rewrite each as 'reports `board_change_transitioned` (`<transition>`) once', or `officer_change_transitioned` (`propose`), keeping what it carries and omits.
- **Why:** The claims are about what a funnel sees exactly once and what it never carries (an email, a Safe, the Seat, the note), which the trace observer checks on the target's event; only the event's name is a mechanism. The old transition names map to the machine's: claimed to claim, seated to seat, removed to remove, lapsed to lapse, handed_over to hand_over, set to set_threshold. The claim's 'seated as of its block' part is kept.

### board.acceptance-pre-v4: Pre-v4 lines and clauses: one retired, one clause cut, the v4 openings stripped

- **Question:** Which board lines describe pre-v4 behaviour, and what becomes of them (P9)?
- **Chosen:** `org board show` #3 (a pre-v4 Organization refused) retires as null; `org member grant` #8 loses 'and is refused before v4'; grant #6, #7 and revoke #5 lose 'On a v4 company'.
- **Why:** P9 names show #3 as one of the two pre-v4-only lines and grant #8 as one of the two with a pre-v4 clause; no pre-v4 Organization exists in the target, so neither can be observed. The openings are P9's mechanical strip. The refusals' FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE for 'Manage people before v4' becomes the gates' refusal, which is what that code still means on v4 (board.proposals-gate); approve gains it as a refusal because its line #5 already claims it.

### board.approval-endings: A Board change's unlanded approval ends with approvals' frozen-code mapping

- **Question:** Approvals asks every opener to map an approval's endings onto the frozen codes (approvals.machine). Board's change process said only 'fails it with that code'. Which code does each ending give?
- **Chosen:** declined and withdrawn CANCELLED; expired and displaced WRONG_STATE; reverted TRANSACTION_FAILED; refused the code approvals stored.
- **Why:** The frozen pages of `org board` commands refuse only CANCELLED, WRONG_STATE and the sponsorship codes, and the change flows already declare CANCELLED, WRONG_STATE and TRANSACTION_FAILED. A person's no is CANCELLED (the vocabulary merged SIGNER_REJECTED into it), a lapse or a used nonce is a state that no longer allows the change, and a refusal at sponsorship already carries its own code (integrations.sponsor-refusals). Agents maps its carriers the same way (agents.failure-codes), so one CLI answers alike. A proposal's own end (`fail`, `reject`, `replace`) is board's and reads as `failed`, `rejected` or `replaced` (board.failed-proposal).

### board.approval-step: The step is decided from the indexed approvals; only the current nonce executes

- **Question:** The old rpc waited up to ten minutes for other Directors' approvals in flight before choosing approveHash or the execution. An rpc is one transaction now. How is the step chosen?
- **Chosen:** From the approvals indexed on chain of Directors who still sign, with the caller's, against the Gate's threshold, and only at the Safe's current nonce; otherwise approveHash. A Director runs approve again to execute later.
- **Why:** Counting an approval that may still fail would build an execution that reverts. Waiting is not an rpc's job (A3). Executing with Capxul's own key would make Capxul act for the Board, which the frozen text never says: the approval that reaches the threshold executes it. A Director whose approval is already on chain may run it again once the proposal can execute, and their approval is then the execution; before that it is WRONG_STATE, as the frozen mistake says.

### board.changes: Board changes from a person's own Safe: one process each, one at a time

- **Question:** Seats, removals, the threshold, the handover, a claim and a refresh are approvals from one person's Safe, never proposals. How are they kept, and may two run at once?
- **Chosen:** One `board-changes` row with its approval and one process each; one change under way per Organization.
- **Why:** Each change is planned from the Board as it stands (the Directors and threshold before and after, the hat's supply, who lapsed), so a second running beside it can make its plan wrong: a removal beside a threshold rise trips the Gate's ThresholdTooLow, two seats beside a full hat both raise the supply. Where the old rules differed by kind, the stricter wins (P10 rung 3) and every frozen refusal still holds. The row keeps what the frozen reads return (`dc_` id, person, removed, counts) and what the chain made of it; the calls and the send are the approval's. Its process replaces the old follow job and record-outcome.

### board.claim-seat: `--seat` names nothing but the one Director hat until Board elections exist

- **Question:** `org board claim --seat <group>` picks a seat group when elected to more than one. No seat groups exist yet (CAP-1153). What does it do?
- **Chosen:** Accept it; any group but the Board's one Director hat is INVALID_INPUT, and `seat` stays null.
- **Why:** The flag is frozen surface (P10 rung 1). Until elections create seat hats the only hat to claim is the Director hat, and saying so is better than ignoring the flag.

### board.director-commands: The four `org director` writes check the caller, then refuse; pre-v4 lines go

- **Question:** `org director add`, `remove`, `replace` and `threshold` propose the company Safe's own owner calls, which the v4 Signer Gate refuses (`CannotCallSafe`); every target Organization is v4 (P9). The designed text moves them to `org board`, with no alias. What do the four frozen operations become, and which of their frozen refusals remain?
- **Chosen:** Each SDK method reads where the caller and the named members stand (`board.directors.authority`) and gives its frozen refusals: NOT_AUTHENTICATED without a sign-in, ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED for a caller who is not a Director, INVALID_INPUT for a person who is not a member or a number the Board cannot take, and for `threshold` its designed answers, RESERVED_MATTER once the Governor wears the Shareholders' top hat and ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED for a caller who does not; otherwise WRONG_STATE naming its v4 command. Lines that describe the pre-v4 owner change retire as null, and line 1 becomes the refusal.
- **Why:** The frozen refusal codes and their exits are the public surface (P10, rung 1), and the old system reaches them on a v4 company: it signs the caller in (NOT_AUTHENTICATED) and checks their Seat (ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED) before `ownerChangeGated`, and its input schema refuses INVALID_INPUT before both. Failing at once would answer a signed-out caller or an outsider WRONG_STATE, exit 2 instead of 3 or 4, which a script reads as a state to wait out, and a refusal is not dropped because the method could skip a read. So each method reads first, and the checks it can make are the frozen pages' own (not a Director, not a member, the last Director, a threshold the Directors cannot meet). For `threshold` the designed text wins (P10): only the top hat may change it, so it answers as `org board threshold` does and sends only the top hat's wearer to that command. `board.directors.safe` names neither the top hat nor the members, hence a read of its own, which writes nothing. An alias is ruled out by the designed text; a Board batch cannot work, because the Director hat's admin is the Shareholders' top hat, not the company Safe. Lines like 'removing the last Director is refused' still hold and are kept. `org director list` is a v4 read and is unchanged.

### board.director-refusals-ask-nothing: The refused `org director` writes ask nothing, so they list no CANCELLED

- **Question:** The shared assembly listed `org director add`, `remove`, `replace` and `threshold` among commands whose terminal preview should end CANCELLED. Do they ask?
- **Chosen:** No: each method reads the Board (`board.directors.authority`) and refuses before any question, with its frozen refusal or WRONG_STATE naming the `org board` command to use; `--confirm` is accepted and changes nothing.
- **Why:** On v4 the Signer Gate refuses an owner change the Safe makes itself (board's refused flows), so there is nothing to confirm. Asking first would make a person agree to a change that cannot happen, and listing CANCELLED would declare an error the flow never produces. The `org board` commands that do ask already list CANCELLED.

### board.dropped: The old proposals', directors' and grants' plumbing is not ported

- **Question:** Which old rpcs and workflows of organizations/proposals, organizations/directors and permissions/grants have no target record?
- **Chosen:** Raw `propose` and `changeOwners` (no frozen command on v4), `pay` and `cancelScheduled` (payments'), every `sendSigned` (approvals'), `load`, `ownerLabels`, `ownerCandidates`, `paymentRecipient`, `record`, `recordSubmission`, `recordOutcome`, `settle` and `emitBoardTransitioned` (steps or transitions now), `noticeFacts` and `notify` (notices'), the directors' follow job (the change's process), and the granter key's reserve, sign, broadcast, drive, verify and retry (P9: grants are Board batches).
- **Why:** P2 keeps only what an operation, flow step, signal or trigger reaches; each of these was a step of one operation, an outside call now owned by approvals or notices, or a pre-v4 path. Events are sent by transitions (P7), so an emit rpc has nothing to do. Old staging keeps running these until Aaron turns it off.

### board.execution-refusal: An execution the chain refuses is WRONG_STATE, from the approval's sponsorship

- **Question:** The frozen refusal says an execution the chain refuses is WRONG_STATE (`execution_reverts`) before the Director signs. The bundler's simulation maps a Gate revert to ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED and others to TRANSACTION_FAILED. Who simulates?
- **Chosen:** Approvals' sponsorship simulates as for every approval; the approve flow answers WRONG_STATE `execution_reverts` for a refused execution.
- **Why:** The sponsorship already simulates the exact operation before anyone can sign (K18), so a second simulation would only race it. The flow knows the step it opened, so it reports the frozen code for an execution and keeps the chain's own refusal as the cause; nothing was signed or sent either way. An approval that lands reverted (the proposal changed on chain first) is WRONG_STATE too, as the frozen mistake says.

### board.failed-proposal: A proposal whose execution the Safe reports failed is `failed`

- **Question:** The frozen statuses are awaiting_approvals, executed, rejected and replaced. A Safe transaction can run at its nonce and fail (ExecutionFailure) when it is executed with gas that lets the inner call fail. What is it then?
- **Chosen:** A fifth state, `failed`: the nonce is used and nothing it carried happened.
- **Why:** Capxul's own executions send no safe gas, so a failing call reverts the whole execution and the nonce does not move; but once the approvals are on chain anyone may execute it, and an ExecutionFailure then uses the nonce. Calling it executed would make openers apply an intent that never happened (budgets, payments and agents settle on it); calling it replaced hides that it was this proposal that ran. The status value is added to the frozen list, which never said what this case is (P10 rung 5).

### board.handover-equity: The handover reads the equity install from the chain and board's own proposals

- **Question:** `org board handover` waits for the equity setup's Board batch and sends the founder calls `buildEquity` returns. Board cannot depend on equity. How does it know equity is installed, and where do the calls come from?
- **Chosen:** Installed when indexing's chain-deployments holds the company's Governor and the equity install's Board proposal (opener equity) executed; board builds the three Hats calls from the deployment's hat ids and the Governor.
- **Why:** Equity sits above board, so its tables are out of reach; the Governor is a chain fact and the install's Board batch is a proposal board itself stores, so both are board's to read. `buildEquity` needs the share classes and terms only equity knows; its founder calls are fixed (the Director hat's eligibility and toggle, then the top hat last), so board builds the same calls and the claim's chain observer checks they equal them, as the old code did.

### board.live-board-reads: The Board reads ask the chain live; proposal reads read tables

- **Question:** The Board reads say 'as the chain answers it now'; the proposal reads were settled from the chain on every read. What reads live?
- **Chosen:** Owners, threshold and each owner's `isValidSigner` live from the chain; proposals from board's tables and indexing's approvals.
- **Why:** Whether the Gate accepts a lapsed owner's signature is the Gate's live answer (`isValidSigner`), which no event records when a term simply ends, so the Board reads ask it (chain-read). A proposal's status changes only when its nonce is used, which its process settles from the indexed execution, so reading it needs no chain call and stays within `read`; its approvals so far are indexed ApproveHash events joined to the Safe's owners now.

### board.officer-changes: Officer hat changes are settled by their proposal's process; Seat permissions go

- **Question:** Old grants wrote `seatPermissionChanges` and, once executed, `seatPermissions`, which books and requests checked. On v4 the permission is the hat. What does board keep, and who settles it?
- **Chosen:** Keep the change (what the frozen grant and revoke return), settled in the proposal's settling transaction; drop the permission rows.
- **Why:** Capxul reads a permission only from Hats (CAP-1101, responsibilities.keepers), so a permission row would copy the chain. The change is still needed: its `spc_` id and state are frozen returns, one waits per member and permission, and a grant's plan counts the seats waiting grants will take. Its proposal is board's own, so settling both in one transaction needs no outbox hop and no second process; it applies only when the execution's indexed facts hold the hat's mint or burn.

### board.one-proposal-table: One table of Board proposals, every one at a nonce, settled only by its process

- **Question:** Old `orgSafeProposals` held raw, rejection and nonce-less scheduled-payment proposals, with each Director's sends as `submissions`, and every read or action settled every waiting row from the chain. What is a target proposal?
- **Chosen:** One row per company Safe transaction at a Safe nonce, carrying its opener's subject; its process settles it from the indexed execution at the nonce; reads only read.
- **Why:** A Safe nonce is used once, so the one execution at it ends every proposal there, and that is a chain event the proposal's own process waits for (A3, P3): no read writes. On v4 a dated Board payment is a proposal carrying one `Budgets.schedule` at a nonce (payments' design), so the nonce-less lane goes and every proposal has a nonce; the frozen kind `scheduled_payment` stays for a proposal that carries a dated payment. The opener's subject is on the row so asking twice answers the same proposal and every opener follows its own. Submissions move to `proposal-approvals`, because approvals now hold each send. A transaction service is not part of the deployment.

### board.people-hat-role-conflict: Granting the People hat to a department's office holder is ROLE_CONFLICT

- **Question:** Budgets refuses a department's or team's office to a wearer of the People hat (budgets.role-conflict-people-hat) and asks board for the reverse. `org member grant`'s frozen page lists no ROLE_CONFLICT, and board sits below budgets, so it cannot read which budgets are departments. Does the grant refuse, and how does it know?
- **Chosen:** Refuse `manage-people` with ROLE_CONFLICT when the member's Safe wears a department's or team's office hat, read from indexing: a budget's hat where the budget is a top budget, or its hat's admin is its parent budget's hat.
- **Why:** P10 rung 3: this is authority, so the stricter version wins until a matter rules. The old Manage people grant refused a department holder (permissions/guards/conflicts.ts, ADR-0040), and on v4 Manage people is this grant, so without the check one person could hold an office and Manage allowances by asking in the other order. ROLE_CONFLICT is already a frozen code with that meaning. Budgets' designed creates place the hats so the chain tells them apart: a top department's hat and an allowance's hat sit under the Board's top hat, a team's under its parent's office hat, and only an allowance is a child budget with its hat under the top hat. Refusing allowance wearers too would be a conflict the old system never had. The refusal is reached from budgets' world org-with-budget-tree.

### board.proposal-approvals: Board records each Director approval it opened, in its own table

- **Question:** A read must say which Directors approved and whose approvals are still on their way. Approvals rows are per signer and indexed by signer; the chain holds only what landed. Where does a proposal's list of approvals come from?
- **Chosen:** A small `proposal-approvals` table: each approval Capxul opened, its Director and its step.
- **Why:** The frozen reads show approvals `sent` and on their way, which the chain does not know yet, and `org proposal wait` waits on exactly those. Approvals must not read its openers or grow an index for one of them; a board row naming each approval id joins approvals' by-id index. The row is written in the transaction that opens the approval, so it never disagrees with it.

### board.proposals-gate: Every Director proposal and approval is behind the `owner-safe-proposals` gate

- **Question:** The old propose, pay, change-owners, grant, revoke and request-approval each checked `owner-safe-proposals`. Keep it, and where?
- **Chosen:** `board.propose` and `board.proposals.approve` refuse with it off and name it on the approval, so a gate turned off later stops the send.
- **Why:** Approve #5 keeps it ('no approval opens, and a gate turned off later stops the send'), and approvals stops a send only for the gate an approval names (approvals.gate). The old code gated every way a Director proposal was made, so checking it once in `board.propose` keeps that for budgets', payments' and agents' proposals too. Manage books also needs the `books` gate, as grant #4 says. The gates are read from access' feature flags through its evaluator.

### board.propose-procedure: `board.propose` takes the calls; board picks the nonce, opens the first approval

- **Question:** What does the one procedure for a Board proposal take from its opener, and what does board decide?
- **Chosen:** The opener gives its subject, its calls, a note and the page summary; board checks the proposer is a Director, picks the next free nonce, packs the calls, computes the Safe transaction hash and opens the proposer's first approval.
- **Why:** The concept that knows the intent builds its calls (dependencies.board-below-proposers); the nonce and the hash are the Board's, and two openers picking nonces would collide. The proposer's own approval is part of the same fact: the budgets, payments and payroll designs already rely on it ('the Board proposal and the caller's first approval'), and on a 1-of-1 Board it is the execution. It reads only tables (indexing's Safe nonce at Ponder's head), so it makes no outside call; a nonce Ponder had not yet seen used ends the proposal as replaced, never as a wrong payment. Board's own grant, revoke and rejection invoke it too, so a proposal is born in one place; a rejection names the proposal whose nonce it takes. The transaction is one procedure from board and one from approvals (sys:tx-width).

### board.reject-dated-payments: A dated Board payment's proposal can be rejected like any other

- **Question:** `org proposal reject` refused a scheduled payment, because it had no nonce to use up. On v4 it has one. Keep the refusal?
- **Chosen:** Reject it like any proposal: its nonce is used up and the schedule is never made.
- **Why:** The refusal existed only because the old scheduled lane had no nonce. Refusing would leave the Directors no way to stop a dated payment they will not approve except waiting forever. The code is unchanged (WRONG_STATE still answers a rejection of a rejection or of a settled proposal); one of its reasons goes.

### board.top-hat-refusals: After the handover, the top hat's commands answer RESERVED_MATTER

- **Question:** The frozen refusal for seat, remove and threshold is ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED for a caller without the Shareholders' top hat; the designed text adds RESERVED_MATTER once the Governor holds it. Which applies when?
- **Chosen:** RESERVED_MATTER when the Governor wears the top hat; ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED for anyone else without it.
- **Why:** The designed text is the v4 surface (P10 rung 1): after the handover the matter belongs to the shareholders, and the founder should be told to put it to a vote rather than that they lack a permission. Elections attached to the Director hat's eligibility are WRONG_STATE, also as designed. The handover itself keeps ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED and WRONG_STATE, as its designed refusals do.

### board.withdraw-on-settle: A settled proposal withdraws its Directors' unsigned approvals

- **Question:** When a proposal's nonce is used, Directors may still have approvals of it open and unsigned. What happens to them?
- **Chosen:** The settling transaction withdraws each through `approvals.withdraw`.
- **Why:** Nobody should be asked to sign for a nonce already used; the approvals designer added the procedure for exactly this case (an ended intent takes its unsigned carrier). A signed approval is left to the chain, which ends it as reverted or displaced.

### board.worlds: The worlds board's refusals and claims are reached from

- **Question:** Which devnet snapshots does board introduce, and what does each hold?
- **Chosen:** org-with-founder-board: a v4 Organization fresh from setup, its founder the one Director (1 of 1) wearing the Shareholders' top hat, a set-up member, a member who has not finished signing up, a signed-in outsider. org-with-two-directors: that member seated, 2 of 2. org-with-waiting-proposals: two proposals waiting at consecutive nonces, one executed, one rejected, one replaced outside Capxul, a waiting rejection, a member wearing the Books hat and a waiting Invoicing grant. org-with-board-change-waiting: a seat waiting for the founder's approval. org-with-lapsed-director: the second Director's hat ruled out while still an owner. org-with-board-handed-over: equity installed and handed to the Governor. org-with-setup-awaiting-install: no deployment yet. org-with-proposals-gate-off: `owner-safe-proposals` off. cli-signed-out (shared with budgets).
- **Why:** Most refusals are reached by who runs the command in a small Board (the founder, the second Director, a member, an outsider); waiting proposals, a waiting change, a lapsed hat, the handover and a gate change what every write answers, so each is its own world. Each is built by running operations from org-with-founder-board, except the lapsed hat, which the founder rules out on chain outside Capxul.

## books (23)

### books.acceptance-rewrites: Telemetry lines become claims about what is reported (P1)

- **Question:** Thirteen frozen books lines name the old telemetry ('posts `books_account_added`', 'schedules nothing', 'delivered once'). Keep or rewrite?
- **Chosen:** Rewrite each as 'is reported once' or 'reports nothing', observed as the machine's event in the local collector; the old line moves to `was`.
- **Why:** P1 rewrites a line that names a mechanism. The rewritten lines still pass against the old system, which posted one event per committed change and none for a refusal. Rewritten: annotate 5; import 2, 3, 4; add 3; rename 4, 5; archive 2, 3; reclassify 4; assets add 4, 5; assets remove 3, 4. Every other line is word for word, including trial balance 4 ('a chart fact posts nothing'), where 'posts' means posting lines, and reclassify 3, whose straddling row on v4 is any row with money facts on both sides of the period.

### books.account-at-posting: A money fact names no account; posting reads the row's annotation

- **Question:** Does a money fact store the account it books to, or is the account resolved when the books are read?
- **Chosen:** The fact records the money; a row posts to its annotation's account now, else its direction's not-decided account by role.
- **Why:** The old reports already posted 'to the account it has now, else the one its fact was booked with, else 4999 or 6999', so the stored account was only a fallback, and a stored account is a copy that disagrees after every recategorization. With facts kept before a chart (books.facts-without-chart) there is no account to store yet. The history of a row's accounts is its account_assigned, recategorized and rows_reclassified facts.

### books.added-token-opening: Openings only for an added token, at the add; none for Capxul's assets

- **Question:** The old books read each Safe's opening balances once after the backfill (CAP-1047). What is left of the opening on the target?
- **Chosen:** Capxul's own assets need none: the v4 company Safe is minted fresh and payments attributes every Transfer of an admitted asset from its first block. An added token, scanned only from the block it was added, gets one `opening_balance` fact for the Safe's balance at that block, read in the same call as its decimals and symbol.
- **Why:** The opening existed because a chart could be imported onto a Safe whose history the books had not seen; with facts kept from the first money that cannot happen for admitted assets. For an added token the frozen text says transfers before the add are not scanned, so without an opening `reconcile` could never match (its line 2 reads added tokens too). One more parallel `balanceOf` at the block already read keeps the add in its chain-read class. The opening credits 3990, which the template keeps.

### books.added-token-scan: The added-token scan: a minute cron, a cursor on the asset, final blocks only

- **Question:** How does books' scan of added tokens (shared concepts.books-token-scan) keep its place and survive reorganizations?
- **Chosen:** A singleton cron each minute; each `added` row keeps its `next_block`; it reads Transfer logs only to the finality depth below the head and books each as outside money keyed by the Transfer; no reversals.
- **Why:** Reading only final blocks makes every fact it writes permanent, so the scan needs no reversal path; the minute it trails costs nothing for tokens Capxul never pays in. The cursor sits on the asset row (one per token per Organization), replacing the old indexing checkpoint and bookCursors. Its facts are what activity shows for an added token, since no Movement exists for it.

### books.annotate-declined-preview: A declined `activity annotate` preview ends CANCELLED

- **Question:** `activity annotate` shows a preview at a terminal and asks before it saves. What does a 'no' end with?
- **Chosen:** CANCELLED (exit 130), listed in the operation's `errors` with CLI_USAGE: the CLI asks before its one rpc.
- **Why:** The shared rule (vocabulary.declined-preview-cancelled) is one answer for every command. A script without `--confirm` stays CLI_USAGE, so acceptance #6 is unchanged, and a 'no' saves nothing, as #5's refused save does.

### books.annotate-known-rows: Annotate checks an Organization's rows against its own facts

- **Question:** `activity annotate` refuses a row the actor does not take part in. The old guard read payments. Books is below payments and cannot. How is the row checked?
- **Chosen:** An Organization row must be one its books hold a money fact for (books keeps every settled side). A person's row is not checked: the annotation lives only in that person's own books and posts nothing. A pending Payment gets its account from `payment send --account`, passed with its money fact.
- **Why:** A registry procedure would be no money fact (rules.procedures), and lifting annotate makes another concept write books' annotations and facts, which the map forbids (concepts.books). The old guard already skipped the check for chain rows it never stored ('its shape and the scope that owns the annotation are all there is to check'). A person annotating a Payment they are not in learns nothing and changes nobody else's books, so no money or authority loosens (P10 rung 3). The refusal text keeps its words; an unsettled Payment's row is refused until it settles.

### books.annotations-one-table: A row's account, suggestion and memo are one annotation row

- **Question:** Old activity kept a row's memo (activityAnnotations) and old books its account (bookAssignments), written together. One table or two?
- **Chosen:** One `book-annotations` row per owner and row, `owner` tenancy, with a machine for waiting or decided.
- **Why:** Both were keyed by the same owner and row and saved by the same mutation (`saveAnnotation` then `classifyRow`); two rows let a save replace one and not the other. The retired free-text fields (`accountingCategory`, `counterpartyLabel`) and their clean-up are not carried (P9). `owner` tenancy because a person's rows and an Organization's share one shape; persons keep no facts (ADR-0042 D6).

### books.births-are-transitions: A row's birth is a transition with no source state

- **Question:** An import, an add or a first annotation inserts a row. How does the insert send its machine's event?
- **Chosen:** The birth is a named transition with `from: []` (import, add, queue, assign), emitting like any other.
- **Why:** The schema accepts an empty `from`, the build's state checks pass, and no phantom state is stored or listed. The event's `from` is null for a birth.

### books.budgets: Every books record takes its class default

- **Question:** Does any books operation, rpc, flow or workflow need a budget other than its class default (P5)?
- **Chosen:** Reads `read`, changes `write`, the two chain reads `chain-read`, the review flow `write`, the two crons `background`.
- **Why:** The reports post from the indexed facts in one query per report; an Organization's facts number in the thousands, well inside a 100 ms server share. The asset add and the reconcile make two dependent chain hops (the head, then parallel reads at it), inside chain-read. An override can come with a measurement.

### books.capxul-assets: Capxul's own assets are configuration, not rows

- **Question:** Where do Capxul's settlement and held assets (USDC, EURC, WBTC...) live for the books?
- **Chosen:** The deployment's admitted-asset list (with each asset's tier and Chainlink pair) is configuration every concept reads; only added tokens are rows.
- **Why:** As in the old code: they change only with a deployment, and payments needs the same list. Only they are priced, so an added token named USDC never takes USDC's price.

### books.dropped: What of old books is not ported

- **Question:** Which old books functions, rows and tables have no target record?
- **Chosen:** keepers.list (no frozen operation: the Books hat's wearers are a chain view); the backfill, observe, open and outside internals; set-price (devnet's); the 9 emit-* actions (P7); assets.target, admit and tokens, and the reports' *At queries (steps of one rpc or workflow, P3); activity's clearRetired (P9); the bookCursors and bookKeepers tables; the door's read functions (bookAssetInTx and the rest), which become plain reads of books' tables.
- **Why:** P2: an rpc with no operation, flow or trigger is not ported; P3: a load, intent, record or emit split becomes steps; P4: a concept above books reads its tables directly, so only the two money-fact procedures cross the door.

### books.export-files-by-cli: The SDK returns the two export files; the CLI writes them

- **Question:** Who writes `chart-of-accounts.csv` and `journal-entries.csv`, and so who refuses CLI_STORAGE?
- **Chosen:** The SDK method returns both files' text and counts; the CLI writes them, never over a file, and removes the chart if the journal fails.
- **Why:** The shared vocabulary says the SDK never returns the CLI's own codes, and the frozen refusal is CLI_STORAGE; MCP and browser hosts want the text, not a file on the host. Writing output is the CLI's printing, not orchestration (A4). The shared landing for books/exports says 'written by the SDK': this decision reads it as 'produced by the SDK'.

### books.facts-without-chart: Money facts are kept from the first money, chart or not; no backfill

- **Question:** The old books kept no money fact for an Organization without a chart, and the import scheduled a backfill that re-read Payments, Deposits and Movements. Books now sits below payments and banking and cannot read them. How does money from before the chart reach the books?
- **Chosen:** `books.record-money-fact` records every Organization side whether or not a chart exists; a money fact names no account, so the chart only maps facts to accounts. The import makes a Safe account for each asset the facts already moved. No backfill, no chart stamps.
- **Why:** Rung 3 of P10: K10 ('a settled Payment always has its book facts') becomes unconditional instead of exempting Organizations without a chart, so the stricter version wins. Backfill-upstream adds two cross-concept workflows that re-run money writes on a books transition, with ordering races against live settlements. Backfill-chain cannot rebuild the row references, counterparties and account choices only payments knows, so its facts would not link to annotations. With `always` the import is one transaction and balance-sheet line 2 ('Money recorded before the chart gets a Safe account per asset when the chart is made') holds literally. Every acceptance line still holds. Two frozen sentences describe the old mechanism and stay as frozen text: import's `changes` ('schedules a backfill') and its note 'An Organization without a chart keeps no money facts'; on the target `facts list` shows money facts before the import. This is a data-model change for Aaron's per-concept review (W1).

### books.guards: Any Seat reads; the Books hat changes; the books gate stays

- **Question:** How does each books rpc authorize its caller on v4?
- **Chosen:** Reads need a current Seat (organizations' org-members); changes need the Seat's Safe to wear the Books hat (org-deployments' `books_hat`, indexing's hat-wearers); every rpc but annotate refuses FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE while the `books` gate is off (access' feature-flags).
- **Why:** Manage books is the Books hat (shared responsibilities.keepers), read from the chain, never a books table. The gate stays because import's line 4 checks 'the books gate off' is refused; annotate never read it (it was activity's). FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE is in each rpc's errors but in no refusal list, since the frozen pages list none.

### books.machines: Four machines, one event each; facts have none

- **Question:** Which stored states are machines, now that every product event comes from a transition (P7)?
- **Chosen:** book-chart (active), book-account (active, archived), book-annotation (waiting, decided) and book-asset (added, removed), each with its `<machine>_transitioned`. Facts are append-only rows with no state.
- **Why:** The frozen lines check that each committed add, rename, archive, import, save and reclassify is reported once; under P7 only a transition reports. The old 8 books events (chart imported, account added, renamed, archived, suggestion accepted, rows reclassified, asset added, removed) and activity's annotation event fold into these four. The chart's backfilled and opened stamps go with the backfill. A reclassify reports each row it moves.

### books.money-key: A money fact is keyed by the chain occurrence that moved the money

- **Question:** What makes a money fact one fact, when payments, banking and books' own scan can each reach the same money, and a reorganization can take it back?
- **Chosen:** `money_key` is the chain occurrence: `payment:<v4 payment id>` (consumed once on chain) or `transfer:<chain>:<tx>:<log>`; with the Organization and a generation it is unique. Same key, same content: no-op. A Payment, deposit or payout side supersedes a standing Movement fact (reversal plus next generation); a Movement side never supersedes theirs.
- **Why:** The old ids came from each writer's own records, so the same Transfer could be booked by a Deposit and as outside money under two ids (M1 in docs/as-is/payments.md). A chain key is the same whoever reaches the money first, so a replay, a second writer or a re-settle reaches the one fact, and a reversal finds what it cancels. The precedence rule makes the outcome independent of order: the specific writer (a settlement, a deposit, a payout's return) wins over a generic outside-money attribution. Both procedures never refuse: a settlement must never fail because of the books, so an account choice that no longer fits leaves the row waiting in review.

### books.one-observer: The old observer folds into payments' Movements; books scans only added tokens

- **Question:** Every minute the old `books.facts.observe` read each charted Safe's Transfers from Ponder and booked what no Capxul Payment explained, beside a second path (`books.facts.outside`, from Movements). P3 sends the observer to a chain trigger. Is it books' workflow?
- **Chosen:** Payments, which owns Movements (indexed Transfers attributed to a Payment, or outside money), invokes `books.record-money-fact` in the transaction that attributes outside money. Books has no observer of admitted assets; its only scan is of added tokens, which Ponder has no source for.
- **Why:** Two observers of the same Transfers with their own rules is how the old system lost an Organization's outside money (M1: a Deposit row hid it from the observer). ADR-0042 D12 wants one observer; on the target map that is payments, whose Movement decides 'Capxul money or outside money' once, from PaymentSent, escrow and Transfer views, and whose Movement id is the row an annotation names. The fact then commits with the Movement (P4). The cursor table (bookCursors), the 150-block lag and the 30-second outside timer go; native ETH is not booked (every v4 money asset is an ERC-20). Pending link: payments must invoke the procedure for outside money and reversals.

### books.prices-sync: One price writer: the Chainlink sync; devnet prices come through its fake

- **Question:** The old prices had two writers (the devnet-only set-price and the five-minute Chainlink sync) and a stand-in series. What stays?
- **Chosen:** Only the five-minute sync writes `book-prices`; on a devnet the Chainlink fake answers the devnet's scripted rates.
- **Why:** Scripted prices are devnet's (concepts.devnet), read by the integration's fake, so books keeps no devnet rpc and one code path writes rates everywhere. The stand-in series hid a missing price on a devnet that staging would show as unpriced. P3 keeps this cron.

### books.reconcile-held: On v4, 'money in agreements' is money held outside the Safe: open escrows

- **Question:** The reconcile shows 'what open agreements hold' and its line 1 says the chain's balance and the money in agreements explain the books. On v4 an agreement reserves its money in Budgets, inside the Safe. What fills the column?
- **Chosen:** `held` is what the Organization's open escrows hold (money that left the Safe and is booked out only when the escrow pays); agreements hold nothing outside the Safe. Line 1 stays word for word.
- **Why:** Subtracting a reservation would count money twice: it is still in the Safe's balance and in the books. A claim escrow is the v4 case the old column covered: money funded out of the Safe before its Payment settles. The frozen claim still holds, since agreements now explain a zero difference. The balance is read live with chain-rpc for every asset, added tokens included, which Ponder does not index.

### books.review-flow: `org books accounts review` is one SDK flow: the queue, then an accept

- **Question:** The old command called `books.accounts.review`, and with `--accept` also `books.accounts.accept`. A4 allows the CLI one SDK method. Which?
- **Chosen:** One flow: read the queue; with `accept`, accept a row only if the queue lists it, else return `notInQueue` for the CLI's CLI_USAGE.
- **Why:** An rpc is one kind (a query or a mutation); a read that sometimes writes would blur both. The frozen surface keeps CLI_USAGE for an `--accept` id not in the queue (P10 rung 1), so the flow reads the queue first and the CLI, not the SDK, turns `notInQueue` into its own code (the SDK never returns CLI codes). The frozen note naming two SDK methods stays as frozen text.

### books.rows-by-id: Accept and reclassify take a row's id; books finds its kind

- **Question:** The old CLI read the review queue to find each `--row` and `--accept` id's kind before calling. Who resolves it now?
- **Chosen:** The rpcs take ids only and find each row's kind from books' own facts and annotations.
- **Why:** Books holds every row it can move, so resolving there removes a client round trip and keeps reclassify a single rpc behind its SDK method (A4).

### books.straddling-row: A row straddles a period when a payout settles in one and returns in the next

- **Question:** Reclassify #3 refuses 'a commitment whose claims fall on both sides of the period'. On v4 each agreement collection is its own Payment with one money fact, so which row can have money facts in two periods, and does payments need to group collections by their agreement?
- **Chosen:** A bank payout's Payment: its settlement is one fact, and the bank's return is a second fact on the same row (`payout_returned`, which banking books referencing the payout's Payment). A payout settled before a month's end and returned after it straddles that month.
- **Why:** The claim is kept word for word (P1), so it needs a real v4 row. Grouping collections would make payments know payroll's agreements, which the map keeps apart (dependencies.payments-payroll), and payments keeps one Payment per collection (payments.chain-born-ids). A payout and its return are the same row's two money facts, the shape the old commitment had. The world is org-with-straddling-payout, built on org-with-chart: a payout settled on the last day of a month and returned by Bridge's fake after a `dev time warp` past it (devnet.straddling-payout-world); banking passes the payout's Payment as the return fact's reference (banking.payout-return-reference).

### books.worlds: The five worlds books' refusals are reached from

- **Question:** Which named devnet snapshots do the books refusals need?
