- **Chosen:** org-without-chart: an Organization whose Safe has settled money in and out, a Director wearing the Books hat, a Seat without it and an outsider, no chart. org-with-chart: the same after the import, with a row waiting in 6999 with a suggestion, an outside deposit nobody annotated, a sub-account and an archived account. org-with-chart-and-added-token: plus an ERC-20 the Safe holds, added. org-with-chart-and-node-down: org-with-chart with chain-rpc's fault switch on. signed-out: no sign-in on the machine.
- **Why:** Each refusal is reached by one command from one of these, as one of its identities, with no other setup. `assets list`'s INVALID_INPUT (an empty id) stays `?`: the CLI's scope refuses an empty `--org` with CLI_USAGE first. Reclassify line 3 needs, in org-with-chart, a row with money on both sides of a month: a bank payout's Payment settled before the month's end and returned after it (books.straddling-row).

## budgets (27)

### budgets.acceptance-events: Lines that name a scheduled event now claim the change is recorded once

- **Question:** Nine lines say a command 'schedules one `payroll_department_change_proposed`' or 'schedules nothing'. What do they claim?
- **Chosen:** 'The change is recorded once as proposed' (one `budget_change_transitioned` in the collector) and 'proposes nothing' (an empty world diff).
- **Why:** P7 replaces the old catalog event with each machine's one event, and P1 makes telemetry and 'schedules' mechanism words; the trace and world observers see the outcome. Rewritten: department budget set #2, ceiling set #5, close #1, reopen #1, holder add #2, holder remove #2, list #6, ceiling freeze #5, ceiling unfreeze #5, allowance create #4 and allowance revoke #4; each keeps its old line first in `was`.

### budgets.acceptance-mechanisms: Lines that name the SDK, a record or client work claim what is observed

- **Question:** 'The SDK accepts only one Budgets.close', 'refused when it records', 'checked before any client work', 'ends its command': what do they claim?
- **Chosen:** Rewrite each as what the chain's calldata, the CLI's refusal or the trace shows.
- **Why:** P1: a component, a record and a process step are mechanisms a black box cannot see. Rewritten: close #4 (refused naming the waiting proposal) and #6, reopen #3 (the proposal carries exactly one call, in its calldata), allowance get #3 (no chain read before the refusal) and allowance change #5 (the change reads replaced, the amount unchanged).

### budgets.acceptance-pre-v4: Lines that describe a pre-v4 path are rewritten as their v4 claim

- **Question:** Some lines name pre-v4 calls or shapes (setBudget, assignRoles, the department role, a yearly conversion, two tokens per department, a per-payment cap, a revision, a Party binding, Manage Budgets as an allowance). What do they claim?
- **Chosen:** Rewrite each as the v4 outcome of the same test, the old line first in `was`; cut a pre-v4 clause.
- **Why:** P9 drops the pre-v4 path and P10 makes the designed text the spec, but the test behind each line still has a v4 form worth checking. Rewritten: department create #1 (payout install, office hat), #5 (kept per its period), budget set #2 (setAmount), #4 (a token not its own), holder add #2 and remove #2 (only the hat call), list #4 (one budget per department), allowance list #1 (pre-v4 clause cut, as P9 says), #2 (no revision), #3 (a Seat, not a Party binding), #5 (no per-payment cap), and allowance assign #3 (the Manage Budgets clause cut). Each must still pass on the old system's v4 path; budget set #4, list #4 and allowance list #3 need that run to confirm.

### budgets.acceptance-retired: Five lines with no v4 form are retired as null

- **Question:** Which lines cannot be stated on v4 at all?
- **Chosen:** Department close #5 (P9's pre-v4 line), allowance create #3 (Manage Budgets as an allowance), allowance replace #2-#4 (a Roles module's state carried across).
- **Why:** Close #5 is one of P9's two pre-v4-only lines. Manage Budgets is the People hat on v4 and an allowance cannot be it, so create #3's test cannot be built. Replace has no module to replace: carrying invitations, queuing a manager's event and showing frozen rows before an approval describe a command v4 removed. The numbers stay.

### budgets.ceilings: Every payout ceiling write is the Installer's batch in a Board proposal

- **Question:** How is a token's payout ceiling set, frozen, unfrozen and resized with the budgets it backs?
- **Chosen:** The Board proposes the calls `Installer.buildSetCeiling` (or, for a first budget in a token, `buildAddToken`) returns from the allowance as it is now; a top budget's create or amount moves it in the same proposal.
- **Why:** The Installer is v4's one encoder of the payout role's allowance, so Capxul cannot get the key or the period wrong. The ceiling is a backstop sized from the top budgets, so their changes move it in the same proposal (frozen budget set #2 and #6). A freeze keeps the allowance it replaced in its change, because an unfreeze must restore exactly that, less all of what the freeze left; a freeze made outside Capxul has no such record and cannot be undone here.

### budgets.chain-is-the-record: Amounts, spends, wearers and ceilings are read from the chain, never stored

- **Question:** The old department row kept budgets per token, holders and `closed`. Which budget facts does the target copy?
- **Chosen:** None: the tree and wearers from indexing's views, the numbers from `Budgets.budgetOf`, `childrenOf` and `leftOf` at the latest block, the ceiling from `Roles.allowances`.
- **Why:** The frozen claims say it: 'Budget and commitment come from budgetOf, so a missed or reordered event changes nothing' and 'Each read goes to the chain again'. A copy would drift whenever the Board acts at the Safe directly, and a budget's period rolls on the calendar, which only `leftOf` knows. Live reads cost a chain-read budget (P5), which fits: they run concurrently, one `budgetOf` and one `leftOf` per budget shown, so a read does not grow with the Organization's other budgets. Closed and open are therefore not states of the budget machine.

### budgets.dropped-old-functions: Old department and allowance plumbing is not ported

- **Question:** Which old rpcs and workflows of departments and allowances have no target record?
- **Chosen:** load, read, record, load-command, record-command, load-list, the replacement preview and prepare, keep-budget-ids and settle (now the change's process), emit-change-proposed (the runtime's outbox, P7), notice-facts and notify (notices'), and the pre-v4 authorities (payments' read).
- **Why:** They were Convex's internal queries and mutations an action ran in the same call (P2: no unreached rpc), telemetry plumbing (P7) or settlement on read (budgets.one-process-per-change). Their behaviour lives in the one transaction of each target rpc and in the change's process.

### budgets.dropped-refusals: Frozen refusals that only had meaning before v4 are not produced

- **Question:** Which frozen refusal reasons does the target drop?
- **Chosen:** `pre_v4` (P9 retires it), an invitation's binding that owns a person's rights or has no Account yet, a stale preview or revision, no verified Manage allowances or a retired Roles module, and FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE for departments.
- **Why:** Each describes the pre-v4 Roles permissions (P9): on v4 an invitation's allowance offer is board's proposal of a hat mint, a wearer with no Seat reads by its Safe, a change is planned from the chain with one waiting per budget so no preview can go stale, and there is no Roles module of allowances. The codes stay (WRONG_STATE, INVALID_INPUT) for the v4 reasons each operation's refusals list.

### budgets.hat-planning: A new office hat's id is planned from Hats.viewHat at the chain head

- **Question:** A create's batch must name the new hat before it exists. Where does its id come from?
- **Chosen:** From `Hats.viewHat(admin)`: its `lastHatId` at the latest block names the next child (lastHatId + 1 at the admin's next level), read through chain-rpc.
- **Why:** Assembly: shared/external.ts now books `viewHat` (contracts.hats-view-hat), so the live read replaces the index, as the first version of this decision said it should. A lagging index can no longer name a hat that already exists. Indexing's other reads (wearers, a full hat's supply, budgets) still wait for Ponder at the head. Two creates under one admin would still plan the same id before either executes, so the one-create-per-admin WRONG_STATE stays. A hat made outside Capxul under the same admin between proposal and execution shifts the id; the batch is atomic, and the change's process applies it only when BudgetCreated names the planned hat.

### budgets.ids-and-keys: A budget is named by key, Capxul id or chain id; its key frees when abandoned

- **Question:** How do commands name a budget, and when is a key taken?
- **Chosen:** By its key (from its name), its `dep_` or `allowance_` id, or its chain id; the key is held while proposed or active (`key_claim`, unique).
- **Why:** The frozen commands take a department's key or `dep_` id and an allowance's id or key (`org allowance change office-costs`); payroll's offers name a department the same way. A budget made outside Capxul has only its chain id. A unique index on a column that is null once abandoned frees the key of a create that never ran, which the frozen list needs ('reads leave it out and its slug is free again'), without a partial index.

### budgets.invitation-grants-return: Budgets proposes taking an ended invitation's hats back, once

- **Question:** An invitation declined, cancelled or expired after its Board batch minted hats waits in `revoking` until the hats are gone. Who proposes their return, and who proposes it when no person is acting?
- **Chosen:** A budgets workflow on organizations' `begin_cleanup` opens one Board proposal burning every hat the grantee's Safe still wears; the proposer is the inviter while a Director, else the Director with the oldest current Seat.
- **Why:** The review gave the return to budgets with the grants (operations.member-invite-in-budgets), and organizations' process waits for it. `board.propose` needs a proposing Director, and the end of an offer is often nobody's act (an expiry) or the invitee's (a decline); the inviter granted the hats, so their approval opens first, and the oldest Director is a deterministic fallback. The hats are burned with `setHatWearerStatus(…, false, true)`, as `org allowance revoke` does, so the Safe can be granted again later. Proposing again after a rejection would loop against the Directors' own answer; they keep the manual commands, and the invitation ends when the chain shows no hat worn.

### budgets.invitation-allowance-offers: An invitation offers officer hats, existing allowances or one new allowance

- **Question:** Organizations' invitation Offer has `officer` (a hat), `allowance` (a budget id) and `new-allowance` (name, asset, limit). Which can the Board batch of an invitation's grants carry, and what does a new allowance's `limit` mean on v4?
- **Chosen:** All three, planned by budgets: an officer hat's or existing allowance's `Hats.mintHat`, and a new allowance's `Hats.createHat`, `Budgets.createChildBudget(parent, limit, hat)` and `Hats.mintHat`, with its budgets row; `limit` is its amount per period, under the one open top budget in that asset and in that budget's period.
- **Why:** Revised at assembly. This record first chose existing-only, because board owned the flow and could not plan an allowance; the review moved `org member invite` to budgets (operations.member-invite-in-budgets), and P10 rung 1 keeps the frozen offer of 'a new spending allowance'. The designed call names exactly `createHat`, `createChildBudget` and `mintHat`, and `createChildBudget` takes one number, so the designed `--limit <amount>` is the child budget's amount; v4 has no per-payment cap. The designed synopsis has no period, and a child takes its parent's period, so the parent is the one open top budget in `--asset` (none or several is INVALID_INPUT, as `org allowance create` refuses without `--parent`). Adding a flag would change the frozen surface. The pre-v4 `--unlimited`, `--recipient-account`, `--any-recipient` and `--actions` are not in the designed synopsis.

### budgets.member-invite-acceptance: `org member invite` #1 is rewritten from rows to what a reader sees

- **Question:** Line 1 says it 'writes one authorizing invitation and one configure command'. A black box sees neither row. What does it check?
- **Chosen:** The invitation reads `authorizing` with one Board proposal of all its grants (its commandId), and the invitee is admitted with no welcome email.
- **Why:** P1: a claim that names a row is rewritten as an outcome an observer can check. The configure command is the v4 Board proposal, which `org proposal get` reads; admission is `waitlist status` as the invitee; the welcome email's absence is `dev mail list`. The old system passes it: one authorizing invitation with one configure command, the invitee admitted. Lines 2 to 5 are kept word for word.

### budgets.member-invite-flow: `org member invite`: plan, invite, then one proposal bound to the invitation

- **Question:** The invitation is organizations' row and the grants are one Board proposal. In what steps does budgets' flow write them, and how is a refusal kept from leaving a half-made invitation?
- **Chosen:** A read first (`budgets.invitations.plan`, also `--preview`) refuses what it can before anything is written; then organizations' invite rpc; then `budgets.invitations.propose`, one transaction that births a new allowance's row and create change, opens the proposal (`board.propose`) and binds it (`organizations.bind-invitation-grants`).
- **Why:** An invitation is not a money fact on its own, so a new organizations procedure to birth one would go past P4; the money fact is the grants and the Safe transaction carrying them, which is what the bind procedure already records. The plan checks the People and Director hats and every grant first, so the frozen 'needs you to be a Director' is refused with nothing written; if the propose step still fails (the Board's gate turned off, the parent's room spent), the invitation stays `authorizing` with no binding, a rerun with the same request key proposes it, and `org invite cancel` cancels it at once. The propose transaction invokes two procedures of two concepts (board, organizations), within sys:tx-width; board.propose opens the approval inside it. The returns add `grants` and `commandId` (the Board proposal, the old configure command) to organizations' invitation view, as the frozen returns list them. Its refusals are reached from org-with-member (Sam has no People hat), org-with-budget-tree (Pat wears it but is no Director), org-with-pending-invitation, org-with-granted-invitation and cli-signed-out.

### budgets.no-departments-gate: No departments feature gate

- **Question:** Old close and reopen refused FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE when departments were not on for the Organization. Keep the gate?
- **Chosen:** Drop it: budgets are v4's money authority, not an optional feature.
- **Why:** On v4 every install wires Budgets, payments' budget spends and payroll's agreements pay from budgets, and an allowance is a budget; a gate that hid budgets alone would leave those unmanageable. The designed refusals of close and reopen do not name it. Payroll keeps its own `payment-agreements` gate on agreements.

### budgets.no-door: Budgets has no procedures; payments' spend check is a read

- **Question:** Old permissions answered `authorize` and `budget-left` for payments. What does the target's door hold?
- **Chosen:** Nothing: payments reads `budgets` (the budget by key or id, its chain id, token, period and status) and decides in its own rpc with `Budgets.authorised(id, payerSafe)`, `Budgets.leftOf(id)` and the token's ceiling, refusing ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED, PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED or WRONG_STATE before it births anything.
- **Why:** A procedure is for a write that is one money fact with the invoker's (rules.procedures), and makes no outside call; the check writes nothing and needs the chain's answer now. An rpc a payments flow calls would be checked apart from the prepare it guards, so the answer could be stale by the time payments writes. Payments depends on budgets, so it may read the table, and the chain is the authority anyway: the bundler's simulation refuses an unauthorised or over-budget spend before anyone signs (OverBudget, NotAuthorised). No other concept writes budgets' tables.

### budgets.notices-need-budgets: The allowance email needs notices to depend on budgets

- **Question:** 'Once a v4 assign executes, the member is emailed the allowance at its amount now.' Notices sends every email, but its dependsOn did not list budgets. Who sends it?
- **Chosen:** Add budgets to notices' dependsOn: a notice workflow triggered by budget-change.apply reads the change and the allowance, and the chain's amount.
- **Why:** Only budgets knows a proposal carried an allowance assign, to whom and on which budget; board's proposal does not. Budgets sits below notices, so the edge makes no cycle (dependencies.notices-top). Settled at assembly: the architecture review added the edge (dependencies.notices-budgets) and notices follows budget-change.apply (books/target/notices/budgets.ts). Budgets books nothing that sends mail.

### budgets.one-process-per-change: A budget change is a long-lived object with one durable process

- **Question:** Old payroll settled every waiting change on the next department action, in nonce order; old allowances settled on read. Who settles a change in the target?
- **Chosen:** One process per change, born with it, resumed by its carrier's proposal_transitioned or approval_transitioned, applying it only on the transaction's indexed effect.
- **Why:** A change waits on people and the chain for days, which P3's test makes a workflow and A3 one process per object (rules.durable-processes lets a designer add objects; this is budgets' analogue of an agent change). Reactors would need one workflow per board and approvals transition and would scatter one object's settlement. Settle-on-read made a read write and left a change unsettled until someone looked. Applying only on the indexed effect (BudgetCreated with the planned hat, the amount set, the hat minted) means a proposal that ran but did nothing never reads applied.

### budgets.one-waiting-change: One waiting change per budget, per payout ceiling and per admin hat

- **Question:** Which changes may wait at the same time?
- **Chosen:** At most one proposed change per budget, one per token's ceiling, and one hat-creating change per admin hat; a second is WRONG_STATE naming the first.
- **Why:** Each change is planned from the chain as it is now: two amounts would undo each other, two assigns would both plan the hat's supply plus one, two creates under one admin hat would plan the same hat id, and two ceiling writes would each restore what the other changed. The frozen refusals already name the ceiling and hat cases; one per budget is stricter than the old department rule but the simplest that is always safe, and the refusal names the proposal to approve or reject first.

### budgets.one-write-per-chain-call: Departments and allowances share the amount, holder and close writes

- **Question:** `budget set` and `allowance change`, `holder add` and `allowance assign`, `holder remove` and `allowance revoke`: one rpc each pair, or two?
- **Chosen:** One rpc per chain write (set-amount, add-holder, remove-holder, close, reopen), called by each pair's own flow.
- **Why:** Each pair is the same contract call on the same kind of object under the same authority rule (concepts.budgets), and the differences (ROLE_CONFLICT for a department's office, the Board for an allowance's hat) follow from the budget's kind and its hat's admin, which the rpc reads. Separate rpcs would duplicate every check. Each command keeps its own SDK method and flow (A4), and `org department close` closes an allowance's budget too, as the designed revoke says.

### budgets.replace-removed: `org allowance replace` is refused FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE with no rpc

- **Question:** v4 removed the Roles module of allowances. What implements the frozen command, and which of its page's refusals (CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE, NOT_AUTHENTICATED, ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED, INVALID_INPUT, WRONG_STATE) can still happen?
- **Chosen:** Its SDK method refuses at once with FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE, for every caller on every environment; no rpc exists, and none of the page's five refusals is reached.
- **Why:** The designed text says it is refused before anything is prepared, always, and that nobody may run it (CAP-1100, ruled A); P10 rung 1 makes those designed refusal codes the surface. An rpc whose only behaviour is a refusal is a server endpoint for nothing (P2). So the page's five refusals are dropped, because none can happen. The method makes no call, and the CLI resolves no environment's deployment and no sign-in for it, so CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE (beta or production chosen) and NOT_AUTHENTICATED (no sign-in, or a session that changed before a signature that never comes) are never reached. ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED (no verified Manage allowances assignment), INVALID_INPUT (an open allowance command, a stale preview's snapshot, a request key of another command) and WRONG_STATE (an abandoned or prepared command, a Roles module not admitted) each check pre-v4 Roles module state that P9 drops, and the command, which takes no flags, reads none of it. Nothing is loosened (P10 rung 3): no caller gets anything. Its acceptance keeps line 1 as the v4 refusal and retires 2-4, which describe carrying a Roles module's state across (budgets.acceptance-retired).

### budgets.returns-designed: Returns are the designed v4 shapes; the pre-v4 command and permission fields go

- **Question:** The frozen returns carry `permissionId`, `command.expectedRevision`, `roleKey`, `currentPolicy`. What do the commands print?
- **Chosen:** The designed shapes (an allowance's budget and hat, a department's key, amount and parent's room) plus the frozen fields that still mean something: status, approvalId, requestKey, proposal.
- **Why:** P10. A revision, a Roles key, an assignment id and a per-payment cap have no v4 meaning (P9); printing them null would mislead scripts. The change id replaces the old command id, read with the same request key.

### budgets.role-conflict-people-hat: ROLE_CONFLICT on v4: a department's office and the People hat

- **Question:** Old ROLE_CONFLICT kept a Manage allowances holder from holding a department. What is it on v4, and can the frozen ROLE_CONFLICT of `org allowance create` and `org allowance assign` (Manage allowances and a department, one person) happen?
- **Chosen:** A department's or team's office cannot go to a member who wears the People hat (Manage allowances on v4); an allowance's hat can.
- **Why:** The frozen department claims keep the separation of duties ('A member who holds Manage allowances cannot hold a department'), and on v4 Manage people is the People hat (org.allowance.create designed, CAP-1101). Allowance wearers were never in the old conflict: the old guard returned for every grant but Manage people (permissions/guards/conflicts.ts:22), and the old v4 allowance path never raised it. `org allowance create` drops its frozen ROLE_CONFLICT, which cannot happen on v4: its condition is a new allowance that is Manage allowances, and none can exist. Its policy is a spending budget (one that allows anything but paying is INVALID_INPUT before any holder is read), and `--assign-to` mints only the new allowance's hat, which a People hat wearer may wear; its designed block does not list it (P10 rung 1). `org allowance assign` keeps ROLE_CONFLICT: it names a budget as every command does, and shares `budgets.offices.add-holder` with `org department holder add` (budgets.one-write-per-chain-call), which reads the budget's kind, so a department's or team's office named to a People hat wearer is refused it (from org-with-budget-tree: `engineering` to Pat). The People hat itself is no budget, so naming it is INVALID_INPUT. No guard is loosened (P10 rung 3): the People hat is granted by board's `org member grant`, which refuses a department's office holder ROLE_CONFLICT (board.people-hat-role-conflict), reading the office hats from indexing's Budgets view. The allowance lines that named Manage Budgets as an allowance are cut or retired (budgets.acceptance-pre-v4, budgets.acceptance-retired).

### budgets.sdk-methods: SDK methods follow the designed names; reads are one rpc each

- **Question:** Which SDK method does each command call?
- **Chosen:** The designed methods: `departments.ceiling.set|freeze|unfreeze` and `departments.tree`; the frozen names elsewhere; each read implemented by its one rpc.
- **Why:** P10 makes the designed text the specification, and only the CLI and the operations are frozen, not the SDK. A read is one rpc call, so A4 implements its operation by the rpc and it needs no flow.

### budgets.signer-rule: The office above a budget signs its changes; the Board signs tops and ceilings

- **Question:** The frozen who-lines say 'a Director'; the designed text says the office above the budget. Who signs what?
- **Chosen:** Budgets' own rule: a top budget, a payout ceiling and a hat under the Board's top hat are the Board's; a team or allowance under a parent is signed by whoever `Budgets.authorised` (or the hat's admin) says acts for the parent, else by the Board when the caller is a Director.
- **Why:** P10: the designed v4 text is the specification, and it says 'Nobody raises their own budget: the office above it does', with the parent office holder signing a team's create, amount, close and holders from their own Safe. Asking the chain (`Budgets.authorised`, `Hats.isWearerOfHat`) rather than a Capxul row keeps authority where v4 puts it. The Board can always act, because the company Safe wears the top hat. Allowances' hats sit under the Board's top hat (the designed create), so their wearers change only by Board proposal, while their amount follows the parent's office.

### budgets.two-tables: Two tables: budgets and budget-changes, for departments and allowances alike

- **Question:** Old permissions kept v4 allowances and their commands in two tables, old payroll kept departments and their changes in two more, and pre-v4 permissions six more. What does the target store?
- **Chosen:** One `budgets` table (a department, team or allowance: what the chain cannot name) and one `budget-changes` table (every change Capxul proposed).
- **Why:** On v4 an allowance and a department are one contract's budgets under one authority rule (concepts.budgets), so their rows differ only in kind and in which hat their office hat sits under. Keeping them apart would duplicate the key namespace, the request keys and every conflict check (P9: consolidate duplicate stores). Nothing at all is not possible: a budget's name and key, its Capxul id, its planned hat before the chain has its id, the request key, the change that blocks another and the ceiling a freeze replaced exist nowhere on chain. The pre-v4 permission, assignment, command, receipt and granter tables are dropped with the Roles commands they served.

### budgets.worlds: The worlds budgets' refusals and claims are reached from

- **Question:** Which devnet snapshots does budgets introduce, and what does each hold?
- **Chosen:** org-with-budget-tree: a v4 Organization, Directors dana@ and eli@ (2 of 2), the People hat on pat@, members mia@, sam@, lead@ and plat@ with claimed Safes, outsider olga@ signed in; USDC top budget `company` 1,200,000 a year (Board) with `engineering` 800,000 (lead@), its teams `platform` 300,000 (plat@) and `hiring` 100,000 closed, and `operations` 300,000 whose team chain `ops-1`...`ops-7` is eight deep; top budget `monthly-spend` 10,000 a month with allowance `office-costs` 2,000 a month worn by mia@, 600 spent; USDC ceiling 1,200,000 a year; EURC admitted with no budget; DAI not admitted. org-with-waiting-budget-changes: that tree plus waiting Board proposals creating `design`, freezing USDC, closing `operations` and changing `office-costs`. org-with-frozen-ceiling: USDC frozen by a Capxul freeze leaving 10,000. org-with-ceiling-frozen-outside-capxul: USDC frozen by a Safe transaction Capxul did not propose. org-with-budget-tree-chain-unreachable: the tree with the devnet node's fault switch on. cli-signed-out: no sign-in. cli-on-beta-environment: the CLI on beta, not provisioned.
- **Why:** One rich tree reaches most refusals by who runs the command and on which budget, which keeps eval worlds few and restores fast; waiting proposals and frozen ceilings change what every write refuses, so they are their own worlds. Claims that need a Board of one Director (allowance change #3) run in org-with-budget-tree after `org board threshold 1`.

## contacts (18)

### contacts.acceptance-rewrites: One claim rewritten: contact list #1

- **Question:** Which of contacts' 19 frozen acceptance lines name a mechanism a black box cannot see?
- **Chosen:** Only `contact list` #1, 'A book read touches no full table scan.', rewritten as: the book lists only its own entries, and its read time does not grow with what lies outside it.
- **Why:** A table scan is storage: no observer sees one. What its test proved was that seeding many unrelated people does not change the answer, and what a scan would cost is time; both are observable (the world and the trace), and the old system passes both, since it read by index. The other 18 are outcomes: 'party-keyed' and 'derived' name what the output shows (a PartyId, a relationship that goes with the Seat), and 'the wire carries no custody evidence' is checked on the CLI's output.

### contacts.budgets: Class budgets, and one override for the guided commands

- **Question:** Which budget does each contacts record take?
- **Chosen:** Reads `read`, writes `write`; the guided get, label, hide and unhide (flow and operation) allow a second call in sequence: read 800/1,600 ms, write 900/1,900 ms, excluding `wait.person`.
- **Why:** No contacts rpc calls the chain, the bundler or a vendor, so `read` and `write` fit (P5). At a terminal the guided commands make a second call before their one read or write (the picker's list, the preview's read), which the class default does not allow for. The person's picking and answering are `wait.person` spans and never system time. A script's run of the same flow makes one call and stays well inside the override.

### contacts.decline-is-cancelled: Declining a terminal preview is CANCELLED

- **Question:** What does a contact command end with when the person answers no to its preview?
- **Chosen:** CANCELLED (exit 130): the person stopped it and nothing was written.
- **Why:** The frozen book does not say (P10 rung 5: the old code is evidence, not specification). A decline is a person's choice, not a usage mistake, and the shared vocabulary's CANCELLED says exactly what happened: the command stopped before it finished and nothing was sent, as a declined approval already ends (vocabulary.drop-signer-rejected). Scripts pass `--confirm` and never see the question, so no script's exit code changes. Other concepts' terminal previews should end the same way.

### contacts.get-bank-accounts: contact get's bank accounts are banking's: the operation belongs with banking

- **Question:** `contact get` returns the bank accounts saved for the contact, which banking owns, and contacts sits below banking. How does the operation get them?
- **Chosen:** Move `contact.get` to banking in OPERATION_OWNERS, as `contact.bank.add` already is: banking's flow calls `contacts.book.get` (banking depends on contacts) and then its own masked account list for that Party.
- **Why:** Only a concept above both can compose the entry and the accounts, and banking is the one above contacts that owns the accounts; the frozen command already lives beside `contact bank add` there in spirit (it prints `bank pay`). The other options break the concept map, the build or the frozen surface. The architecture review made the move (shared operations.contact-get-in-banking), so contacts no longer books the operation or its flow (contacts.get-moved); `contacts.book.list` and `contacts.book.get` stay for banking's flow to step through.

### contacts.get-moved: `contact get` leaves contacts: banking books it over contacts' rpcs

- **Question:** OPERATION_OWNERS now gives `contact.get` to banking (shared operations.contact-get-in-banking). Contacts still booked the operation and its flow `client.account.address-book.get`. What stays in contacts?
- **Chosen:** Remove the operation and its flow; keep `contacts.book.list` (the picker) and `contacts.book.get` (the entry, or null) as the rpcs banking's flow steps through before its own masked accounts.
- **Why:** A4 gives the operation one SDK flow, and banking's flow is that flow: it alone can add `bankAccounts`. A second contacts flow for the same SDK method would be a duplicate record or a second answer to one call, and keeping the operation would book it twice. Both rpcs stay reached: label, hide and unhide step through them for their picker and preview. The removed acceptance observations (null outside the book, exit 0; the owner reads their entry back and `--org` without a Seat is ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED; a get given a PartyId is one `contacts.book.get` call with no list or `wait.person`) and the guided-get budget (p95 1,600 ms) pass to banking's record.

### contacts.guided-flows: get, label, hide and unhide are Core SDK flows; add and list are one rpc

- **Question:** Which contact commands are one rpc and which compose several calls?
- **Chosen:** The commands that pick an entry and show it before acting at a terminal are flows (list, prompt, get, prompt, write); add and list are one rpc each.
- **Why:** A4: the CLI calls exactly one SDK method. At a terminal, label, hide and unhide read the entry for their preview before the one write, and get, label, hide and unhide may pick from the book: that composition belongs in the SDK, with its questions as prompt steps the host answers (a terminal, or none for a script). Given a PartyId and `--confirm`, each flow is one call, so 'an explicit get never lists or prompts'. Add's preview shows only what was typed and needs no read, so the CLI shows it before calling one rpc; list pages on the server. The old CLI made two or three SDK calls per guided command.

### contacts.members-derived: An Organization's members are read from its Seats when the book is read

- **Question:** How does an Organization's book show its members?
- **Chosen:** Each read joins the Organization's active Seats (organizations' `org-members`) to its rows, by PartyId.
- **Why:** The Seat is the one record of membership and contacts depends on organizations, so reading it keeps one source and no lag: when a Seat ends, the member relationship is gone on the next read, which is what `contact list` #2 claims. A projection would be a second copy that can drift and a workflow whose only job is copying (P3). The read stays on indexes: the book's own index and the `org_id` range of the Seats' `by_org_party`. A row is written for a member only when the book curates it (a label or hide), and it keeps that curation if the member leaves and returns; an entry is in the book while it is `manual` or `member`. The member's label falls back to the Party's name, never the invitation's mailbox, which the old book showed.

### contacts.members-write: Any member may write an Organization's book

- **Question:** Who may add, label, hide and unhide in an Organization's book?
- **Chosen:** Any active Seat, as the frozen `who` says ('a member of the Organization for its book').
- **Why:** The frozen `who` of every contact command says a member, and the only Organization refusal is for one 'you are outside'. P10 rung 3 (the stricter wins for money and authority) does not apply: an entry moves no money and grants nothing, the label never resolves a payee (parties resolves; every payment shows its PartyId and needs its own budget authority and approval), and the book cannot hide a member from itself. The stricter check is also not buildable here: whether a Safe wears the People hat is an indexing fact, and contacts depends only on access, parties and organizations. If a ruling wants the People hat, the map must let contacts read hat wearers.

### contacts.no-destinations: Contacts holds no receiving destinations

- **Question:** Where do a contact's wallet and bank destinations live?
- **Chosen:** A contact's wallet is its Party's Safe (parties); its bank accounts are banking's. Old parties' `counterpartyDestinations` and `parties.destinations.*` are not ported into contacts.
- **Why:** The concept map gives old parties' destinations and resolution to parties and a contact's bank accounts to banking. On v4 money to a Party lands in its one Safe (or the outside address itself), so a per-book wallet list would be a second, conflicting answer to where money goes. No frozen operation reaches the old destination rows (P2), and the old Solana clean-up and its report were one-off plumbing.

### contacts.no-door-no-process: Contacts has no procedures and no workflows

- **Question:** Does contacts need a door for other concepts or a durable process?
- **Chosen:** No procedure and no workflow: concepts above read `contacts` and call its rpcs from their flows; every change is inside one rpc.
- **Why:** A procedure needs a money fact (P4, rules.procedures); an entry is not one: if a saved bank account's contact entry lagged or was missing, no money would move or be owned unaccounted for, since the account belongs to the Party, not the entry. So banking adds a contact through `contacts.book.add` (by the Party's id) from its own flow, and reads the table to find one. Short-lived rows have no process (rules.durable-processes), and membership is read, not copied, so nothing reacts to anything. Old parties' `recordContactEdges`, `ensureEmployedEdge` and the `emitRelationshipChanged` telemetry action are not ported (P2, P4, P7).

### contacts.not-in-book-code: A Party outside the book is INVALID_INPUT at a terminal too

- **Question:** label, hide and unhide of a Party not in the book: which code, in a script and at a terminal?
- **Chosen:** INVALID_INPUT in both modes, from the rpc or from the flow's preview read.
- **Why:** The frozen refusals of all three say INVALID_INPUT when 'the party is not in the book', and `contact unhide` #1 says they refuse it, with no mode. Refusal codes are surface, so the book wins (P10 rung 1); the old CLI's terminal-only CLI_USAGE was a reviewer's finding, not a design. CLI_USAGE stays for what the CLI checks itself: a malformed PartyId, a missing ID or `--confirm` in a script, an empty `--label`.

### contacts.one-book-table: One contacts table per owner, keyed by the counterparty's Party

- **Question:** How is the address book stored, now that it leaves old parties' party edges?
- **Chosen:** One `contacts` table with tenancy `owner` (`owner_kind` person or organization, `owner_id` their tenant id), one row per owner and counterparty PartyId.
- **Why:** Every frozen contact command runs the same way for both books, so one table and one set of rpcs serve them, with the `owner` tenancy P8 gives a row a person or an Organization can own. Keying by the tenant ids, not by the owner's Party, lets the generated row-level security match the session's tenant directly. The key (owner, counterparty PartyId) is what 'every locator lands on one party-keyed entry' needs. The old edge table also held payroll's `employed` engagements and the provenance other concepts wrote; both go (contacts.relationships), so a plain table of entries is all that is left. `owner_id` leads the key and the index with `owner_kind` second, so the two kinds' ids never need to differ.

### contacts.ref-and-label: An entry's ref is its party locator; its label the owner's words or a name

- **Question:** What does an entry show as `ref` and `label`, and what are a label's rules?
- **Chosen:** `ref` is always `{kind: party, partyId}`. `label` is the owner's label, or the mailbox they typed when adding by email with none, else the Party's display name, handle, then PartyId. A given label is trimmed and must be 1 to 120 characters.
- **Why:** A party ref is what the money path takes and carries no custody detail; a typed address ref would print a 0x address (`contact add` #1 forbids custody evidence on the wire, which the old test checks as no 0x address), and a typed email ref would show a mailbox for every locator after a second add by another one. The old code returned the party ref too. The label never falls back to a mailbox or an address: a PartyId is public (any address resolves to one), so a mailbox fallback would turn an address into an email in two calls (`contact add` #3). An explicit empty label on add is INVALID_INPUT, as the frozen refusal says ('the label is empty'); the old code read it as no label. That is surface, so the book wins (P10 rung 1).

### contacts.relationships: Relationships are `manual` and `member`; last activity is the last change

- **Question:** The old book merged how you know a counterparty from edges other concepts wrote: paid, paidBy, invoiced, invoicedBy, member, manual and employed. Which can the target keep?
- **Chosen:** Keep `manual` (added by hand, contacts' own) and `member` (an active Seat, read from organizations, which contacts depends on). Drop the rest; `lastActivityAt` is the entry's last change.
- **Why:** The concept map puts contacts below payments, payroll and requests, so contacts may neither read them nor react to their transitions (the reacting concept depends on the one that changed), and making it depend on requests would be a cycle (requests depends on banking, which depends on contacts). They cannot write the book either: none of them depends on contacts, and a relationship is a projection that may lag, never a money fact, so rules.procedures forbids a procedure. No frozen acceptance line needs the dropped values: the frozen text names a `relationship` field and says 'how you know them', and `manual` and `member` keep that meaning, so the shape is kept and its values narrowed (a reader of the old values never sees a new one). Where someone was paid or invoiced is activity's feed. Last activity was the last payment or invoice with them; it becomes the entry's own last change, or its member's Seat's.

### contacts.resolve-in-add: contact add resolves its Party in its own transaction, through parties' door

- **Question:** Adding a mailbox or an address nobody has named yet needs a Party first. Who makes it, and when?
- **Chosen:** `contacts.book.add` invokes `parties.resolve-recipient` in its transaction, then writes the entry; `contact add` stays one rpc.
- **Why:** Parties' door already has the procedure for this, and names a contact among its users: its money fact is that the first record naming a counterparty binds it to the Safe its money routes to, so every later naming, the person's claim and the chain views agree. Invoking it keeps `contact add` one rpc (A4) and one transaction, so a refusal after resolution (your own Party) commits nothing, a newly named Party included: 'refused before any write' holds. The flow would leave a named Party behind a refused add and need an rpc parties does not offer. The label is checked before the procedure runs. One procedure from one other concept is within sys:tx-width.

### contacts.server-pages: The server pages the book with a keyset cursor

- **Question:** How is `contact list --limit --after` served?
- **Chosen:** `contacts.book.list` takes `limit` (1 to 100, default 25) and `after`, an opaque cursor over (last change, PartyId) bound to the book, and returns `nextCursor`.
- **Why:** The frozen notes promise 25 at a time and a cursor; reading the whole book on every page grows with the book, and P5's read budget is per call. A keyset over the book's own index answers any page in the same time. The CLI still refuses a `--limit` out of range or a malformed `--after` with CLI_USAGE before calling, as the old CLI did; a cursor that decodes but names another book is the rpc's INVALID_INPUT.

### contacts.visibility-machine: Hidden or listed is the `contact` machine, with one event

- **Question:** Is hiding an entry a status with named transitions, or a plain boolean?
- **Chosen:** A `contact` machine on `contacts.visibility`: listed and hidden, with `hide`, `unhide` and `add_again`, each sending `contact_transitioned`.
- **Why:** P7: every write to a status is a named transition, and a machine sends one event. Hidden is a status: two verbs and contact add change it, and the old system already told its funnel about each change (`contact_relationship_changed`, now gone with its emitter). `add_again` is separate from `unhide` because a different command takes it: adding a hidden contact by hand lists it again, as the old add did and as `contact add`'s `hidden: false` says. Hiding a hidden entry or unhiding a listed one is no transition and returns the entry as it is (`contact unhide` #2). Label changes are not a status, so they send nothing. The event carries the book and the PartyId, never a label or mailbox.

### contacts.worlds: One world for every contacts refusal: person-with-contacts

- **Question:** Which devnet world reaches each refusal of the contact commands (contacts' five, and banking's `contact get`)?
- **Chosen:** Ada, a signed-in person (claimed, mailbox ada@example.com), whose own book holds: Bea, a claimed person added by handle with the label `Bea`; vendor@example.com, a provisional person added by email with no label; Cy, hidden; two different Parties both labelled `Sam`. Zed is a claimed person in no book of Ada's. Acme is an active Organization Ada holds no Seat in, with Seats for Dan (a person) and Rex (an agent Dan owns); Acme's book holds Dan added by hand (`manual` and `member`) and Rex as `member` only, and an unknown handle `nobody` names no Party.
- **Why:** Every refusal needs only a signed-in person with a book and an Organization outside it: INVALID_INPUT from adding Ada herself, `nobody` or a 121-character label, or curating Zed; ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED from `--org acme` as Ada; CLI_USAGE from a script without `--input` or `--confirm`; NOT_AUTHENTICATED from the same world with no session. Acme's Seats and book serve `contact list` #2 and #4 from the same snapshot (Dan acting, then Rex removed on chain).

## devnet (26)

### devnet.added-refusals: Four refusals the frozen pages do not list

- **Question:** Some failures a devnet command can give are not on its page. Which are booked as refusals with a world?
- **Chosen:** NOT_AUTHENTICATED for `dev gates set` and `dev prices set` (their `who` is a signed-in person); INVALID_INPUT for `dev prices set` on an asset Capxul does not price; WORK_DIED for `dev jobs run` when a job fails (the page's mistake).
- **Why:** Each is reachable from a catalogued world and is what the command answers, so an eval should pin it; the frozen refusals stay first and word for word. WORK_DIED is reached from bridge-unreachable, where the Bridge catch-up fails.

### devnet.approve-as: `dev approve` signs in by the sink's code and signs with a dev signer

- **Question:** The old command signed in with a fixed e2e code and a shared seed. How does the target play another person?
- **Chosen:** Reuse a kept session for `--as`, else send a code and read it from the mail sink; read the approval; sign its digest with the dev signer derived from the supervisor's handoff seed and the email; give the signature.
- **Why:** A fixed code is a sign-in bypass in access, which no production concept may carry; the sink already holds every code on a devnet (access sends codes there), so the ordinary sign-in works. The approval page's own rpcs (get, then sign) do the rest, so the approval's process sends it as for anyone. Without the handoff the dev signers are unknown here (a browser run without it signs with a real Openfort test signer): WRONG_ENVIRONMENT, as the frozen mistake says. The kept session is not made current, so the eval's current person is unchanged.

### devnet.beta-world-by-variable: The beta world selects beta by CAPXUL_ENV, not by `use env`

- **Question:** cli-on-beta-environment was built with `capxul use env beta`, but access's client.use.env refuses CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE before it saves an environment this machine has no deployment for, so the world cannot be built as written. How does the world reach the CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE refusals that name it?
- **Chosen:** The world sets CAPXUL_ENV=beta for every command an eval runs in it (a World's env), the way the old CLI resolves a deployment: --env, then CAPXUL_ENV, then the saved choice.
- **Why:** The old CLI's config (apps/cli/src/runtime/config.ts) reads --env, then CAPXUL_ENV, so a variable is the surface's own way to point one invocation at beta without saving anything. A flag in each eval would put the world's state into every refusal, and saving an unusable environment would break use env's frozen CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE refusal. Found by the budgets refusal review.

### devnet.budgets: Class defaults for every devnet record; waits on the supervisor are background

- **Question:** Which budget class fits each devnet command?
- **Chosen:** read for the clock, list and mail reads; write for the scripts and the fail switch; background for jobs run, warp, save and restore; chain-settle for start-at; vendor for approve and gates set; chain-read for prices set.
- **Why:** Save, restore and a warp move a whole world or run the crons and wait for them, which the background class (15 s p95) covers; start-at mines one block and waits for Ponder, like a settlement. `dev approve` may send a code (Mail) and `dev gates set` runs a sync that reads PostHog's fake, so neither can take read or write (budget-class-fit); `dev prices set` runs the price sync's feed reads (chain-read). No number leaves its class, so no override needs a why.

### devnet.control-refusals: The supervisor's refusals follow the frozen pages

- **Question:** devnet-control's calls named only INVALID_INPUT beyond the socket's failures, and refused a start-at after a move as INVALID_INPUT. Which codes does the supervisor answer?
- **Chosen:** snapshotSave: WRONG_STATE for a saved name without replace, INVALID_INPUT for a bad name; snapshotRestore: WRONG_STATE for other backend code; clockStartAt: INVALID_INPUT for a date not earlier, WRONG_STATE once the clocks moved. Changed in shared/external.ts.
- **Why:** The frozen refusals are the surface (P10): save's WRONG_STATE, restore's WRONG_STATE and start-at's WRONG_STATE are decided where the state is, in the supervisor, and a check in the SDK before the call would race the call. A refusal must be an error the implementation produces (refusal-not-produced), so the integration's failures carry them. Only devnet uses devnet-control, so the change touches no other concept. The SDK still refuses a malformed name or date before any call, as the old SDK did.

### devnet.devnet-only-rpcs: Devnet's rpcs are mounted only by the devnet build, which serves only loopback

- **Question:** How are devnet-only rpcs kept off staging, and what does "anyone on the machine that runs the local devnet" mean for them?
- **Chosen:** The devnet concept's rpcs are in the RpcGroup only of the devnet build, whose backend listens on loopback; each `who` begins `devnet only:`. WRONG_ENVIRONMENT comes from the CLI's devnet scope (before any network call) and from the supervisor's socket being absent.
- **Why:** A gate in every rpc leaves devnet code reachable on staging, which P2's devnet-in-production rule exists to refuse; an rpc that is not mounted cannot be reached at all. The devnet backend serves only 127.0.0.1, so the machine is the boundary the frozen `who` names; commands a signed-in person runs (`dev gates set`, `dev prices set`) still need a session. Every devnet command keeps WRONG_ENVIRONMENT in its operation's `errors`, as the CLI produces it.

### devnet.dropped: What of old operator's devnet tools is not ported

- **Question:** Which old operator rows land in devnet, and which go?
- **Chosen:** Kept as devnet rpcs: the sink's list and fail switch, the notices read, jobs run. Gone: clocks.read (the supervisor's clockShow), jobs.shift (timers in backend time), outboxes.record (the mail fake), notices.record (notices' own), the receipt hold (the bundler's fault switch), and the gates and price rows of access and books.
- **Why:** Each kept rpc is reached by a frozen operation or a devnet flow (P2). The rest had no caller in the target: the supervisor or the fakes now do their work, or another concept owns it. Old proof identities, fixtures and probes are dropped by the architecture (responsibilities.operator-proof-tools, concepts.devnet).

### devnet.equity-worlds: Equity's twelve worlds join the catalog on their own company, Initech

- **Question:** Equity landed after the catalog: `worldGaps` named eleven worlds its refusals start from, and equity.worlds names a twelfth (the held deployment). Which cast are they built on, and how is a held deployment made?
- **Chosen:** A fresh Initech (founders Nia and Omar, 2 of 2; Ray a member who is no Director; outsider Olga; investors Ivan and Jo), then each step of equity's life as a chain of worlds from org-without-equity. The held deployment arms a new `service-signer hold-receipt` switch before `org equity setup`.
- **Why:** equity.worlds asks for two founders who are both Directors, a member who is not one and an outsider, all with ready Safes; Acme's two-Director world has a member who never finished signing up, and its budget tree would make every equity build pay for the money worlds. Investors need funded Safes of their own for commitments and SAFEs. `deployEquity` is sent by Capxul's service key, not the bundler, so its receipt hold is the service signer's switch (devnet.fault-switches). Every refusal's `given` now names a catalogued world (`worldGaps` is empty).

### devnet.fault-switches: Fault switches and chain actions are supervisor controls, saved with the world

- **Question:** Several worlds hold a failing dependency (Bridge down, a node down, Ponder behind) or a chain fact no Capxul surface makes. How are they made and kept?
- **Chosen:** `capxul-devnet fault <switch> <mode>` arms the devnet layers' switches (chain-rpc node-down and garbage-answers, bridge fail-all, indexer hold <n>, service-signer refuse-send and hold-receipt, bundler hold-receipt, documents tamper) and `capxul-devnet clock hold`, `chain` and `bridge` make what no Capxul surface makes (a still backend clock, a Safe transaction or a Hats ruling outside Capxul, an outside transfer, a token, Bridge deposits, a payout's return). The armed switches are in the world's manifest and re-armed on restore. The mail switch is `dev mail fail`, in Postgres.
- **Why:** The switches belong to integration layers and processes the supervisor runs, and outside facts are by definition not made by Capxul's operations; rpcs for them would be reached by no operation (P2) and would put eval controls in the backend. Saving them with the world makes a world like bridge-unreachable one restore. The old receipt hold is the bundler switch (responsibilities.operator-proof-tools). `documents tamper` is the one control that changes stored bytes, as disk corruption would, for documents' org-with-tampered-note; it is a fault, never a way to build state.

### devnet.jobs: `dev jobs run` runs P3's crons once and wakes the due timers

- **Question:** What are the backend's jobs in the target, and how does a devnet run them now?
- **Chosen:** The crons P3 keeps (each another concept's singleton workflow) and every durable timer due at the backend clock; one rpc asks the engine for one run of each, waits for them and reports each by name.
- **Why:** P3 leaves six crons (Bridge catch-up, prices, flags, the client-grant sweep, the daily agreement notices, the added-token scan) and gives every other wait to a process's own timer, which the engine keeps in Postgres in backend time; on a devnet that clock is the supervisor's, so after a warp the due timers are due, and nothing needs shifting (old shift goes). The rpc names the workflows by id because the schema has no link from an rpc to a workflow; it writes no table of theirs. `only` serves the gates and prices flows. A failed job is WORK_DIED naming it, as the frozen mistake says, after the others finish.

### devnet.mail-sink: The mail fake keeps the sink and spends the faults

- **Question:** Who writes the mail sink and the fail switch, and what does an armed fault look like to the sender?
- **Chosen:** The `mail` integration's devnet layer writes `mail-messages` and spends `mail-faults` inside its `send`; a spent fault fails the send as Resend unavailable (PROVIDER_ERROR), which a notice's workflow retries.
- **Why:** Every send (notices' workflows, access' sign-in code) goes through Mail.send; on a devnet its layer is the fake, so the fake is where a message is kept and a fault is spent. A record rpc would be reached by no operation, flow or workflow (P2's unreached-rpc) and would put a network hop inside every send. The schema has no link from an integration's fake to a table, so the build shows `mail-messages` as unwritten: an expected gap. PROVIDER_ERROR is retryable, so `dev mail fail` shows a notice retry, which is its purpose; a send keeps the first message per idempotency key, as Resend does.

### devnet.no-acceptance: The dev commands without acceptance lines get none from the design

- **Question:** Ten devnet commands (dev time, warp, start-at, snapshot save and list, prices set, gates set, approve, jobs run, mail list) had no acceptance lines in the old book, so each shows a no-acceptance gap. Should the design write lines for them?
- **Chosen:** Write none. Their returns, refusals and spans are still evals, and the harness exercises every one of them when it builds the worlds; new lines need Aaron, as for equity.
- **Why:** An acceptance line is frozen surface (P1), and the frozen book has none here, so a line the design wrote would be a new claim on the public surface, which is Aaron's to accept (equity.acceptance makes the same call). Nothing is unchecked meanwhile: every eval world is built through these commands (B2), so a broken one fails the harness before any operation's eval runs.

### devnet.no-machines: No state machine, so no event

- **Question:** Does any devnet table carry a lifecycle that needs a machine and its `<machine>_transitioned` event (P7)?
- **Chosen:** None: a message is kept once, a fault is armed and spent, a gate or rate is a value that is replaced.
- **Why:** No row moves through states a reader follows: the sink is append-only, a fault is a counter deleted at zero, and a scripted gate's on or off is a setting, not a status. A machine only to send an event would be telemetry booked as behaviour (P7's emit-only review), and nothing downstream follows these rows.

### devnet.no-procedures-no-processes: Devnet has no procedure and no workflow

- **Question:** Does devnet's door offer a procedure, and does it own durable work?
- **Chosen:** Neither: nothing depends on devnet, it owns no long-lived object and no P3 cron, and the supervisor holds the waiting work.
- **Why:** A procedure is for another concept's transaction, and no concept may depend on devnet (its tables serve the fakes, concepts.devnet). The fakes are layers of the devnet build, not concepts. A warp, a save and a restore are waited on by the developer and run in the supervisor; `dev jobs run` runs other concepts' workflows. procedures.ts and processes.ts are empty modules that say so.

### devnet.notices-read: `dev mail notices` reads notices' delivery records in place

- **Question:** Notices owns the delivery records and no operation. How does devnet's `dev mail notices` read them?
- **Chosen:** A devnet query that lists notices' delivery-record table in its reads, by its conventional export name.
- **Why:** Devnet depends on notices (dependencies.of-devnet), so a read needs nothing more, and a copy would be a second store of one answer. The filters by recipient and template run inside the query, so a filtered list never misses older records, as the old 200-then-filter did. The table is notices' `notices`, its mailbox-tenanted delivery records, which notices gave cross-tenant indexes for this read; the fields printed (state, attempts, last error) are the frozen ones.

### devnet.open-beta-world: The open beta is a world the supervisor seeds, not a `dev gates set` step

- **Question:** accounts' `waitlist status` #1 starts from person-on-waitlist-open-beta (the `beta-access` gate on for everyone), which the catalog lacked, and `dev gates set` scripts only Organization gates. How is it built?
- **Chosen:** From person-on-waitlist: the supervisor seeds the PostHog fake's person gate `beta-access` at rollout 100, then `dev jobs run` syncs it into access' flags through the one production path.
- **Why:** The frozen `dev gates set` refuses a missing `--org` with CLI_USAGE, so widening it would change a frozen refusal (P10 rung 1). A supervisor control seeds what no Capxul surface makes, as the fault switches and chain actions do, and the sync job keeps access' flags written only by access.

### devnet.own-refusal-worlds: Devnet's own refusals reuse catalogued worlds

- **Question:** Which worlds do devnet's refusals start from, and does devnet introduce any?
- **Chosen:** None new. WRONG_ENVIRONMENT: cli-on-beta-environment. Time, snapshots and mail: empty-devnet; start-at's WRONG_STATE: org-with-expired-invitation (built with a warp). Gates: org-ready. Prices: person-signed-in. Approve: person-with-payment-history (its expired approval). Jobs' WORK_DIED: bridge-unreachable. Not signed in: signed-out.
- **Why:** Each refusal needs a state another concept already builds. Two are a step after the restore, as access' unreadable state file is: `dev approve`'s WRONG_ENVIRONMENT removes the devnet's signer handoff, and restore's WRONG_STATE rewrites a saved world's manifest to name other backend code (the harness owns the supervisor's store, as it owns the CLI home). Save's WRONG_STATE saves under `empty-devnet`, a name that exists.

### devnet.own-schema: Devnet's tables live in a `devnet` schema only a devnet migrates

- **Question:** Old operator kept its devnet mail, mail faults and test holds in the one production schema, empty everywhere but a devnet. Where do devnet's tables live now?
- **Chosen:** In Postgres' own `devnet` schema, which only the devnet build of the backend migrates; staging and production never create it.
- **Why:** Devnet-only data must never be in the production schema (concepts.devnet, P8's move of test tables). A separate schema in the same server keeps it out of every staging migration while one snapshot of the server still carries it with the world, so a restored world brings back its mail (the invitation links and codes evals read), its armed faults and its scripted gates and rates. Files outside Postgres would need their own save and restore beside the database and could drift from it.

### devnet.scripted-gates: Gates are scripted per Organization and reach access through one sync

- **Question:** How does `dev gates set` change a gate without a second production path, and report the value that applies now?
- **Chosen:** Devnet's `scripted-gates` feeds the PostHog fake; the flow scripts the row, runs access.gates.sync once through devnet.jobs.run, then reads access' evaluator for the effective value.
- **Why:** Writing access' table from devnet is a write-boundary violation and no money fact (P4); access chose one flags table fed through the PostHog fake (access.gates). Waiting a minute would make the frozen `effective` (the value that applies now) false for a minute. Running the one sync keeps the production path the only path. The table is Organization-tenanted (P8) with a cross-tenant index for the fake's flag list. Only Organization gates: the frozen command refuses a missing `--org` with CLI_USAGE, so a person gate (beta-access) is not scriptable by it.

### devnet.scripted-prices: Scripted rates feed the Chainlink fake, dated when they take effect

- **Question:** How does a scripted rate reach the books' one price writer, and what does a pair read before any script?
- **Chosen:** The Chainlink fake answers the scripted row in force at the backend's now, dated the later of its start and when it was set; with none, the frozen defaults. The flow runs books.prices.sync once.
- **Why:** The frozen notes are the surface (P10): every asset has a default rate until set (USDC and USDT 1.00, EURC 1.08, WETH 3,000, WBTC 60,000), and a set rate is used for money facts written after it, while written facts keep theirs. Books keeps a round keyed by its update time and skips a kept one; dating a scripted round by when it took effect makes a replaced rate a new round and never back-dates one before facts already written. A forked Base Sepolia feed is not those defaults, so the chainlink fake's text in shared/external.ts now says so.

### devnet.straddling-payout-world: Reclassify's straddling row is its own world, built on org-with-chart

- **Question:** Books' reclassify #3 needs a row with money facts on both sides of a period: a bank payout settled on a month's last day and returned in the next (books.straddling-row). Books asked for it in org-with-chart. Where is it built?
- **Chosen:** A new world, org-with-straddling-payout, from org-with-chart: Acme's KYB, a GBP account, a `payouts` budget, a payout on the month's last day, a warp, then the Bridge stand-in's return.
- **Why:** Reclassify #1 lists every waiting row of the period and books' list and report lines count org-with-chart's rows, so a payout in it would change what those lines see, and its two dependents (the added token, the node down) would carry it too. A world of its own adds only what #3 needs. The return is a supervisor control, `capxul-devnet bridge return <payout>`, beside `bridge deposits`: the stand-in posts Bridge's return events and sends the refund to the Safe, which no Capxul surface makes (devnet.fault-switches).

### devnet.supervisor-owns-time-and-worlds: The clocks and the saved worlds are the supervisor's

- **Question:** Old warps set an env var through the deployment's admin API and imported data with the Convex CLI. Who owns the clocks and the worlds now?
- **Chosen:** The supervisor: it runs Anvil, Postgres, Ponder and the backend, so it moves Anvil's time, holds the backend's clock offset (the devnet build's Clock layer reads it from the supervisor), saves and restores worlds on this machine's disk, and calls devnet.jobs.run after a warp. The SDK's devnet flows call devnet-control only.
- **Why:** A world is the whole of Postgres, so its catalog cannot live inside it, and only the process that runs every component can stop them, swap their state and start them again. The backend's clock must agree with the chain's, and the supervisor is the one place that moves both together (devnet-control's clockWarp). Clock rpcs only the supervisor called would be unreached (P2). Old operator.clocks.read and jobs.shift are not ported: clockShow reads both clocks, and timers stored in backend time need no shift. The durable processes resume from their stored state after a restore.

### devnet.supervisor-steps: A supervisor call is a flow's `cache` step, with the call in `calls`

- **Question:** A flow's steps are rpcs or local work of a fixed kind (sign, build, encrypt, wait, poll, prompt, cache). How is a call to the supervisor booked?
- **Chosen:** As a `cache` step (this machine's devnet state), its about naming the devnet-control call, which the flow's `calls` lists.
- **Why:** `wait` is a person or the chain and is never system time, but a save, a restore and a warp are the developer's system time and must count against the budget. The supervisor is local state on this machine behind an owner-only socket, which `cache` describes best, and the flow's `calls` gives the call its span and its failures, so its refusals are produced (refusal-not-produced reads `calls`).

### devnet.world-aliases: Four names are second names of one snapshot

- **Question:** Some concepts named the same state differently. Are those separate snapshots?
- **Chosen:** No: `signed-out` and budgets' `cli-signed-out` are empty-devnet's state, payments' `person-without-account` is person-signed-in's and board's `org-with-setup-awaiting-install` is org-awaiting-founder's; each is saved once and listed under both names (`sameAs`).
- **Why:** A NOT_AUTHENTICATED refusal needs only no session here, which empty-devnet has; `person-without-account` is a person signed in with no admitted account or Safe, which person-signed-in is. Two names for one state cost one link, not a build, and leave the owners' records untouched. Budgets may still prefer `signed-out` for one name everywhere.

### devnet.world-collisions: One `org-with-frozen-ceiling`; the Organization money worlds share one tree

- **Question:** Budgets and payroll each named `org-with-frozen-ceiling` for a different state, and payments, payroll and requests each invented an Organization with budgets. One name, one snapshot: which?
- **Chosen:** Build payroll's org-with-department, payments' org-with-budgets and requests' org-with-requests on budgets' org-with-budget-tree, and `org-with-frozen-ceiling` on payroll's org-with-agreements (itself on the tree), frozen by a Capxul freeze leaving 10,000, less than the released pay.
- **Why:** Every refusal both owners attach to the name is reached from the merged world: budgets' (a token already frozen, a non-Director unfreezing, the tree's ceiling reading frozen) need any Capxul freeze, and payroll's (a collection over what the ceiling left) needs released pay above what is left. Building payroll's department on the tree also gives it EURC with no budget and DAI not admitted, which its TOKEN_NOT_BUDGETED and TOKEN_NOT_SUPPORTED refusals need. One cast and tree for the Organization money worlds means one long build instead of four. Contacts', banking's and books' Organizations keep their own casts, which their claims name.

### devnet.worlds: One catalog of eval worlds, each built by a script of black-box steps

- **Question:** The designed concepts' refusals name 78 worlds and their claims and worlds decisions 10 more. How is each built and saved, and where is that written?
- **Chosen:** devnet/worlds.ts: each world's name, what it holds, the world it starts from (or a fresh devnet), its steps (CLI commands, Core SDK methods of surfaces with no command, supervisor controls) and who names it. The supervisor's `capxul-devnet world build` restores `from`, runs the steps, saves with `--replace`, in dependency order.
- **Why:** A world made by running operations is the black-box baseline spec 30 asks for, and restoring it takes seconds where rebuilding takes minutes of chain and Bridge time; seeding rows writes other concepts' tables from outside, which old operator's fixtures did and no target rule allows. Chaining worlds (`from`) builds each prefix once. A step never writes a table: codes come from the mail sink, signatures from `dev approve`. Worlds are rebuilt when the backend code changes, since a world saved with other code refuses to restore. `worldGaps` checks every refusal's `given` against the catalog; catalogProblems checks its own links. Worlds named only by concepts not yet designed join the catalog as they land.

## documents (16)

### documents.acceptance-rewrites: `document render` #1 names the target's employer document

- **Question:** The frozen line 'A payslip names its employer and employee, not their Party IDs.' names a document the target no longer stores (payroll's runs and their Payslips are dropped). Keep, retire or rewrite it?
- **Chosen:** 'A document that names an employer and an employee (a payslip, or an agreement's terms) renders their names, not their Party IDs.', the old line in `was`.
- **Why:** P1 rewrites a line only when it names a mechanism, and a payslip is not one, so this goes beyond P1 and is flagged for Aaron's review (W1). The line's outcome is that a reader sees who employs and who is employed, never an id; the target keeps that outcome on the document that now plays the payslip's part, a salary agreement's terms. The rewrite still passes against the old system, whose payslips render names (render.test.ts:89). Retiring it would lose the outcome; keeping it would leave a line no target world can reach. Every other line of the four operations is kept word for word: none names a mechanism.

### documents.kinds: Four kinds in one envelope version: Memo, Invoice, terms and receipt

- **Question:** Old documents stored v3 envelopes (Memo, Invoice, Payslip, Receipt), a v4 Invoice with a note and a discount, a v5 Money Invoice and opaque receipt files. Which does the target store?
- **Chosen:** One envelope version with three typed kinds, a Memo (a Payment's or request's, or a Movement's), an Invoice with its lines and note in the typed message, and the terms of an offer (an agreement, an equity offer), plus receipt files of at most 128 KB.
- **Why:** The target is a fresh v4 deployment with nothing migrated (P9 option A), so no stored v3 or v4 envelope needs reading and one version is enough. Payslips were payroll runs' documents, and runs are dropped (concepts.payroll): a v4 Agreement commits to one terms document whose hash every collection carries, and equity's grants, rounds and transfers commit to theirs, so 'terms' is the new kind. Money Invoices are dropped with requests' fiat invoices (requests' decisions). The Invoice's lines go into its typed message instead of a separate column checked against a lines hash, so the two hashes are the whole integrity check. Receipt files stay: payments' `document` input is one, and the frozen render line names an opaque receipt.

### documents.movement-memos: A Movement's Memo is stored with the Movement, by payments

- **Question:** Old payments' scan and evidence paths wrote a Movement's Memo through documents' Memo writers, documents tracked each occurrence and its reorgs, and `documents.memos.ensure` could write Memos on demand. Who stores a Movement's Memo in the target?
- **Chosen:** Payments' Movement record invokes `documents.store` with the Transfer's Memo (basis movement, issued at its block time, the Movement's sides as participants), so one Transfer always gives one Memo. Ensure, its authorize and persist steps, the orphan markers and the occurrence copy go.
- **Why:** A frozen line needs them ('A standalone Movement participant can verify its exact Memo'), so they stay. Documents cannot follow a Movement (payments sits above it), and a procedure is the only way into documents' table, so the Memo is written where the Movement is, in the same transaction: a Movement and the document that records it are the same money fact, as a Payment and its document are. `ensure` had no caller in the SDK or the CLI (P2) and proved on chain what Ponder already indexes. The Memo is a pure function of the Transfer once its issue time is the block time, so a replayed or restored Transfer stores nothing new; a reorg is the Movement's `remove`, and the Memo, immutable, stays. Payments' `payments.movements.record` does not invoke `documents.store` yet: that is a change for payments' designer, and until it lands the frozen verify line has no Memo to verify.

### documents.no-machine-no-process: No state machine, no event and no process

- **Question:** Does a document have a status, an event or a durable process?
- **Chosen:** None: a document is written once and never changes; its subject's machine carries the funnel.
- **Why:** P7 sends an event from a status write, and a document has no status to write: its subject's birth transition (a Payment's `open`, a request's `issue`, an agreement's `prepare`) already marks the same moment for the funnel, and a machine with one birth and no other state would be an emit-only record. Nothing about a document waits, so it has no process (A3), and the old background work goes with it (documents.movement-memos).

### documents.no-org-hint: The Organization hint and the read observer go

- **Question:** Old verify and render took the caller's Organization hint and reported what the read proved (integrity, the Organization) to a wide event. Does the target keep them?
- **Chosen:** Drop both: access needs no hint, and the runtime's span records the read.
- **Why:** The hint never granted access ('never selects an unrelated Organization'); it only labelled telemetry. Spans are the runtime's (P6) and no rpc sends an event (P7), so an input that exists for telemetry has no place on the wire.

### documents.one-table: One immutable documents table holds content, subject and readers

- **Question:** Old documents kept four tables: the content (`paymentDocuments`), a link per actor and subject (`financialDocumentLinks`), a copy of each Movement's Transfer (`financialDocumentMovements`) and its fingerprint conflicts. What does the target keep?
- **Chosen:** One `documents` row per document: the exact bytes, both hashes, what the bytes say (kind, format, media type, issue time), its subject and its participants, written once by `documents.store` and never changed.
- **Why:** A document now has exactly one subject and its readers are fixed when it is stored (concepts.documents), so a link per actor and subject has nothing left to say that the row cannot: the old links existed because readers were rederived from subjects documents had to read. The Movement copy duplicated the Transfer Ponder already holds and the Movement payments already records, with a third status (included, orphaned, unresolved) beside payments' (P9's duplicate stores); a Memo is immutable, so a reorg changes the Movement, never the document. The conflict log recorded two different fingerprints for one logical event: in the target a different Transfer is a different log and a different Memo, and nothing reads the log. The line-item and adjustment projections go too: the lines live in the Invoice's typed message (documents.kinds), and adjustments were Payslips'.

### documents.participants: Readers are given at store time and checked against current Seats

- **Question:** Who may read a document, and how is that decided at read time, now that documents cannot read its subjects?
- **Chosen:** The storing concept names the participants (Safe, Party, role). A reader's Safes are their own Party's Safe and the company Safe of each Organization where they hold an active Seat now; one must be a participant. A caller with no Party of their own has none. Missing and unreadable answer alike.
- **Why:** Documents sits below payments, requests and payroll (concepts.documents), so the old rule, which read payments' participants and requests' invoice access at every read, would need the edges the map removed. Fixing readers at store time also keeps the old privacy guarantee by construction: an issued Invoice or Memo is readable only by its issuer and named payer, and a later carrier cannot widen it, because nothing adds a reader.
