- **Chosen:** One workflow per transition, sharing its steps with the kind's other workflows (kit.ts).
- **Why:** A workflow's trigger is a transition the outbox delivers (P4); `after` an rpc would couple notices to the producer's rpcs, miss transitions its processes drive (`open` is the invitation process's, `finish` the setup's), and make notices name rpcs of concepts that know nothing of it. Per-kind would leave transitions nobody follows while the book claimed they were. So the kinds with several causes have one small workflow each, and the build links each to exactly the transition it follows: 30 outbox workflows, each of three to six steps.

### notices.priority-and-budgets: Notices run at normal priority on the background budget

- **Question:** What priority and budget does each notices workflow take?
- **Chosen:** Normal priority (low for equity's); `background` for every workflow; one override for the daily cron.
- **Why:** No caller waits on a notice (P3), so its time is background time; a timer between retries is a wait, never system time. One read, a render and a 2 s p95 mail call per recipient fit the class's 15 s. A notice that lags never rolls back its change, so none is critical, even after a critical operation; equity's follows equity's low priority. The daily cron's override says why where it is set.

### notices.recipients: Who a notice reaches: a mailbox, a principal's email, or the Directors

- **Question:** Notices must turn a subject's parties into mailboxes. How, for a person, an agent and an Organization?
- **Chosen:** A person at the mailbox the source row carries, else at their principal's email; an agent never (it has no mailbox); an Organization through its Directors, the company Safe's owners now, each to its principal's email.
- **Why:** Parties holds only a mailbox's digest (parties.mailbox-digest), so the mailbox comes from the row that named it (an invitation's grantee, an offer's payee, a request's payer, a named person) or from access' principal. One rule for every Organization recipient replaces three old ones (Seats, Directors, Directors plus holders): the Directors can act on every subject a notice tells about, while every Seat would tell people with no authority. Who wears a budget's hat is an indexed chain fact notices cannot read (it does not depend on indexing), and the Directors are the company Safe's owners, which chain-rpc reads now (notices.chain-reads).

### notices.refused-address: A refused address fails at once; an outage or a throttle is retried

- **Question:** The mail call fails three ways: PROVIDER_ERROR, RATE_LIMITED, INVALID_INPUT (the recipient refused). Retry which?
- **Chosen:** Retry PROVIDER_ERROR and RATE_LIMITED; record INVALID_INPUT as `failed` at once (`refuse`).
- **Why:** A provider that refused the address will refuse it a minute later too; retrying only delays a record that says so. The devnet's mail sink answers its fail switch as a provider failure (PROVIDER_ERROR), so `dev mail fail` still shows a notice retrying, as its frozen about says. The error codes are the shared vocabulary's; EMAIL_DELIVERY_FAILED stays dropped (vocabulary.drop-email-delivery-failed).

### notices.round-invitation: An investor invited to a round or SAFE is emailed, at low priority

- **Question:** Equity's design says notices reacts to an investor's `invite`. Old Capxul sent no such email. Send one?
- **Chosen:** Send `round-invitation` on `fundraise-investment.invite`, low priority like equity.
- **Why:** concepts.notices and dependencies.of-notices both name 'a round or SAFE invitation', and the equity designer built the transition for it. An invited investor is often outside the company and has no other way to learn of it. Equity is low priority (CONVENTIONS rule 4), so this workflow is too.

### notices.worlds: Notices introduces no world

- **Question:** Which devnet worlds does notices introduce for its refusals?
- **Chosen:** None: notices has no operation, so no refusal; its workflows are proven from other concepts' worlds.
- **Why:** A world is where a refusal is reached from, and notices has no operation. Its workflows are observed from the worlds the operations that cause them already name (organizations' `org-with-pending-invitation` for a resend, budgets' and payroll's worlds), with `dev mail fail` arming a refusal and `dev mail notices` reading the record.

## organizations (25)

### organizations.acceptance-rewrites: Twenty-one acceptance lines rewritten as outcomes (P1)

- **Question:** Which frozen acceptance lines name a mechanism a black box cannot see, and what replaces them?
- **Chosen:** org create 4 (row); org me 1 (fixture), 2 (copied signers), 4 (stored limit); org retry 1 (no-hash attempt); org show 3 (authority hold, receipt-backed), 4 (proof); org wait 1, 2 (scheduled attention); invite list 1 (cursor); invite decline 1 (configuration), 2 (verified), 4 (dispatch, commands), 5 (scheduled event); org invite list 3 (cursor); org invite cancel 1 (triple, configure command), 2 (signed configuration), 3 (verified), 5 (scheduled event); org invite resend 2 (records), 3 (records failed).
- **Why:** P1 option C: keep every claim a black box can check, and turn each line that names a row, a cursor, a scheduled job, a command or a copy into the outcome it guarded, checked by cli, read, chain, trace or world; each keeps its old line and test citation in `was`. A rewrite must still pass against the old system: each says what the old test observed, without the old mechanism (a cursor becomes `--after`, a scheduled event becomes the traced org_invite_transitioned, a stored delivery outcome becomes the mail sink).

### organizations.agent-seats: AgentRemoved ends an agent's Seats, as a chain-event workflow

- **Question:** The concept map says an agent's Seat ends when the chain shows the agent removed. How?
- **Chosen:** A workflow triggered by AgentOwner's AgentRemoved removes every active Seat at the agent's Safe.
- **Why:** Agents depends on organizations, so organizations cannot follow its transitions; the chain event is the fact both concepts agree on, and indexing delivers it (dependencies.of-organizations names AgentRemoved).

### organizations.approval-endings: A setup stalled by its install approval keeps approvals' code for the ending

- **Question:** Approvals asks each opener to map an approval's endings onto the frozen codes (approvals.machine). The setup records a stall reason (approval_declined, approval_expired, approval_displaced, install_refused, install_reverted) and a code. Which code goes with each?
- **Chosen:** declined CANCELLED; expired and displaced WRONG_STATE; reverted TRANSACTION_FAILED; refused the code approvals stored.
- **Why:** `org show` and `org wait` print the setup's failure with a code, and agents and board answer their carriers' endings with the same mapping, so one CLI says the same thing for the same ending. The reason stays, because `org retry` resumes from the step it names. No frozen refusal of the setup operations changes: these are the setup's recorded failure, not a refusal.

### organizations.bind-invitation-grants: New procedure: the proposer binds an invitation to its grants' transaction

- **Question:** Budgets proposes an invitation's grants to the Board, and organizations cannot follow budgets' or board's transitions (it depends on neither). How does the invitation learn which proposal carries its grants, so it can open, or be cancelled, from the chain?
- **Chosen:** Budgets invokes `organizations.bind-invitation-grants` in the transaction that opens the proposal; the invitation then follows that Safe transaction on chain.
- **Why:** Money fact: the grants (hats with authority over the company's money) and the Safe transaction that carries them are one fact, the rules.procedures case 'a chain write and the proposal that carries it'. Bound in one transaction, the invitation never waits on a proposal that does not carry it, and its process decides everything from chain proof: ExecutionSuccess of the bound hash opens it, the nonce going to another transaction cancels it, and the cancel rpc can tell whether the proposal may still run. Waiting for hats cannot know a new allowance's hat, and letting the proposer drive the phase would write organizations' machine from above, a second writer P4 forbids. The invoking transaction calls two concepts' procedures (board.propose and this one), within sys:tx-width.

### organizations.company-safe-address: `begin` computes the company Safe's address in-process

- **Question:** The Party registered at birth needs the company Safe's address before the Safe exists. How does `begin` get it?
- **Chosen:** Compute the minter's pure `companySafeAddressFor` in-process (CREATE2 from fixed inputs), so `begin` stays one write with no node call.
- **Why:** The function is pure, so the backend's computation is checked once against the contract (an eval on the devnet) and never needs a node; a node call would put `begin` in chain-read and make a write fail when a node is down.

### organizations.create-budget: `org create` takes chain-submit at 4 s / 8 s, not the 2 s / 4 s default

- **Question:** Which budget does `org create` take end to end, excluding the founder's wait and chain confirmations?
- **Chosen:** chain-submit at p50 4,000 ms and p95 8,000 ms.
- **Why:** Before the founder can sign, two service-key transactions are prepared and sent in series (the mint, then deployMoney, which needs the minted Safe) at about 1.5 s p95 each, and the install approval is sponsored (about 1.2 s p95), on top of the begin write. Confirmations are excluded, the sends are not: three hops of the chain-submit class cannot fit its one-hop default.

### organizations.create-refusals: `org create` refusals: FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE for the waitlist; no GS026

- **Question:** The frozen refusals of `org create` name no code for a founder still on the waitlist, and the designed text adds ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED for a batch approved by the wrong Safe. What does the target refuse with?
- **Chosen:** FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE for the waitlist (the old code's), WRONG_STATE for an unclaimed Safe (the frozen text), and no GS026 path.
- **Why:** P10: where the book is silent, the old code's behaviour stands, and the old begin refused the waitlist with FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE (requireBetaAccess). An unclaimed Safe is the frozen WRONG_STATE, not SMART_ACCOUNT_MISSING. The install approval is only ever opened for the founder's own Safe, which owns the company Safe, so no other Safe can approve the batch and the designed GS026 refusal has no world.

### organizations.declined-previews: Five organizations commands end CANCELLED on a declined terminal preview

- **Question:** Under vocabulary.declined-preview-cancelled a 'no' at a terminal preview ends CANCELLED (exit 130). Which organizations commands ask, and where is CANCELLED listed?
- **Chosen:** `org create`, `org retry`, `invite decline`, `org invite cancel` and `org invite resend` ask at a terminal before they call their one SDK method (their frozen notes: a script must pass `--confirm`; org create 'shows a preview and asks first'). CANCELLED goes in each operation's `errors`, with the CLI's own codes, as `contact add` does.
- **Why:** The CLI asks before it calls the method (org create's flow says so), and three of the five are one rpc with no flow; giving them a flow only to ask would break A4's one-call operation is its rpc. A 'no' writes nothing, and a script passes `--confirm` and never sees the question, so no frozen refusal or exit of a script changes.

### organizations.deployment-row: The deployment is one row written once from the indexed MoneyDeployed

- **Question:** MoneyDeployed is a chain fact indexing already holds. Does organizations keep its own deployment row?
- **Chosen:** Write one row per Organization from the event at finality; it names which hat is which, and never changes.
- **Why:** The concept map gives organizations the deployment ('which officer hat is which') so every concept above can name hats without knowing the install's officer order. The event is emitted once per company and its ids never change, so the row cannot drift, which is what P3's 'nothing copies chain rows' guards against. The install batch is built from it, so the setup needs it anyway. Who wears a hat now, the budgets and the Board's signers stay indexing's views.

### organizations.dropped: Old rpcs and workflows that are not ported

- **Question:** The old workspace areas had 46 rpcs and 4 workflows. Which survive?
- **Chosen:** Only what a frozen operation, a flow, a signal or a trigger reaches (P2), and no plumbing (P3): 13 rpcs and 3 workflows, with the grants, officer hats and treasury in their own concepts.
- **Why:** Setups: prepare and submit authorization, the proof receipt and list-mine go to approvals or into `org list`; the ten load, record, accept, fence, claim, apply, recover and emit rpcs are steps of the setup process or the runtime's outbox. Invitations: preview is the budgets flow's read before it writes, get and retry have no frozen caller (a retry has nothing to retry once grants are hats), sweep and expire are each invitation's timer, and the five emit rpcs are transitions' events. Members: grant, revoke, permission-target and record-permission-change are board's officer hats; advance and the legacy states are gone; the emit and notify rpcs are events and notices. Roles: the list is dropped (responsibilities.roles). Treasuries: activity's.

### organizations.event-names: Machines org-setup, org-member, org-invite; org_invite_transitioned stays

- **Question:** What are the three machines called, and so their events (P7)?
- **Chosen:** org-setup, org-member and org-invite, so the invitation's event stays `org_invite_transitioned`, the name the frozen notes use.
- **Why:** The frozen notes of `org member invite` name `org_invite_transitioned`, so a journey or a reader of those notes finds the same event; the prefix also keeps the ids clear of accounts' personal Safe setup and banking's verification invitations. The three old setup events (organization_creation_started, _ready, _failed) fold into org_setup_transitioned. Transition names are verbs (send, accept, decline), so the event's `transition` carries `send` where the old note said `sent`; the new values are listed on the machine.

### organizations.flows: Seven flows, seven single rpcs

- **Question:** Which operations need a Core SDK flow, and which are one rpc call (A4)?
- **Chosen:** Flows: `org create` and `org retry` (rpcs, the approval page, waits), `org show` and `org wait` (find the one unfinished setup when no Organization is given), `use org` (list, then save), `org member get` (the list, then a pick), `invite list` (its own offers, or with `--org` the Organization's). The other seven are one rpc each.
- **Why:** A4: the CLI calls one SDK method, so whatever composes rpcs or saves local state is the SDK's flow. `org show` and `org wait` find 'exactly one unfinished setup' only by listing; `org member get` must 'read the member from the same list'; `invite list --org` lists an Organization's invitations instead. The founder signs the install on the approval page in the browser, so the flows open it and wait, and call no approvals rpc; `org show` and `org wait` share the live setup rpc.

### organizations.invitation-delivery: The invitation email and its delivery are notices'; resend is a transition

- **Question:** Old invitations sent their own email and kept its delivery outcome. Notices now sends every email. What stays here?
- **Chosen:** Organizations records when it last asked for a send (the resend cooldown) and moves `resend`; notices sends from org_invite_transitioned and keeps sent or failed. Views report `none` for an offer to an `@handle`, else `not_confirmed`.
- **Why:** No other concept sends mail (concepts.notices), and an email may lag its change, so it is an outbox consumer (P4); a delivery outcome is no money fact, so notices cannot write it here. Organizations cannot read notices (it sits above), so its views say truthfully that delivery is not confirmed from here. The cooldown needs only the time of the last send organizations asked for. The resend acceptance lines are checked against the mail sink.

### organizations.invitation-machine: No pending_grant or revoked: grants are minted before the offer opens

- **Question:** On v4 an invitation's grants are a Board batch minting hats to the invitee's Safe. Which phases remain?
- **Chosen:** authorizing, pending, active, revoking, declined, cancelled, expired: an offer with grants opens once the Board's batch executed, and accepting has nothing left to grant.
- **Why:** The designed call mints the offered hats to the personal Safe the invitee's email names as part of the Board batch, before the offer is open for acceptance, so acceptance only takes the Seat. Without a grant after consent there is no late cleanup, so revoked has no source. Ending an offer whose hats are on chain still needs the Board to take them back, so revoking stays, and the offer ends with its recorded reason when the chain shows them gone. The frozen returns keep `pending_grant` in their literal lists; the target never produces it.

### organizations.invitation-process: One invitation process: chain proof, its own expiry timer, answers as signals

- **Question:** What drives an invitation after it is offered, now that the daily expiry cron, the grant drive and its verify loop are gone?
- **Chosen:** One durable process per invitation, started after the invite rpc: it waits on the bound Safe transaction, then on accept, decline or cancel (its signals) or its seven-day timer, then on the hats leaving the grantee's Safe.
- **Why:** An invitation is on rules.durable-processes' list: it waits days for a person and for the Board, and must survive a crash. P3 sends the daily expiry to a timer per invitation. Its trigger is `after` the invite rpc because an invitation has two births (with and without grants) and a trigger names one transition; the about says why it cannot be a step. Every transition it drives is guarded by the phase it leaves, so a replayed step is a no-op.

### organizations.invite-grants-in-budgets: Budgets proposes an invitation's grants and their return; every Offer stays

- **Question:** The architecture review moved `org member invite` from board to budgets (operations.member-invite-in-budgets). Organizations' records said board proposes the grants, binds their transaction and takes granted hats back, and budgets had asked to offer existing allowances only (budgets.invitation-allowance-offers). What does organizations change?
- **Chosen:** Name budgets as the proposer and binder everywhere; keep the three Offer kinds (officer hat, existing allowance, new allowance with name, asset and limit) and the interface: invite rpc, bind procedure, begin_cleanup.
- **Why:** The designed synopsis keeps `--new-allowance-name --limit --asset` and its call is the allowance's createHat, createChildBudget and mintHat (P10 rung 1); budgets can now plan them, which is why the review moved the flow. What `limit` becomes on chain is budgets' to plan. The invitation holds offers as data and learns the minted hats from the bound transaction's indexed facts, so a new allowance's unknown hat id is no problem. Budgets' rpc invokes `board.propose` and `organizations.bind-invitation-grants` in one transaction (two concepts, sys:tx-width), and its own workflow on `org-invite.begin_cleanup` proposes the hats' return; organizations' process ends the offer once indexing shows them gone.

### organizations.member-kind: `org member list` keeps `ai` for an agent's Seat; the rest say `agent`

- **Question:** The table stores `agent`; the designed invite text replaces `--ai` with `--agent`; `org member list` returns `human` or `ai`. Which does each surface print?
- **Chosen:** Member list and get keep their frozen `ai`; invitation views and `org list` use `agent`, as the designed text does.
- **Why:** P10 rung 1: returns are surface, and `org member list` has no designed block that changes it. The mapping is one line in its view.

### organizations.members-are-seats: One Seat per Party, born active, ended only by the chain

- **Question:** What is a member in the target, and what can end one?
- **Chosen:** One Seat per Party per Organization, born active by the setup (the founder) or an acceptance; it ends only when AgentRemoved names an agent's Safe. Authority is the hats the Seat's Safe wears.
- **Why:** No old writer ever inserted pending, pending_safe or pending_grant, and the old revoke came from a reconciliation of copied chain state. A person invited and not yet joined is an open invitation, which `org member list` shows as pending. No frozen operation removes a person's Seat (`org member revoke` takes a hat back, which is board's), so the only end is the agent's removal on chain, which the concept map gives organizations. One Seat per Party gives every concept above one guard: a current Seat for the session's principal.

### organizations.provisional-party: An invitee is resolved through parties, named provisional when new

- **Question:** An email may name nobody Capxul knows yet. How does the invite rpc get the invitee's Party?
- **Chosen:** Invoke `parties.resolve-recipient` in the invite transaction: a new mailbox is named provisional at its email-derived Safe.
- **Why:** The old invite ensured the Party by email, and an invitee is often someone who has never signed in. Parties' own door binds a mailbox to the Safe its money routes to (its money fact), once, with the record that first names it, and an invitee is one of the records it names. The invitation keeps the mailbox it was sent to, because parties stores only a digest and notices must deliver to it. With `accounts.admit` the transaction invokes two procedures of two concepts, within sys:tx-width.

### organizations.reads-from-chain: Standing, capabilities and the Board summary are read from the chain

- **Question:** `org me`, `org list`, `org member list` and `org show` report roles, capabilities, Directors and waiting proposals. Where from?
- **Chosen:** Indexing's views (hat wearers, the company Safe's owners, threshold and approvals, budgets), named through the deployment row; waiting proposals are approved hashes not yet executed at or above the Safe's nonce.
- **Why:** On v4 authority lives only in Hats, the Signer Gate and Budgets ('Capxul reads the permission only from Hats'), and the frozen line 'A Director is whoever the Safe's signers name, with no Capxul grant' says the same. Board sits above organizations, so a count of waiting proposals comes from ApproveHash and ExecutionSuccess, which every Director's approval leaves on chain. The member list's `permissions` are the officer hats the Seat's Safe wears.

### organizations.setup-machine: Five setup states, the steps as data, and failed only when nothing can finish it

- **Question:** The old setup had six statuses (with rechecking after a reorg and a park outside the status). What does the target setup machine hold?
- **Chosen:** processing, awaiting_authorization, needs_attention, ready and failed; the finished steps (safe_minted, money_deployed, installed) as a column; ready only at finality.
- **Why:** `org show` already reports the steps separately, and `org wait` and `org retry` decide on the status alone, so per-step states would multiply the resume transitions without telling a reader more. Rechecking existed because the old setup went ready before finality and a reorg could orphan its proof; the target waits for the install's facts at the deployment's finality depth, so ready is terminal. Failed is kept for what no retry can fix: a minted company Safe that is not owned by the founder's Safe, or a provider answer that will not change (PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID); an install the Installer refuses (InvalidInstall) stalls and stays resumable, as the designed text says.

### organizations.setup-process: One setup process replaces observe, the recovery cron and ten internal rpcs

- **Question:** The old setup was a prepare action, a submit action, ten internal rpcs, a scheduled observe and an every-minute recover cron. What is the target?
- **Chosen:** One durable process per setup, started by `begin`: the service signer sends the mint and deployMoney once each, indexed facts at finality confirm them, the install is one approval opened with approvals.open, and approval_transitioned tells the outcome.
- **Why:** A3 gives an Organization's setup one durable process that owns its machine, and P3 sends the recovery cron to the process's own waits. Sponsoring, signing, sending and following the founder's UserOperation are approvals' for every concept (concepts.approvals), so organizations only builds the calls (buildMoney's batch and the officer hat mints) and opens the approval in the transaction that writes the deployment. The Party registered at birth turns active when the Safe is minted to the founder (`parties.activate`), and is released if it is not (`parties.release`), as parties' door asks. The CAP-1097 rule (record the signed bytes before broadcast, look a lost answer up, never send deployMoney twice) is the service signer's. The process's only signal is `org retry`; reads never wake it.

### organizations.tables: Five tables from the old nineteen: the workspace's own records only

- **Question:** Which of the old concept's tables does the target workspace keep, merge or drop?
- **Chosen:** organizations, org-setups, org-deployments, org-members and org-invitations. Roles and the Roles-module deployment merge into the deployment; Seat permissions and their changes go (officer hats are board's); Directors, proposals, equity, rounds and votes leave with their concepts.
- **Why:** On v4 a permission is a hat and an allowance a budget, so seat-permissions and their changes copied the chain (P9 and responsibilities.keepers). The old setup row was mostly plumbing for Convex scheduling (lease, dispatch and send tokens, generation, park, unresolved counters) that a durable workflow holds itself (P3). The roles catalog and the Roles deployment were pre-v4 shapes; on v4 the deployment's named hats replace them. Every table is tenant-first on org_id (P8); the cross-tenant indexes are the caller's own memberships, offers and unfinished setups, an Organization by its Party, and Seats by an agent's Safe.

### organizations.treasury: The treasury is the company Safe; its balance is activity's

- **Question:** What does the workspace keep of the old treasuries area?
- **Chosen:** The Organization's `safe_address`; `balance --org` and the feed are activity's.
- **Why:** The old treasury read was a balance of the company Safe, which is a read of whoever you act as (activity, with the landing map's note). No frozen operation of organizations returns a balance.

### organizations.worlds: The devnet worlds organizations' refusals and acceptance lines start from

- **Question:** Which named devnet snapshots does this concept introduce, and what does each hold?
- **Chosen:** signed-out; person-on-waitlist; person-unclaimed (admitted, Safe not claimed); person-ready (admitted, Safe claimed, no Organization); org-awaiting-founder (setup waiting for the founder's signature); org-two-unfinished-setups; org-setup-stalled (install approval expired); org-setup-stalled-unsent (stopped before the mint was sent); org-setup-failed (a step answered PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID); org-ready (founder is the one Director and wears the three officer hats); org-with-member (plus a hatless Seat); org-with-agent-member; org-with-removed-agent; org-with-allowance; org-with-uncapped-allowance; org-with-pending-invitation (Seat-only, to a person); org-with-granted-invitation (its hats minted, pending); org-with-authorizing-invitation (its proposal open); org-with-rejected-grant-proposal; org-with-accepted-invitation; org-with-expired-invitation; two-founders (two ready Organizations).
- **Why:** Each world is built by running operations as the devnet's fixed identities and saved as a snapshot (responsibilities.operator-proof-tools), named for what it holds. Two refusals keep `?`: WRONG_STATE for an offer whose Organization or grantee no longer matches, which no operation can produce; it is a defect guard.

## parties (13)

### parties.birth-outcomes: Two procedures end a registered birth: activate and release

- **Question:** An Organization's or agent's Party is registered before its Safe exists. Who says the Safe is live, or that the birth failed, given that parties is below organizations and agents?
- **Chosen:** `parties.activate` and `parties.release`, invoked in the transition that ends the birth.
- **Why:** Each is one money fact under rules.procedures. Activation: a handle must not route money to a Safe that is not live, and an agent's predicted Safe can only ever be deployed by AgentOwner with its key, so a payment to a pending agent could be stranded; payments cannot read agents, so the directory must carry the answer. Release: a handle whose Safe will never exist must stop naming it in the same commit that fails the birth ('fails and frees its handle'). The outbox would need parties to depend on agents and organizations, which depend on parties. Old parties had the same pair as `claimAgentParty` and `releaseAgentHandle`.

### parties.claim-at-profile: A person's Party becomes theirs when they complete their profile

- **Question:** The concept map says a person's Party is provisional 'until they sign in'. Which write claims it?
- **Chosen:** `parties.profiles.save`, the first parties write a person makes after signing in.
- **Why:** Access is below parties and cannot invoke it (concepts.access), and a sign-in has no display name to give the Party. Accounts' Safe setup already needs the profile first (`account show` refuses with PROFILE_NOT_FOUND before it), so the profile is the earliest write that knows who the person is and what to call them. The claim keeps the Party's id, so what payments, contacts and invitations saved for the mailbox before signup still points at the person. Whether money to the person goes straight to their Safe or into a claim escrow is the chain's CapxulSafeClaimed, read by payments, not the Party's state.

### parties.destinations: A Party's receiving destination is its Safe; saved destinations are not ported

- **Question:** Shared responsibilities.destinations keeps receiving destinations in parties. Old `counterpartyDestinations` saved bank, mobile-money and wallet rows per counterparty and per actor. What is a receiving destination on v4?
- **Chosen:** The Party's `safe_address` is its one receiving destination. A saved bank row is banking's saved bank account; a saved wallet is an outside-address Party (a contact names it); mobile money has no rail; the actor's own wallets are their Safe.
- **Why:** On v4 every payment is a `Payments.send` to one address, and the directory already says which address each Party is paid at. A wallet row beside it would be a second answer to 'where does this counterparty get paid' that could disagree with the Party's Safe. The bank rows are Bridge external accounts under banking's record key (concepts.banking). `client.destinations.*` had no caller outside tests (P2), and the onboarding payout addresses it fed are the person's own Safe. The landing stays parties, as the shared decision says: the destination is the Party's Safe.

### parties.directory-tenancy: The directory is global; a person's private profile is theirs

- **Question:** P8 gives every table a tenant. Every read of the directory crosses tenants (by handle, mailbox, Safe or id), and a provisional person or an outside address has no owner. What is the directory's tenancy?
- **Chosen:** `parties` is `global`: written only through parties' own procedures and rpcs, readable by every concept that depends on parties; it holds nothing private (no mailbox, no profile). The private half is `profiles`, tenancy `person`, `person_id` first.
- **Why:** An `owner` table must lead its key with the owner, but a provisional person or an outside address has none, and a Party's owner appears only when it is claimed or registered, so the key would change under a row that payments and contacts point at. Row-level security on owner would hide exactly the rows resolution must find. Splitting owned from unowned Parties would put one Safe and one mailbox in two uniqueness domains, the reason shared dependencies.parties-registration gives against separate handle tables. The directory is a namespace like the chain: what it shows (kind, handle, name, photo, Safe) is what any counterparty may see, so a global table loses nothing to row-level security, and no tenant writes it directly. Its owner columns are what indexing's views join (concepts.parties-scope). The person-only fact, the country, is in `profiles`, behind the person's own policy.

### parties.dropped-stores: Old parties' edges, terms, purges and ledger seams are not ported

- **Question:** Old parties also held contact and employed edges, engagement terms, an operator purge and the money seams' ensure helpers. Where do they go?
- **Chosen:** Contact edges go to contacts; employed edges and engagement terms are dropped for payroll's agreements; the purge is dropped; the ledger seams become `parties.resolve-recipient` or a read of `parties`.
- **Why:** The address book needs an Organization's membership, so it sits above organizations (concepts.contacts); an edge table below would be written from above for a fact that is not money. v4 Agreements replace engagements and their rates (concepts.parties). The operator purge wrote other concepts' rows and no frozen operation reaches it (responsibilities.operator-proof-tools). `ensureLedgerParty` and `partyKeyFor*` keyed old ledger rows on a Party; a Payment now names its recipient's Party through resolution, and activity reads the counterparty by Safe.

### parties.handles: Handles for agents and Organizations; only a failed birth frees one

- **Question:** Who has a handle, what is a valid one, and when is one free again?
- **Chosen:** Agents and Organizations, in one namespace; 3 to 30 of a-z, 0-9, `_`, `-`, not reserved; freed only when a birth is released.
- **Why:** The designed v4 text names people by email and agents and Organizations by `@handle`: 'wherever a command takes a person's email, it takes an agent's `@handle`; an agent has no email', `org member invite` refuses a handle no agent has, and an agreement's payee is 'a person's email or an Organization's `@handle`'. The old rule and reserved words are kept. `agent create` 'fails and frees its handle' when the chain refuses it or its approval lapses, and `agent remove` says 'its handle stays taken', so only `release` (from `pending`) clears one.

### parties.party-machine: Five Party states; births are transitions; one event

- **Question:** Old parties had a status of provisional or claimed and a kind that included `unresolved`. Which states does a target Party have?
- **Chosen:** provisional (a named mailbox), outside (a named address), pending (a registered birth), active (owned and live), released (a failed birth); eight transitions, four of them births.
- **Why:** The old two states carried four meanings: a named mailbox, a bare address, an agent reserved before its Safe landed (stored as a provisional person) and a claimed identity, and an agent's release only cleared its handle without a state. Each of those is a different answer to 'may money be routed here, and whose is it', which every reader of the directory asks. `pending` is what lets a payment refuse an agent whose create has not landed, since parties cannot read agents. Births are transitions (from no state) so every new Party sends `party_transitioned`, which carries the owner as its tenant and never a mailbox, handle or name. The kind `address` replaces `unresolved`; a claim changes it to `person`.

### parties.mailbox-digest: The directory keeps a digest of a person's mailbox, never the mailbox

- **Question:** A person Party is found by mailbox. Does the global directory store the mailbox?
- **Chosen:** Store the SHA-256 of the trimmed, lowercased mailbox in `mailbox_digest`; never project it.
- **Why:** Old parties stored the mailbox and relied on every view leaving it out; its own invariant was 'an entry never shows a mailbox its owner did not type'. In a global table any mistake in a projection leaks every mailbox. A digest still finds the Party for a typed or signed-in mailbox (the normalization is the Safe salt's, so the digest and the derived Safe always agree) and shows nothing. Whoever must mail a provisional person holds the mailbox they typed (the invitation, the escrow's payment). A keyed digest would add a secret whose rotation re-keys every row; revisit it if the directory is ever exported.

### parties.no-operations: Parties owns no frozen operation; its rpcs serve the web app's setup page

- **Question:** OPERATION_OWNERS gives parties no operation. Which old parties and profiles methods survive, and as what?
- **Chosen:** Two rpcs, `parties.profiles.save` and `.get`, each behind a Core SDK flow for the web app's setup page; old `details.create`, `details.update` and `onboardings.complete` fold into `save`; `details.handleAvailable` and the public `recipients.resolve` and `recipients.lookup` queries are dropped.
- **Why:** P2 keeps a method only with a named caller. The frozen `auth login` and `account retry` send a person whose account is not ready to 'the web link where you finish setup', and the profile is what that page asks for first, so the page is a named surface. Create, update and complete were three writes of one row; one idempotent save covers them. A handle check has nothing to check once people have no handles, and an Organization's or agent's taken handle is refused by its birth. Resolution before a write is the procedure, and the commands that name a counterparty resolve inside their own rpcs, so a public resolve query has no caller. With no operation, parties introduces no world.

### parties.no-process: Parties has no durable process

- **Question:** Does any parties object wait on something outside a request (rules.durable-processes)?
- **Chosen:** None: every Party change is inside one transaction; the births' waiting is in organizations' and agents' processes.
- **Why:** A Party waits only as long as the birth it belongs to, and that birth is already one durable process in its own concept (an Organization's setup, an agent change), which invokes `parties.activate` or `parties.release` when it ends. A second process per Party would follow the same chain event twice. Old profiles' account-ready email moves with the Safe's setup to accounts and notices, and its replaced-photo deletion to media's retention.

### parties.profile-shape: A profile is a display name and photo on the Party, and a country

- **Question:** Old profiles held a display name, country, KYC tier, photo, onboarded flag, withdrawal address, member kind and the Party link. What does the target keep?
- **Chosen:** The display name and photo go on the Party, where counterparties read them; the country stays in `profiles`. The KYC tier, withdrawal address, member kind and onboarded flag are dropped.
- **Why:** A display name is what a counterparty sees of a Party, so it belongs on the Party (an Organization's and an agent's come from their births). The photo is the same, as a media file id (`dependencies.of-parties`: a photo points at a stored file). The KYC tier and withdrawal address were marked transitional in old profiles and grant nothing; on v4 money leaves a person's Safe only by an operation they sign, to the address they choose then, and no frozen operation reads either field (P2). The member kind is gone: an agent is its own kind of Party. The onboarded flag is the Party's own `claim` or `onboard` and the profile row's existence. The country stays: banking's verification prefills it. The photo's admission is read from media's `files` at the person's own key (purpose `image`, state `kept`), as organizations reads a logo.

### parties.resolve-recipient: A procedure names a new counterparty; reading one is a table read

- **Question:** Many concepts name a counterparty by handle, mailbox, address or Party id. Is resolution a procedure, a read, or an rpc, and what does it do with a mailbox or address no Party holds?
- **Chosen:** `parties.resolve-recipient`, a procedure that resolves and names a new mailbox or address in the invoker's transaction and answers `resolved: false` instead of refusing; a query or a check reads `parties` directly.
- **Why:** The money fact (rules.procedures: 'a name and the Safe it routes money to') holds: the record that names a counterparty and the one Party per mailbox and Safe it keys on must commit together, or a contact or a request's payer could point at a Party nothing holds. An rpc step would be reached only from other concepts' flows and would leave a named Party behind every refused record. The frozen refusals for a locator that names nothing differ by command (INVALID_RECIPIENT on `payment send`, INVALID_INPUT on `contact add`, `agent sweep`, `org member invite`, `org agreement offer`), so the procedure answers and the invoker refuses in its own words; old E8, where nothing raised INVALID_RECIPIENT, is then the payments rpc's own error. A transaction that only shows or checks a counterparty (a feed, an inbox, a payment's prepare, which already invokes approvals and documents and must stay within sys:tx-width) reads `parties` and invokes nothing.

### parties.safe-derivation: A person's Safe address is computed in the backend, not read from the chain

- **Question:** A person's Party needs the Safe their mailbox derives. Is that a chain read?
- **Chosen:** Compute it in the backend with CompaniesHouseClaimer's pure `safeAddressFor` formula and the deployment's namespace.
- **Why:** `safeAddressFor` is `pure`: its answer depends only on the claimer's address, the namespace and the mailbox, all known at boot, so the backend computes the same address the chain would (old `personalSafeAddress` did this). That keeps `parties.resolve-recipient` and `parties.register` free of outside calls, as a procedure must be, and `parties.profiles.save` in the `write` class. The devnet's eval compares the computed address with the chain's.

## payments (28)

### payments.acceptance-rewrites: Eight acceptance lines rewritten as outcomes; three v4 openings stripped

- **Question:** Which of payments' 41 frozen acceptance lines name a mechanism a black box cannot see, and what replaces them?
- **Chosen:** Rewrite send #9, retry #1, cancel #2, reschedule #4, get #3, list #2, wait #1 and department pay #1 (plus the three v4 openings of send #7, #8 and cancel #2) as observable claims, keeping each old line in `was`; keep the rest verbatim.
- **Why:** P1: send #9 names the job-nonce lookup and a retry; retry #1 a nonce lane; get #3 and list #2 'schedule nothing' and 'Payment row'; wait #1 an unsubscribe; department pay #1 'sending re-proves the holder'; cancel #2 the schedule and command rows. Each rewrite names what the cli, read, chain, trace or world observer sees and still passes against the old system. Reschedule #4 says only a reschedule may cancel in Budgets, but payment cancel's v4 text cancels a budget's dated payment with `Budgets.cancel` (P10: the designed text wins), so it now admits a cancel of that same payment and still refuses any other scheduled id. P9 strips the 'On Protocol v4' openings; the old text stays in `was`.

### payments.birth-width: A send's birth invokes three procedures from three concepts

- **Question:** prepare and propose invoke parties.resolve-recipient, documents.store and approvals.open or board.propose: wider than sys:tx-width allows. Split it?
- **Chosen:** Keep the three in the birth's transaction.
- **Why:** Each is the same money fact as the Payment: the payee and the Safe its money routes to (a wrong binding pays the wrong Safe), the document whose hash goes on chain (a hash with no content is a money bug), and the carrier (nobody may sign a payment Capxul has no record of). Naming first would leave named Parties for births that then failed, and K2 says the command, its Payments and their documents are born together. The width is three procedures, at the rule's limit of three, from three concepts instead of two: this record is the decision P4 asks for. Reading parties' table cannot replace the procedure: a mailbox nobody has yet is named a provisional Party at its email-derived Safe in the same transaction (the claim escrow's payee), and that is a write to parties. Still open for the sys:tx-width reviewer's sign-off.

### payments.board-outcomes: A failed or replaced Board proposal lapses its Payment; a rejection cancels it

- **Question:** Board's proposal now ends executed, failed (ExecutionFailure: the nonce used, nothing happened), rejected or replaced (another transaction took its nonce). How does each move a Board payment?
- **Chosen:** executed → `submit`; failed or replaced → `lapse` (outcome reverted, displaced); rejected → `cancel` (outcome rejected).
- **Why:** A failed or replaced proposal moved no money and its terms still stand, as an approval that reverted or was displaced: `payment retry --as-directors` proposes the same payment id again, which the chain consumes once. A rejection is the Directors' answer, as a decline is a person's, so it ends the Payment. Board asked every follower of proposal_transitioned to treat `fail` as 'ran without effect' (board.failed-proposal).

### payments.board-rpc: A Board payment is its own rpc

- **Question:** `--as-directors` births a Board proposal instead of an approval. One prepare rpc or two?
- **Chosen:** `payments.commands.prepare` (approval) and `payments.commands.propose` (Board proposal); the flow calls one.
- **Why:** Each rpc's `invokes` then lists what its transaction really does, so sys:tx-width and the generated authorization (a Director against a hat wearer) are per rpc. The calls differ too: an immediate Board payment cannot be a budget spend from inside a Safe transaction (the Gate refuses re-entry), so it is `approve` and `Payments.send` from the company Safe, while a member's is `Budgets.spend`.

### payments.carrier-waits: The Payment's process follows its approval and proposal as waits

- **Question:** How does a Payment's process learn that its approval was signed, sent, landed or ended, or its proposal executed?
- **Chosen:** The process waits on its carrier: approvals' and board's events reach it by the opener and subject they carry; approvals' sign and decline and payments' change rpcs are its signals.
- **Why:** The brief's shape is one process per Payment resumed by signals and waits. A reactor per transition would be eight workflows each writing the same machine, which is the old system's many writers (S3) in a new form. Polling is the sweep P3 removes. approval_transitioned carries `opener` and `subject_id` for exactly this (approvals' event), so the runtime resumes the one instance waiting on it. The signature itself is approvals' signal (approvals owns the one send, K4); listing approvals' sign and decline rpcs as this process's signals says the person's answer is what moves the Payment. A Board proposal's `proposal_transitioned` is followed the same way.

### payments.chain-born-ids: A Payment born from a chain fact is named by its v4 payment id

- **Question:** Payroll reports a collection's payment from its indexed PaymentSent and cannot read payments; `payment get` refuses an id that does not start with `payment_` (frozen #1). How does payroll name a Payment `payment get` finds?
- **Chosen:** A chain-born Payment's id is `payment_` and the 64 hex digits of its v4 payment id; payroll prints the same.
- **Why:** Accepting `0x…` would weaken `payment get`'s frozen refusal of an id that does not start with `payment_`, and the map keeps payroll and payments apart (dependencies.payments-payroll: neither reads the other). The v4 payment id is unique per chain (the by-chain-payment-id index) and known to both sides from the same fact, so the id needs no lookup. Prepared Payments keep their ULID ids: nothing outside payments needs to derive them. Payroll's other ask is met as designed: `payment wait` follows any `approval_` id its signer holds (payroll's holder writes included), with no Payments when it moves no money.

### payments.command-without-state: A command is an idempotency record with no state of its own

- **Question:** The old command had a state and a verification lease. What does the target command keep?
- **Chosen:** Owner, request key, request digest, route, purpose and the approval or proposal it opened; no machine.
- **Why:** K1 needs only the key, the digest and what the command birthed: a repeat answers the first command as its Payments and approval stand, another request under the key is refused by the digest, and the key stays closed for good. The old command state duplicated its Payments' (prepared ⇄ submitted) and its verification lease was the sweep's bookkeeping, which the Payment's process replaces; `applied` meant three things (old S6). A second machine would be a second writer of the same outcome.

### payments.count: Old against new: 82 functions and 13 tables become 18 behaviours and 3 tables

- **Question:** How much of the old payments concept does the target need?
- **Chosen:** Old: 71 rpcs and 11 workflows (82 Convex functions: 17 public, 65 internal), 65 door procedures, 8 SDK flows, 13 tables, 8 machines, 3 crons. New: 8 rpcs, 3 workflows, 1 procedure, 6 flows, 3 tables, 2 machines, no cron.
- **Why:** The 8 frozen operations need 6 flows and 2 rpcs at the boundary, 6 more rpcs behind the flows (2 births, 3 changes, 1 live read), 1 door procedure for requests and banking, and 3 workflows for time and the chain. Everything else was Convex's split of one write into many hops, executions now in approvals, pre-v4 paths, or stores that duplicated Ponder. The 60 old door procedures with no booked caller have none now: their callers' needs are prepare-command, the outbox (payment_transitioned) and plain reads of payments' tables.

### payments.dated: Dated payments: approvals holds the personal send, the process pays a budget's

- **Question:** Who keeps a dated payment's day, its day-ahead check and its send, now that approvals owns every send?
- **Chosen:** Personal: one approval opened with `sendAfter` and the Safe's need, held and sent by approvals (K16 there). Budget or Board: the reservation is on chain and this process asks `Budgets.execute` with the executor key on its day, hourly while refused. Both come due a day ahead in this process.
- **Why:** Approvals is the only sender of a person's operation (K4), so it holds a signed dated one and checks its needs on the day (approvals.held-sends). `Budgets.execute` is not a person's operation: anyone may call it, and the executor key is Capxul's (service-signer), so its timer is the Payment's. 'No retry window ends a dated budget payment: only the chain does' (payment send notes): the process retries hourly until PaymentSent or ScheduleCancelled. The day-ahead check stays the opener's, as `come_due`, so notices has a transition to warn the payer from. The old due-job cron (every 5 minutes, 25 schedules under a lease) becomes one timer per Payment (P3).

### payments.designed-surface: Where a designed block exists, its who and refusal codes are the operation's

- **Question:** Five of payments' operations carry a v4 designed block whose `who` and refusals differ from the top-level text. Which wins?
- **Chosen:** The designed `who`, and the union of the top-level and designed refusals, with the designed code where they disagree: an over-budget department payment is PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED, not ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED `budget_exhausted`.
- **Why:** P10 rung 1: surface goes to the book, and where `designed:` exists its v4 text wins (P9). The designed refusals add what v4 can answer and the old surface could not: PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED for OverBudget and the Roles ceiling, WRONG_STATE for a closed budget, INVALID_INPUT for a date not in the future. `department pay` #4 still names `budget_exhausted`: it is the reason, the code is the designed one. The top-level `who` lines are kept in the frozen pages the operations point at.

### payments.document-bytes: `--document` passes the receipt's bytes, not a media file id

- **Question:** SendInput's document named a media file (`content_hash`, `file_id`), but `documents.store` takes a receipt's bytes (at most 128 KB), and neither payments nor documents depends on media. How does the file reach documents?
- **Chosen:** The SDK flow reads the file and passes its bytes, media type and name in the prepare input; prepare stores them.
- **Why:** The CLI already holds the file it was given, and 128 KB fits an rpc input. A media edge would add a dependency only to read bytes the client had a moment before, and the file's stored copy would then be documents', not media's. Documents keeps the bytes exactly and checks the media type.

### payments.door-inputs: The door takes a date, a deadline, a resume, and items with no document

- **Question:** Requests and banking asked the door for more: a dated `inbox pay` (`pay_at`), the approval's expiry in the answer, `inbox pay --resume` reopening a lapsed Payment under the same key, an item with no document (banking does not depend on documents), and a payout's Payment that cannot land, or be retried, after its quote expires. Which does `payments.prepare-command` take?
- **Chosen:** All five: `pay_at`, `not_after` (kept on the command; it caps the approval's deadline and `payment retry` refuses after it), `resume` (a repeat whose Payments all lapsed opens one new approval for the same payment ids and moves them `retry`), `expires_at` in the answer, and an item's `document_id` null with the zero hash.
- **Why:** Each is the same money fact the door already records. A dated Payment is the same birth with `send_after` or `Budgets.schedule`, as `payment send --on` does. A resume is a retry: same terms, same job nonce, same payment id, so the chain still consumes it once, and it must commit with the request staying `paying`, which a later rpc call could not promise; it is asked for explicitly, so K1's plain repeat still answers as it stands. `not_after` belongs on the command because a retry opens a new approval long after the first: only the command remembers the quote. The deadline answered is an upper bound, since the paymaster cuts the window when it sponsors. A payout's Payment has no document of its own on chain today (its Bridge transfer is the record), and the zero hash names none; a new edge to documents would make banking store a document nobody reads.

### payments.dropped: What the old payments concept had that the target does not port

- **Question:** Which old payments functions, crons and behaviours are not ported, and where did each go?
- **Chosen:** Plumbing (1 emit, 8 intent, 11 load, 3 record operations, 10 scheduler sites, 20 telemetry schedules) becomes steps or the outbox; executions go to approvals; the three payment crons become waits; operator retries, inclusion migrations, the money-writes switch and V2/V3 code paths go.
- **Why:** P3: a load, intent, record or emit is a step inside one rpc or workflow, and transition events leave through the outbox (P7), so telemetry no longer decides money (M7). The crons: settlement verification recovery → the process's own resume; scheduled payments due → one timer per Payment; settle from the indexer → the PaymentSent wait. retry-mismatch and retry-unresolved had no caller (P2); migrate- and rollback-inclusion only renamed a stored value; the 18 money-writes checks could never fire (D7). V2 commitments, V3 sends and their claims (K15) are pre-v4 (P9). Payroll runs (K11) are dropped with runs (concepts.payroll). Bank delivery and return (K13) are banking's payout machine. Schedule notices are notices' workflows on `payment_transitioned` (old R8, M9).

### payments.keep-list: Where each money rule of the keep list lives in the target

- **Question:** Each of K1-K18 is real money behaviour in the old code. Which target record keeps it?
- **Chosen:** K1 request key: payment-commands' unique key and digest. K2 born together: prepare's one transaction. K3 funding before birth: prepare's reads and prepare-command's funding input. K4-K6 one send, execution binding, chain proof: approvals. K7 exact terms, K8 settle once, K9 single use, K10 book fact: the process's settle step.
- **Why:** Every rule the reviewers kept has one named home, and the build links each home to the records here. Funding is still read, not reserved: the old race of two unsent batches on one budget (M6) is closed by the chain, which refuses the second at sponsorship before anyone signs, so the user hears it before signing rather than after landing.

### payments.machine: The Payment machine: nine states, every write a transition

- **Question:** Which states does a target Payment have, and what goes from the old ten?
- **Chosen:** prepared, scheduled, due, escrowed, submitted, needs_retry, settled, mismatched, cancelled; a birth is the `open` transition from no state; only the Payment's process and the change rpcs drive it.
- **Why:** `in_transit` is a bank payout's, so it is banking's payout machine (K13, dependencies.payments-banking). `streaming` and `pending_claim` were V2 commitments (P9); a claim escrow is `escrowed`. `unresolved` existed because a receipt could be missing; now approvals ends every approval on chain proof (K6) and the settlement waits for the indexed fact, so nothing is unprovable. `failed` merged two meanings: an approval that ended without money moving is `needs_retry` (retry sends the same payment id, so it cannot pay twice), and a Payment that will never move is `cancelled` with its `outcome`. `due` gives notices the day-ahead warning approvals leaves to payments (approvals.held-sends). `mismatched` keeps a fact that disagrees visible instead of booking it. A reorg reopens only settled or mismatched (old S1), and nothing else writes `status` (old S3).

### payments.movement-memos: A Movement stores its Memo in its own transaction

- **Question:** Documents asked payments to store each Movement's Memo, so `document verify` has one to verify. Where?
- **Chosen:** `payments.movements.record` invokes `documents.store` beside the book fact, once per Transfer.
- **Why:** The frozen `document verify` #1 ('A standalone Movement participant can verify its exact Memo') needs it, and a Movement and the document that records it are one money fact (documents.movement-memos). The Memo is a pure function of the Transfer (basis movement, reference `<chain id>:<tx hash>:<log index>`, issued at the block time, the Capxul sides as participants), so a replay stores nothing new. The record transaction then invokes two procedures of two concepts (books, documents), within sys:tx-width.

### payments.movements-outside-money: Movements are outside money only, one row per side

- **Question:** What does payments keep of the old Movements, attributions and Deposit Payments?
- **Chosen:** A Movement is a side of an indexed Transfer that no Payment of that side explains, booked as that side's outside money in the same transaction; a Payment's own Transfer is never one; no Deposit Payments.
- **Why:** Ponder holds every Transfer, so a Movement row for a Payment's own Transfer would be a third copy of one fact; suppressing it is K17. Judging each side on its own Payments fixes old M1, where a person's Deposit row made an Organization's direct transfer look explained. Deposit Payments were Payments born for inbound Transfers with no command, the same fact as the Movement; dropping them leaves one record per money fact. The book key is books' `transfer:<chain>:<tx>:<log>`, which banking's deposits use too, so books keeps one live fact whoever writes first. The old `observed` and `finalized` states had no reader (old S10).

### payments.one-process-per-payment: One durable process per Payment, absorbing the command's and the dated payment's

- **Question:** rules.durable-processes gives a payment command and a dated payment one process each. Which instance owns a Payment's machine?
- **Chosen:** One process per payments row, started by its birth, following its carrier, its dates, its escrow and its settlement; a dated payment is a Payment with a date; a command has no process.
- **Why:** A Payment is the object that settles, lapses, is retried, rescheduled or cancelled on its own: `payment retry` and `payment cancel` name one Payment, and a batch's items are scheduled and settled one by one (`payment send` #9). A per-command process would hold N machines' worth of state; a per-dated-payment process would be a second owner of the same machine. Each Payment process follows the shared approval, so a void still cancels every Payment of the operation (K14), and the command keeps K1 without a process (payments.command-without-state). This drops the two listed processes in favour of one, as rules.durable-processes allows with a decision.

### payments.one-settlement-path: One settlement path: the Payment's process, from the indexed PaymentSent

- **Question:** The old system settled from a receipt and from the indexed fact, with different checks. What settles a target Payment?
- **Chosen:** Only the Payment's own process, from the indexed PaymentSent with its payment id read with every fact of its transaction, every term compared, the log unique, the book fact in the same transaction; a Payment Capxul did not prepare is born first and settled by the same step.
- **Why:** Two paths racing were old M2-M4: different terms checked, side effects copied at five sites, a Memo or evidence row depending on which arrived first. One step makes K7 (the union of both old checks: Payments' address, chain, sender Safe, recipient, token, raw amount, kind, document hash; the sender is the one term v4's payment id does not hash), K8 (settle once: the transition moves only from an open state), K9 (the unique log) and K10 (the book fact in the transaction) true in one place. Birthing a chain-born Payment settled would be a second settle site; birthing it `prepared` and letting its process settle it costs one transaction and keeps the path single.

### payments.prepare-command: The door takes the invoker's funding read and stored documents

- **Question:** `payments.prepare-command` cannot call the chain, and K3 needs funding read before birth. How does a request's or payout's birth keep it?
- **Chosen:** The invoking rpc reads the paying Safe's balance or the budget's left before its transaction and passes it with the block; the procedure refuses a shortfall and writes nothing; documents come already stored; it invokes only approvals.open.
- **Why:** A request paid and its Payment are one money fact (dependencies.payments-requests), so they must commit together; a payments rpc called after would leave a paid request with no Payment if it failed. The read is server code of a concept that depends on payments, at a named block, so the procedure can check it. The invoice or payout document is the invoker's, stored when the request was issued, so the transaction spans requests (or banking), payments and approvals: two procedures from two other concepts, within sys:tx-width.

### payments.priority: `payment send` is critical; the other seven are normal

- **Question:** Which payments operations are the spine's pilots (critical)?
- **Chosen:** `payment send` critical, with the process and the chain birth it rides on; the rest normal.
- **Why:** Spec 40 makes the spine and its three pilots critical without naming the pilots here; `payment send` is the one payments operation every money journey starts with, and it exercises every layer (flow, prepare rpc, procedures of four concepts, the approval, the process, settlement and books). Marking all of them critical would leave the word meaning nothing. Change it when the spine names its pilots.

### payments.reads: Reads are one indexed page, answered from payments' own rows

- **Question:** How do `payment get`, `list` and `wait` read, given the old list read everything and the projection read four neighbours?
- **Chosen:** Payments' rows, the caller's own Party, membership and the carrier's approval; one page per call.
- **Why:** The old list read two whole indexes into one page (M14) and the projection read profiles' tables payments did not depend on. With labels, the document id and the outcome on the row, a read needs only the caller's Party (to tell 'no Account' from 'not yours', old E5), the Seat for `--org`, and the approval for the execution line. `null` is never an answer: a read that finds nothing the caller can see is INVALID_INPUT (`payment wait` #4).

### payments.reorg: Settle at the finality depth, confirm later, reverse forward

- **Question:** How does a target Payment or Movement keep K12 (reorgs reverse what they removed) without the old any-status reset?
- **Chosen:** Consume a fact at the deployment's finality depth; read it again at twice that depth; if it is gone, `unsettle` (or `remove`) and reverse the book fact in one transaction.
- **Why:** The finality depth is a setting, not a law: a deeper reorg, or Ponder rebuilding past it, would leave a settled Payment with a book fact for money the chain no longer shows. One more read per Payment is cheap and makes K12 a declared transition from `settled` only (old S1 reopened failed and in-transit Payments too). Settling at the head would make every settlement provisional and every reader care.

### payments.reschedule: A reschedule births a new Payment; a personal one in two runs of one rpc

- **Question:** Does a rescheduled payment keep its row, and how does a personal one's void-then-new-date work?
- **Chosen:** The old Payment is cancelled when the chain says so and a new one is born for the new day (new job nonce, so a new payment id); a personal move is the same rpc run twice, the second once the void landed.
- **Why:** `payment reschedule` #3 says the old payment reads cancelled and the new one waits on its day, and on chain they are two scheduled ids. A personal void consumes the held operation's nonce, so the new day needs a new operation; the frozen notes say running it again after the void goes straight to the new date. Moving in place would need a `scheduled → prepared` edge that only reschedules use, and would reuse a payment id the chain may still hold.

### payments.three-tables: Three tables replace the old thirteen

- **Question:** Which of the old 13 payments tables does the target keep, merge or drop?
- **Chosen:** payment-commands (with the document intents), payments (with dated payments, consumed evidence and mismatches) and movements (outside money only); executions to approvals; Transfer copies, scan checkpoints and the sweep cursor gone.
- **Why:** Each old store that went is a duplicate or plumbing. money-executions and provider-send-attempts are approvals' now (concepts.approvals). movements copied Ponder's Transfer logs and movement-scan-checkpoints was the scan's cursor: Ponder indexes Transfers and indexing serves them. settlement-sweep-cursors existed for a minute sweep a chain-event wait replaces (P3). payment-schedules held signed bytes (approvals' held operation) and a lease (the process's timer); what remains is `pay_at`, `scheduled_id` and `shortfall` on the Payment. commitment-evidence's job (single use, K9) is a unique index on the settled log; its preState replay was for V2 commitments (P9). payment-evidence-conflicts is the `mismatched` state with its first differing term. payment-document-intents is the command's request digest, which covers the document hashes. deposit-materializations went with Deposit Payments (payments.movements-outside-money). Per-route tables would split one ledger the activity feed and books must read as one.

### payments.two-sided-tenancy: A Payment is held by one owner and read by both sides

- **Question:** A Payment between two Capxul tenants is read by both. Whose tenant is its row (P8)?
- **Chosen:** `owner` tenancy: the payer when the payer is Capxul's, else the payee; the payee's tenant on the row with a cross-tenant payee index; the generated policy admits either column. Labels snapshotted at birth.
- **Why:** One row per side would copy the status into two rows that two transitions must keep equal: the duplicate store P9 and old D1 warn about. Payer-only would hide a Payment from the person it pays, who must `payment get` it. Two tenant columns keep one machine and let row-level security fail closed for anyone else. The labels (how the payer named the payee, how the payee reads the payer) are written once at birth, so reads never reach into parties' tables, which was the old projection's read-boundary bug on 27 records.

### payments.withdraw-by-opener: Banking withdraws an unsigned payout's approval; the process cancels the Payment

- **Question:** Banking ends a payout before anyone signs (its bank account changed). Approvals asked openers to withdraw such an approval (`approvals.withdraw`). Does payments add a procedure for it, and who cancels the Payment?
- **Chosen:** Banking, which now depends on approvals, invokes `approvals.withdraw` with the approval id the door answered, in the payout's own transaction; the Payment's process cancels the Payment on that approval's `withdraw` (outcome withdrawn).
- **Why:** P4 adds a procedure only for a money fact no existing door records. The fact here is the payout ended and its unsigned carrier with it, which `approvals.withdraw` already is; since dependencies.payers-sign-and-name-budgets banking may invoke it. The Payment's status has one writer, its process, which already follows every end of its approval; a withdrawn approval is one more end. If the payer signed first, `withdraw` answers `signed` and banking knows the payout may still land. Payments' own changes never withdraw: `payment cancel` acts only on a signed, held payment (frozen), and a retry opens its new approval only after the old one ended.

### payments.worlds: Seven worlds reach every payments refusal

- **Question:** Which named devnet snapshots do payments' refusals start from?
- **Chosen:** signed-out: no session on this machine. person-with-funded-safe: a signed-in person whose live Safe holds 1,000 USDC, another person with a live Safe, and a mailbox with no account. person-with-empty-safe: a signed-in person whose live Safe holds no USDC, with one payment whose approval expired. person-without-account: signed in with no Party or Safe yet. person-with-payment-history: a person with a settled payment, a scheduled personal dated payment, a payment whose approval expired, one received from another person, and a payment between two others.
- **Why:** Each is made by running operations as the devnet's fixed identities and saved as a snapshot (environments.devnet), named after what it holds. Seven cover all 36 refusals of the eight operations: a refusal that needs an approval to lapse (SUPERSEDED) is reached by running the command in its world and moving the clock past the window.

## payroll (25)

### payroll.acceptance: Payroll's acceptance lines: verbatim but for v4 openings and one rewrite

- **Question:** Which of payroll's 66 acceptance lines change, and how?
- **Chosen:** Keep every claim word for word; drop 'On v4' from its eight lines (P9); rewrite agreement list line 4, which named the old settle-on-read, as an outcome.
- **Why:** 'Is settled when read' describes the old mechanism (settledContext settled writes inside each read), which the target replaces with the agreement's process; 'reads settled once it is on chain' is what a black box sees and still passes against the old system. 'Reports' and 'receipt' name outcomes an observer checks (an event in the trace, a reverted transaction on chain), so they stay, read as payroll.reports says. Lines naming the bundler or the mail sink stay too: both are faults or sinks a world can drive.

### payroll.budgets: Payroll's budgets are class defaults

- **Question:** Which budget class does each payroll record take, and does any need an override?
- **Chosen:** Write commands and flows chain-submit; reads and every prepare rpc chain-read; the write watch live-read; the process chain-settle. No overrides.
- **Why:** Each prepare rpc reads two to four contract views in parallel and writes one transaction: chain-read's 1-3 parallel eth_calls. A list reads one view per agreement, also in parallel, within the same class at the sizes the product sees. A person's signature and chain confirmation are waits and never system time (P5), so the flows fit chain-submit. Each step of the process is a chain event to its recorded transition: chain-settle.

### payroll.carrier-endings: A failed Board proposal fails its write; an expired approval answers WRONG_STATE

- **Question:** Board added a `failed` proposal (the company Safe ran it and the inner call failed, board.failed-proposal), and approvals asks every opener to map an approval's end onto the frozen codes. What does an agreement write do with each?
- **Chosen:** A `failed` proposal fails the write (`execution_failed`): it ran without effect, as a reverted operation does. The flows answer a declined approval CANCELLED, one that expired unsent WRONG_STATE, and a refused, beaten or failed write TRANSACTION_FAILED.
- **Why:** A failed proposal used its nonce on chain; abandoned means nothing reached the chain, which would read wrongly in `agreement show`. Board asks openers to read `fail` as 'ran without effect', which is exactly a failed write. The codes are the mapping of decision approvals.machine: expired is WRONG_STATE, declined is CANCELLED (contacts.decline-is-cancelled).

### payroll.collection-payment: A collection reports its payment from the indexed PaymentSent

- **Question:** `agreement collect` returns 'the one payment the payer made', but payroll does not depend on payments. Where does it come from?
- **Chosen:** The write keeps the PaymentSent id, amount and window from the transaction's indexed facts; payments births its Payment from the same fact.
- **Why:** dependencies.payments-payroll keeps payments below nothing of payroll's and payroll below nothing of payments', and concepts.payments has payments settle every PaymentSent, a collection included. The PaymentSent id is the key both sides share, so `payment get` finds the Payment payroll reports without either concept reading the other.

### payroll.collection-payment-id: A collection prints its Payment's id the way `payment get` takes it

- **Question:** Payments names a Payment born from a chain fact `payment_` and the 64 hex digits of its v4 payment id (payments.chain-born-ids), because `payment get` refuses an id that does not start with `payment_`. What does a collection's `paymentId` print?
- **Chosen:** The same derived id, built from the PaymentSent's payment id: no read of payments.
- **Why:** `agreement collect` returns 'the one payment the payer made', and a person follows it with `payment get`; the raw id would be refused there. The id is a pure function of the indexed fact both concepts read, so payroll still never reads payments (dependencies.payments-payroll).

### payroll.drop-token-required: Org agreement offer drops TOKEN_REQUIRED and gains the designed WRONG_STATE

- **Question:** The offer's frozen refusals include TOKEN_REQUIRED, which its mistake text calls pre-v4. Keep it?
- **Chosen:** Drop it: on v4 a budget pays in one token. Add WRONG_STATE for a closed budget, 64 open agreements or no budget yet, as the designed text has.
- **Why:** The mistake reads 'Before v4, the department budgets in more than one token', and on v4 'every agreement belongs to one budget, and through it to one Safe and one token' (agreements spec). P9 drops pre-v4 paths, and the designed refusals (the v4 text, P10) list PAYROLL_BUDGET_EXCEEDED, WRONG_STATE and ROLE_PERMISSION_DENIED. A refusal no implementation can produce fails `refusal-not-produced`. The code stays in the vocabulary for budgets' own use.

### payroll.dropped: Old agreement rpcs that are folded in or dropped

- **Question:** Old payroll registered 37 agreement functions. Which become target rpcs?
- **Chosen:** Twelve rpcs and one process. The Organization-payee rows fold into the payee rpcs (`orgId`); preview-collect folds into collect; cancel-change into change; the load, record, submission, settle, send, notice, sweep, claim and emit functions are dropped; the org-side get and wait flows go.
- **Why:** P3 turns load, record and settle functions into steps and emit functions into the outbox; approvals sends every operation, so the send functions have no place here; notices owns the notice reads, sweeps and claims. No frozen operation reaches `payroll.agreements.get`, `preview-collect` or the org-side `wait` (P2): a write flow follows its own write, and the payslip is part of the collection it previews. An Organization payee is the same payee with `--org`, so it needs no second set of rows.

### payroll.flows: One flow per write command, one rpc per read; the designed SDK names win

- **Question:** How are the eleven commands implemented, and which SDK method names do the flows take?
- **Chosen:** Each write is a flow (prepare, approvals' sponsored read, sign, submit, wait, follow); each read is one rpc. Names follow the designed `method` where one exists (`client.me.agreements.collect`).
- **Why:** A4: the SDK owns signing and the CLI calls one method; a write composes a prepare, a signature and two waits, so it is a flow, and approvals asks every opener's flow to compose its signing rpcs. The frozen sdk of `agreement collect` is `client.agreements.collect` but its designed method is `client.me.agreements.collect`, and the designed text wins (P10), so both payee writes sit under `client.me`. The reads keep their frozen methods.

### payroll.gates: The payment-agreements gate refuses holders only

- **Question:** Which payroll rpcs read access' `payment-agreements` gate, and which read `payslip-preview`?
- **Chosen:** The six holder writes refuse FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE when it is off; payees and reads never do; collect reads payslip-preview to add the payslip.
- **Why:** The frozen refusals put FEATURE_NOT_AVAILABLE on the holder commands only, and a frozen claim says a payee can still accept what was offered with agreements off: the gate stops new commitments, never pay already promised. The payslip is shown 'unless the payer turned previews off', which is the payer Organization's gate.

### payroll.holder-writes: A holder's write is an approval from their own Safe, never a proposal

- **Question:** dependencies.of-payroll says an agreement on a top budget is offered or changed by Board proposal. The frozen text says a holder write 'is not a Director proposal'. Which does payroll build, and what does it use board for?
- **Chosen:** Every holder write is one approval from the holder's Safe; board serves only an Organization payee's accept and collect.
- **Why:** The frozen `changes` lines of all six holder commands say it is not a Director proposal, and surface text wins (P10). On v4 a department is a top budget whose office hat its holders wear, and Budgets authorises a hat's wearer from their own Safe, so no holder write needs the Board.
